# Changelog

## [1.0.0-rc.6] - 2026-07-12

### Added
- **`extraParams.hxml` ships with the haxelib package** — the library requires `--macro bh.base.AtlasMacroInit.init()` to register the `.atlas2` resource extension; previously that line lived only in the repo's own `hx-multianim.hxml`, so a haxelib consumer using `-lib hx-multianim` never got it and every `.manim` using atlas resources failed at load. Haxelib applies `extraParams.hxml` automatically for `-lib` consumers; the README quick-start now notes that `-cp` consumers must add the macro line themselves.
- **DevBridge bind address is configurable via `HX_DEV_BIND`** — the dev HTTP server previously hardcoded `0.0.0.0`. The default is unchanged (`0.0.0.0`, all interfaces, so LAN MCP clients keep working), but `HX_DEV_BIND=127.0.0.1` now restricts the bridge to the local machine, and `new DevBridge(screenManager, port, bindAddress)` accepts an explicit override. The startup trace now prints `Listening on port <port> (bind <addr>)` — the documented `Listening on port N` substring that MCP tooling points AI clients at is preserved. Security note added to `docs/devbridge.md`: DevBridge has no authentication and answers with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — anything that can reach the port can screenshot, inject input, eval `.manim`, or quit the process, so bind to loopback on untrusted networks.
- **`@:manim` codegen instances expose `beginUpdate()` / `endUpdate()` / `batchMode`** — the batch-update API previously existed only on `BuilderResult` (and `IncrementalUpdateContext`), so a multi-param state change on a codegen instance (e.g. `setStatus("hover")` + `setDisabled(true)`) ran the full `_applyVisibility` → `_updateExpressions` → `_fireRebuildListeners` pass once per typed setter — two rebuilds and two rebuild-listener fires where `BuilderResult.beginUpdate`/`endUpdate` coalesces to one. Generated instances now carry the same API: between `beginUpdate()` and `endUpdate()`, typed setters update their backing field immediately but defer the rebuild; `endUpdate()` runs a single combined pass (full `_applyVisibility`, no per-param gate, then `_updateExpressions` + `_fireRebuildListeners`). Outside a batch, setters apply immediately (non-batched behavior unchanged). `beginUpdate()` while already batched throws (nesting unsupported); `endUpdate()` without a matching `beginUpdate()` throws; `batchMode` is a `(get, never)` flag. New fixture `test/examples/136-codegenBatchUpdate/` + `CodegenBatchUpdateParityTest` (listener fires exactly once when batched / once-per-setter unbatched, visibility deferred until `endUpdate`, `batchMode` flag transitions).
- **`UIStandardMultiAnimButton.setStyleParameter(name, value):Bool`** — runtime styling pass-through to the button's underlying programmable. Construction-time `extraParams` was previously the only way to drive design-specific parameters (e.g. `accent`, `tintColor`); now they can be changed on a live button. Returns `false` (no-op) when the build does not declare the parameter — safe to call against any button design, and callers that expect the parameter to exist can assert on the return value. The widget-managed parameters `status`, `buttonText`, and `disabled` are rejected with a `BuilderError` (`code="widget_managed_param"`): silently writing them desyncs the widget (stale `getText()`, status overwritten by the next hover event, disabled visuals while clicks still fire) — use `setText()` / `disabled` instead. Three tests in `UIComponentTest` (`testButtonSetStyleParameterAppliesDeclaredParam`, `testButtonSetStyleParameterUnknownReturnsFalse`, `testButtonSetStyleParameterRejectsWidgetManagedParams`).
- **04b-03 font in the font showcase** — added [04b-03](http://www.04.jp.org/) (Yuji Oshimoto, free) as a baked BMFont (`test/res/fonts/o4b03.{fnt,png}`), registered as `o4b03` in `TestApp`. Test `26-fontShowcase` gains an 04b-03 sample row rendered at 10x with the lineHeight/baseLine reference overlay.
- **Departure Mono font + box-drawing support in the font showcase** — added [Departure Mono](https://departuremono.com) v1.500 (Helena Zhang, SIL OFL) as a baked BMFont (`test/res/fonts/departuremono.{fnt,png}`), registered as `departuremono` in `TestApp`. It carries the Unicode box-drawing block (`U+2500–257F`), so `.manim` `text(departuremono, "┌──┬──┐", …)` renders connected box frames — strokes are centred and tile across rows at the font's native 11px pixel grid (lineHeight 14, base 11). Baked from the v1.500 OTF into a monochrome, no-AA BMFont page covering ASCII + box-drawing/block-element ranges. Test `26-fontShowcase` gains a Departure Mono sample row and a box-drawing junction-grid demo.
- **Codegen instances expose `hasSlot(name, ?index, ?indexY):Bool` and `hasDynamicRef(name):Bool`** — the existence companions to the generated `getSlot` / `getDynamicRef` were previously only emitted on `BuilderResult`, not on `@:manim` codegen instances. So the documented "guard before access" pattern (`hasSlot` before `getSlot` for indexed slots whose iteration count can shrink) failed to compile on a typed codegen instance, and surfaced as a missing-field runtime error via a `Dynamic` instance. `ProgrammableCodeGen` now generates both: `hasSlot` mirrors `BuilderResult.hasSlot` (exact kind+index match, never throws — returns false on any miss including kind/index mismatch), emitted whenever `getSlot` is (static slots OR runtime sinks present); `hasDynamicRef` mirrors `BuilderResult.hasDynamicRef` (presence-only, never throws), consulting the same static-ref / dynamic-name-ref / runtime-sink sources as the `getDynamicRef` dispatcher. New non-throwing `SwitchArmResults.hasSlot` / `hasDynamicRef` let the dispatchers consult the lazily-built switch/repeat sinks without risking a throw. New fixture `test/examples/127-codegenHasSlotDynRef/` + `CodegenHasSlotDynRefTest` (two tests: present/absent named+indexed slots without throwing; `hasDynamicRef` agreeing with `getDynamicRef` for present/absent keys).
- **Allocation watchdog counter on `CellCoord`** — `bh.ui.UIMultiAnimGridTypes.CellCoord` (allocated on the per-mouse-move / per-drag-tick grid hit-test path) gained a `creationCount` static counter gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`. Required promoting the `@:structInit` class to an explicit `new(col, row)` constructor (the synthetic ctor cannot run the increment); the `{col: x, row: y}` literal call sites are unchanged. See the matching `### Changed` entry for the grid hover no-alloc optimization this counter pins.

### Changed
- **Release workflow is now gated on the full test suite** — `release-and-publish.yml` previously built, tagged, and `haxelib submit`-ed with no test step, so failing tests could still ship. The workflow is restructured into `check-version` → `tests` → `build-and-publish`: the tests job reuses `tests.yml` (new `workflow_call` trigger with a `skip-report-deploy` input so the release run doesn't race the regular push run's GitHub Pages deploy) and runs the complete Standard + Dev matrix; tagging and publishing only happen after both pass.
- **`UIMultiAnimGrid.onMouseMove` no longer allocates a `CellCoord` when the cursor stays within the hovered cell** — the hover path called `cellAtPoint()` (which always allocates a `CellCoord` on a hit) on every move, then discarded it via `cellCoordsEqual` when the hit matched the current `hoveredCell`. A cursor jiggling inside one cell — or a game re-dispatching moves while the pointer is stationary — leaked one `CellCoord` per move. Fix: new alloc-free `cellAtPointInto(sceneX, sceneY):Bool` writes the hit cell's col/row into `_hitTestCol`/`_hitTestRow` instance scratch and returns whether a cell exists; `hitTestRect`/`hitTestHex` were rewritten as `hitTestRectInto`/`hitTestHexInto` that write the scratch instead of allocating. `onMouseMove` compares the scratch col/row against `hoveredCell` and allocates a `CellCoord` only when the hover actually changes. The public `cellAtPoint` is unchanged (still allocates, for callers that retain the result — click, drag-find). Pinned by two regression tests in `GridCellHoverRegressionTest` (`testRepeatedMouseMoveOverSameCellDoesNotAllocateCellCoord` rect, `testRepeatedMouseMoveOverSameHexCellDoesNotAllocateCellCoord` hex) asserting zero `CellCoord.creationCount` delta across 100 same-cell moves after warm-up.
- **`applyUpdates` walks a flat `changedParamList` array instead of `changedParams.keys()` (eliminates the last per-call iterator alloc, refines "applyUpdates now uses a reverse index keyed by paramName" below)** — the reverse-index entry left a single per-call allocation: the two changed-param walks (mark relevant trackeds, collect dynamicRef forwarding bindings) iterated `changedParams.keys()`, and on HashLink each `.keys()` call heap-allocates a Map iterator object — so every non-batched `setParameter` (a UI hover/press/drag hot path) leaked two transient iterators. Fix: new `changedParamList:Array<String>` kept in sync with the `changedParams` map — pushed on first set of a param (guarded by `changedParams.exists` so a re-set within a batch dedups), `resize(0)` alongside every `changedParams.clear()`. Both walks now iterate the flat array; the map is retained only for its O(1) `.exists()` membership checks (`findTransitionSpec`, dynamic name bindings). Capacity persists across calls, so steady-state is zero-alloc. New `changedParamsKeysIterCount` instrumentation counter (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`); regression test `DynamicRefTest.testApplyUpdatesDoesNotAllocateChangedParamsIterators` asserts the counter delta is 0 across 20 follow-up `setParameter` calls after warm-up.
- **`UIScreenBase.update()` skips iterating the postponed `customAddToLayer` queue when it is empty** — `update()` unconditionally ran `for (obj => v in postCustomAddToLayer)` every frame, allocating a key-value iterator over the map even in the common steady state where nothing is queued (the map is only populated when an element defers its scene-graph placement via `customAddToLayer` returning `Postponed`). Fix: new `postCustomAddToLayerPending:Bool` flag, set true at the two enqueue sites and cleared after the drain; `update()` only enters the drain block (and only allocates the iterator) when the flag is set. New `postponedDrainCount` instrumentation counter (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`); new `UIScreenUpdateAllocationTest.testUpdateSkipsPostponedDrainWhenQueueEmpty` asserts zero drains across 30 empty-steady-state frames, exactly one drain when an element is queued, and zero drains again afterward.
- **`MultiAnimBuilder` recycles `StoredBuilderState` records through a free-list pool** — the previous fix for the empty-Map/anon-struct allocation in `applyUpdates` (entry under `### Fixed` below — `pushBuilderStateNoReset()`) eliminated the dead `indexedParams = []` and `builderParams = {}` writes but still allocated a fresh 6-field `{indexedParams, builderParams, currentNode, incrementalMode, incrementalContext, currentInternalResults}` anon-struct on every push, even after `popBuilderState` had just thrown one away. `applyUpdates` pushes/pops once per call and `setParameter` calls `applyUpdates` once per non-batched UI event (every hover transition, drag step, slider tick), so a typical interactive screen burned thousands of throwaway anon-struct allocations per minute. Fix: new `stateStackPool:Array<StoredBuilderState>` field on `MultiAnimBuilder`. Both push variants (`pushBuilderState` resetting, `pushBuilderStateNoReset` non-resetting) drain the pool before allocating a fresh literal. `popBuilderState` nulls the reference fields (`currentNode`, `incrementalContext`, `currentInternalResults`) — so the pooled record doesn't pin destroyed nodes / contexts / results during long idle periods — and pushes the cleared record onto the pool. `indexedParams` / `builderParams` are non-nullable in the typedef and get fully overwritten on the next push, so they're left alone. New `pushBuilderStateNoResetAllocCount:Int` watchdog (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`) counts pool-miss allocations on the hot path — one regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testSetParameterReusesPooledBuilderStateAcrossApplyUpdates`) warms the pool with 3 calls then asserts the counter delta is 0 across 10 follow-up steady-state `setParameter` calls.
- **`UIDefaultController.getEventElements` reuses a cached hit-test callback closure instead of allocating one per input event** — the previous fix for the `Array<UIElement>` and sort comparator allocations (entry below) hoisted `_hits` to a final instance field and `_priorityComparator` to a static field, but the streaming-arm callback `function(element) { if (element.containsPoint(pos)) _hits.push(element); }` still captured the per-call `pos` local and was reconstructed on every dispatched input event (hover, click, wheel, key). On a busy mouse-move sweep at 60 fps that's 60 throwaway closures per second per UI screen. Fix: new `_hitsPos:Null<Point>` instance field threaded into a `_hitsCallback:UIElement->Void` final field assigned once in the constructor (mirrors the `_updateCallback` / `_updateDt` pattern already used for the update path). `getEventElements` writes `pos` into `_hitsPos` before calling `integration.forEachElement(SETReceiveEvents, _hitsCallback)` and clears it back to null on exit so the field doesn't pin a stale `Point` between events. Net: zero per-event closure allocations. One regression test in `EventPriorityTest` (`testGetEventElementsReusesCachedCallbackInstanceAcrossInputEvents`) via a new `CallbackTrackingIntegration` mock that records every `forEachElement(SETReceiveEvents, …)` callback — fires 6 different input shapes (move ×2, click push, click release, wheel, key) and asserts every captured callback is the same instance via `Reflect.compareMethods`.
- **`applyUpdates` now uses a reverse index keyed by paramName for tracked expressions and dynamicRef bindings** — `IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` previously walked every entry of `trackedExpressions` and `dynamicRefBindings` on every `setParameter`, scanning each entry's `paramRefs` / `referencedParams` against `changedParams`. Work was O(T·R) per call — with `T` ≈ tens-to-hundreds of trackeds on a typical UI screen (cards, hex cells, dropdowns), and the inner `Map.exists` lookup happening on every paramRef of every tracked, the cost dominated the per-mouse-move `setParameter("status", …)` path even when only one or two trackeds depended on the changed param. Fix: two new reverse-index maps on `IncrementalUpdateContext` — `trackedByParam:Map<String, Array<TrackedExpression>>` and `dynamicRefBindingsByParam:Map<String, Array<DynamicRefBinding>>` — populated alongside the main arrays in `trackExpression()` / `trackDynamicRef()` and pruned in `cleanupDestroyedSubtree()`. The two anonymous structs were promoted to private classes (`TrackedExpression`, `DynamicRefBinding`) so each entry carries a `gen:Int` field for fire-once dedup across paramRef overlaps. `applyUpdates` now bumps a per-cycle `applyGen` counter, walks `changedParams.keys()` → reverse-index lookup → marks/fires relevant entries — zero per-call allocations beyond the `keys()` iterator. Pass-1 (collect unique forwarding contexts) and pass-2 (dispatch under `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate`) of the dynamicRef forwarding loop are now driven by the same reverse-index walk: pass-1 fills a reusable `fwdBindingScratch:Array<DynamicRefBinding>` scratch in addition to `fwdCtxScratch`, and pass-2 walks the scratch (size = relevant bindings only) instead of re-scanning every binding per unique context. The defensive `!hasChanges` fallback (fire every tracked) is preserved for safety, though it's currently unreachable since `setParameter` sets `hasChanges=true` before invoking `applyUpdates`. Two new always-on instrumentation counters (`trackedRelevantScanCount`, `dynamicRefBindingRelevantScanCount`) count entries actually fired/dispatched per `applyUpdates`. Two regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testSetParameterScansOnlyTrackedsReferencingChangedParam`, `testSetParameterScansOnlyDynamicRefBindingsReferencingChangedParam`) build a programmable with 5 trackeds / 5 dynamicRef bindings, each referencing a distinct param, and assert `setParameter("a", …)` examines exactly 1 entry instead of 5.

### Fixed
- **`repeatable2d` had no incremental-mode handling — param-dependent counts silently froze after `setParameter`** — the REPEAT case collects `repeatParamRefs`, marks untracked params, suppresses conditional tracking for the body, and registers a structural-rebuild expression; REPEAT2D did none of it, so `repeatable2d($x, $y, step($nx, dx: 10), step(2, dy: 10))` built with `nx=2` stayed at 2×2 children forever after `setParameter("nx", 3)`. Fix: the full REPEAT incremental machinery is ported to REPEAT2D — iterator refs from both axes plus child-conditional refs feed a structural rebuild closure (`trackExpression`) that re-resolves both axes and rebuilds all iterations; the body disables incremental tracking during param-dependent builds (with up-front `markUntrackedParamsInSubtree`, second loop var excluded via the refs list) and suppresses conditional tracking for constant-count incremental builds, mirroring REPEAT. The axis resolution and double loop are shared between initial build and rebuild via local helpers (new private `Repeat2DAxis` typedef). New `Repeat2DIncrementalTest` (param-dependent count rebuild, plus full-build/incremental parity anchors for loop-var and param+`@else` conditionals — the conditional shapes rendered correctly before the port too and must stay correct).
- **`collectParamRefs` missed six `ReferenceableValue` classes — expressions like `tint: palette(pal, $idx)` were frozen in incremental mode** — the builder's ref collector only recursed into references, binops, parenthesis, ternaries, unary ops, and array-element indices. Refs inside `RVColor`/`RVColorXY` palette indices, `RVMethodCall`/`RVChainedMethodCall` args, `RVCallbacks`/`RVCallbacksWithIndex`, `RVArray` elements, and the `RVElementOfArray` array name were invisible to every consumer — tracked expressions (`trackExtendedFormExpressions` registers nothing on an empty ref list), repeat rebuild triggers, and dynamicRef forwarding — so `setParameter` on such a param silently never re-fired the expression. Fix: `collectParamRefs` now recurses into all of them. New `PaletteIndexTrackingTest` (incremental `tint: palette(pal, $idx)` re-tints on `setParameter("idx", …)`; full-build baseline).
- **Codegen `step($dx, $dy, N)` param-dependent offsets silently became 0 and `range($start, $end, $step)` param-dependent start/step produced loop values 0, 1, 2…** — `resolveRepeatInfo` substituted 0/1 literals when an iterator scalar wasn't statically resolvable, in both the static-unroll path (offsets baked as `0*i`) and the runtime rebuild path (`_rt_val = Std.int(0 + _rt_i * 1)`, `setPosition` skipped entirely); the builder resolves these at build time and re-resolves on rebuild. The rebuild trigger list also only carried the count refs (for ranges, only `end`), and the rebuild early-out compared only the count — so `range($a, $a + 6)` with a shifted `$a` (same count, new values) never rebuilt. Fix: a repeat with param-dependent per-iteration scalars routes through the runtime rebuild path; the rebuild method now takes `(count, start, step, dx, dy)` — runtime `rvToExprInt` expressions where param-dependent, literals otherwise — guards on *all five* values via tracking fields (count clamped to ≥ 0 so the `-1` sentinel always runs the first build), and applies `_rt_cont.setPosition(_rt_dx * _rt_i, _rt_dy * _rt_i)` per iteration. New `collectRepeatTriggerRefs` feeds count + offset + bound refs into the trigger/untracked-exclusion list, mirroring the builder's `repeatParamRefs`. Fixture `test/examples/137-codegenRepeatParamParity/` + `CodegenRepeatParamParityTest` (static-count and runtime-path param offsets incl. `setParameter("dx", …)` reposition, range loop values incl. `setParameter("start", …)` re-derive; builder baselines).
- **Codegen always wrapped `repeatable` iterations in one container — a repeatable inside `flow` presented a single child, so iterations overlapped** — the builder only creates a wrapper when it's needed (`dx`/`dy` offsets, layout iterator, or incremental rebuild), otherwise building iteration children directly into the parent so `h2d.Flow` lays out each child; codegen created the `_eN` container unconditionally. Fix: `processRepeat` mirrors the builder's rule — a fully static, zero-offset, non-conditional repeat with no own position (`ZERO`) unrolls its children directly into the parent field (the node still joins the sibling chain for `@else`/`@default` bookkeeping); conditional, positioned, offset, or param-dependent repeats keep the container (needed for visibility toggling and `removeChildren`). Same fixture + `CodegenRepeatParamParityTest.testFlowRepeat_*` (flow must see 3 iteration children; builder baseline).
- **Codegen `$ctx.width` / `$ctx.height` crashed `create()` with a raw null access** — the emitted expression was `this.getScene().width`, but `getScene()` is null while the generated constructor runs (the instance isn't attached to a scene yet), so any programmable using `$ctx.width` in codegen died with `Null access .width`; the builder resolves the same expression from `BuilderParameters.scene` and throws a structured `BuilderError` when no scene is provided. Fix: codegen now emits `ProgrammableBuilder.ctxSceneWidth/ctxSceneHeight(this, this._pb)` — prefers the live scene when attached, falls back to the new injectable `ProgrammableBuilder.scene` field (set it on the factory like `tweenManager`; the runtime builder gets the same via `BuilderParameters.scene`), and otherwise throws a `BuilderError` matching the builder's error semantics. Fixture `test/examples/138-codegenCtxSize/` + `CodegenCtxSizeTest` (builder and codegen both fail structurally without a scene).
- **`AllocationSmokeTest` was never registered in the test runner** — the test was fully written and advertised in the rc.5 CHANGELOG and `.claude/rules/testing-and-debugging.md`, but was missing from `TestApp`'s `addCase` list, so it never executed. Now registered (and passing): +6 unit assertions in both Standard and Dev runs.
- **README code samples fixed against the real API** (first-contact surface — every listed snippet failed to compile or called nonexistent methods): `animSM.addCommand(SwitchState("idle"), ExecuteNow)` → `animSM.play("idle")`; `screenManager.showScreen(...)` → `switchTo(...)`; `screenManager.showDialog(...)` → `modalDialog(...)`; `public override function load()/onScreenEvent()` dropped the `override` (they implement **abstract** methods — `override` is a compile error) and `onScreenEvent`'s `source` is `Null<UIElement>`; `addCheckbox`/`addSlider`/`addRadio` samples gained the required `settings:ResolvedSettings` argument. Quick-start also gained install commands (haxelib + lix) and the `.atlas2` macro note; license section links `ATTRIBUTION.md`.
- **Non-numeric conditional values on numeric params and non-boolean values on `bool` params silently diverged between builder and codegen** — the follow-up to the `string`/`float` conditional fix below. `@(intParam => foo)` / `@(uintParam => …)` / `@(rangeParam => …)` / hex- & grid-direction params, and `@(boolParam => maybe)`, routed a non-numeric / non-boolean lexeme through `stringToConditional`'s `default` arm to `CoStringValue`. The builder then silently never matched (`Std.string(0/1) != "maybe"`), while codegen emitted `_field == "maybe"` over an `Int` field — an `Int == String` macro compile error that broke the whole `@:manim` build. Fix: `stringToConditional` now rejects a non-`Std.parseInt`-able value for numeric param types (`int`/`uint`/`range`/`hexDirection`/`gridDirection`) and a non-`true/false/yes/no/1/0` value for `bool` params at parse time, with an actionable message naming the offending value — parallel to the existing `PPTFloat` equality guard and the unknown-enum-member guard. Covers single-value (`@(p => v)`), quoted (`@(p => "v")`), bracket multi-value (`@(p => [a, b])`), and `@switch` arms. Seven `ParserErrorTest` methods (`testNumericParamRejects*` ×4, `testBoolParamRejectsNonBooleanConditionalValue`, plus `testNumericParamAcceptsNumericConditionalValue` / `testBoolParamAcceptsBooleanConditionalValue` controls guarding against over-rejection).
- **Codegen ternary `?(… == …)` / `?(… != …)` on an enum or string operand was an `Int == String` compile error** — the builder's `resolveAsBool` evaluates `==` / `!=` as STRING equality (enum params compared by value NAME, string params by content), but codegen's `rvToExpr` emitted a numeric comparison for the ternary condition. With an enum or string operand (both typed `:Int` / `:String` in codegen), `?($status == "hover") a : b` generated `_status == "hover"` — `Int == String`, a macro compile error — so the expression could not be written in a codegen programmable at all. Fix: new `isStringOrEnumRV` helper; when either side of `==` / `!=` is a string or enum reference, `rvToExpr` emits a string comparison with `forString=true` operands (enum refs routed through their `names[]` lookup via `enumToStringExpr`, the rest wrapped in `Std.string`), matching the builder. Pure-numeric operands keep the numeric comparison (same boolean result). New fixture `test/examples/135-codegenTernaryEquality/` + `CodegenTernaryEqualityParityTest` (enum `==`, enum `!=`, string `==`; builder baseline + codegen + false-branch controls).
- **Codegen recurring indexed `#name[$i] dynamicRef(...)` in nested loops silently dropped all but the last iteration** — the sibling fix to the recurring indexed name/slot collision below. A 1-D indexed dynamicRef whose index recurs (e.g. `#cell[$j]` inside `repeatable($i) { repeatable($j) { … } }`, or a duplicate-value iterator) collided on the `"name idx"` key in codegen's `indexedDynamicRefFields` map — last-writer-wins, silently discarding the earlier iterations' result fields. Fix: codegen now rejects the second registration under a key with a macro-time `Context.error` naming the colliding index and pointing to a unique index or a 2-D indexed name (`#name[indexX, indexY]`) — symmetric with the runtime builder (which already rejects the recurring key via its duplicate-dynamicRef `BuilderError`) and the existing codegen indexed-name / slot guards. New `NestedIndexedCollisionTest.testBuilder_NestedRecurringDynamicRefIndex_Throws` (asserts the recurring `#cell[$j]` dynamicRef across nested loops is rejected at build time).
- **Codegen param-dependent compound `repeatable` counts truncated once instead of per node** — the static-resolve and integer-coordinate truncation paths were already fixed (entries below), but a `repeatable` whose count *references a parameter* (so it can't be statically unrolled) routes through the rebuild path (`rebuildRepeatChildren` / `rebuildRepeat2DChildren`), which built the count expression with plain `rvToExpr` — float math with a single final truncation. The builder's `resolveAsInteger` truncates at every node, so `step($n / 2 * 2)` with `n=7` ran builder `Std.int(7/2)*2 = 6` iterations vs codegen `Std.int((7.0/2.0)*2.0) = 7`. Fix: both rebuild paths now build the RangeIterator start/end/step and the StepIterator count via `rvToExprInt` (per-node `Std.int`), matching the builder. New `repeatCountParamTrunc` programmable in fixture `test/examples/132-codegenIntTruncation/` + `CodegenIntTruncationParityTest.testRepeatCountParam_Builder_TruncatesPerNode` / `_Codegen_TruncatesPerNode`.
- **DevBridge `click_button` RPC alias didn't route to a handler** — `click_button` is documented (`docs/devbridge.md`) and exposed as an MCP tool as an alias of `click_interactive`, but the dispatch `switch` only matched `click_interactive`, so a `click_button` call fell through to the unknown-method default and errored. Fix: the dispatch arm now matches `"click_interactive" | "click_button"`, both routing to `handleClickInteractive`. Regression test `DevBridgeTest.testDispatch_clickButtonAliasRoutesToClickInteractive` asserts the alias reaches the handler (same `"Interactive not found"` error as the canonical name for a missing interactive) and produces output identical to the canonical name.
- **Conditionals on `string` and `float` params diverged between builder and codegen** — `@(stringParam => 2)` (integer-parseable value), `@(floatParam => 1)`, and string `@switch` pipe arms (`"alpha" | "beta"`) all routed through paths that assumed integer/enum semantics. `stringToConditional` had no `PPTString` or `PPTFloat` arm, so both fell into the `default` `Std.parseInt` branch → `CoValue(int)`: a string param then hit the builder's `'invalid param types'` throw and codegen emitted `_version == 2` (`String == Int`, a macro compile error); a float param threw in the builder but compiled to a silent `_weight == 1.0` match in codegen. String `@switch` pipe arms were routed to `CoEnums`, which codegen resolves via `findEnumIndex` (`Std.parseInt`) — dropping non-numeric arms (the arm became unreachable and `label="beta"` fell to `default`) and emitting `case <int>:` over a `String` subject for numeric ones (compile error). Fix: `stringToConditional` now maps `PPTString → CoStringValue` (string equality on both backends) and rejects `PPTFloat` equality (`=>` / `!=`) at parse time with an actionable message — consistent with `@switch` already rejecting float params; float comparisons (`>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`) and ranges (`a..b`) are unaffected. String `@switch` pipe arms now route through `CoAnyOf(CoStringValue)` (plain string equality) instead of `CoEnums`. New `StringFloatConditionalParityTest` (builder + codegen string equality, codegen pipe-arm match) and three `ParserErrorTest` methods (float `=>`/`!=` rejected, comparison/range still parse). Fixture `test/examples/134-stringFloatConditional/`.
- **Recurring 1-D indexed `#name[$i]` / `#name[$i] slot` in nested loops silently diverged or hard-failed** — a 1-D indexed name or slot whose index recurs (e.g. `#tile[$j]` inside `repeatable($i) { repeatable($j) { … } }`, or a duplicate-value array iterator) produces a colliding key. The runtime builder pushed onto an array under `"name idx"` and silently returned the first iteration (named) / coexisted (slot); codegen emitted duplicate `switch` cases (named) or declared the same `_slotHandle_<base>_<index>` field twice ("Duplicate class field declaration", a cryptic compile error). Fix: reject the collision loudly on both backends. The builder throws `BuilderError` (`code="indexed_name_collision"` / `"indexed_slot_collision"`) on the second registration under a key within one build pass — `cleanupDestroyedSubtree` splices stale `names`/`slots` entries before an incremental rebuild re-registers, so rebuilds don't false-positive. Codegen emits a clear `Context.error` naming the colliding index and pointing to a unique index or a 2-D indexed name (`#name[indexX, indexY]`). `NestedIndexedCollisionTest` (builder reject for recurring named + slot indices, plus a single-loop control that must still build cleanly).
- **Codegen integer-division and integer-coordinate truncation diverged from the builder for compound / float-operand expressions** — the runtime builder's `resolveAsInteger` truncates at *every* node (operands of `+`/`-`/`*`, the quotient of `/` and `div`, the result of `%`) and integerizes `div` operands before dividing; codegen's `rvToExpr` is a single float-valued resolver that truncated only once at the coordinate site. So `9.0 div 2.5` gave builder `Std.int(9/2)=4` vs codegen `Std.int(9.0/2.5)=3`; `$grid.pos(7/2*2)` gave builder `Std.int(7/2)*2=6` (cell 6) vs codegen `Std.int((7/2)*2)=7` (cell 7); and `%` with float operands in an integer context (`$grid.pos($a % $b)`, `a=7.0`/`b=2.5`) gave builder integer mod `1` vs codegen float mod `2`. (The single-op grid case, e.g. `$grid.pos($col/2)`, was already fixed in the preceding `$grid.pos` entry; this covers compound and `div`/`%` operands.) Fix: new `ProgrammableCodeGen.rvToExprInt` mirrors `resolveAsInteger` (per-node `Std.int`, integerized `div`/`%` operands). It now backs the operands of `div` in `rvToExpr` and every integer coordinate argument — `$grid.pos` x/y and the `$hex.*` integer args (offset/doubled col & row, corner count/cornerIndex, edge direction) — at the position-emit, pixel-offset, and value-extraction sites. Float-context coordinates (`$hex.cube` q/r/s, `$hex.pixel`, corner/edge `factor`) keep `rvToExpr`; offset-context `%` stays float on both backends (the builder's `resolveAsNumber` agrees). New `CodegenIntTruncationParityTest` cases cover `div` (offset + grid), `%`, and compound `7/2*2` (builder baseline + codegen).
- **`ScreenManager.closeDialogWithTransition` left a broken state when closing a dialog opened over another dialog** — the animated-close path (transition != `None`) hand-rolled the restore of `previousMode` with an inline `switch` that only handled `Single` and `MasterAndSingle` (whose underlying screens stay in the scene while a dialog is open, so they only need their controllers restored). A `Dialog` `previousMode` fell through `default:` and got nothing — but unlike the other modes, a previous dialog is *removed from the scene* when the current dialog opens over it, so after `mode = previousMode` the underlying dialog was left detached and absent from `activeScreenControllers`: invisible and dead to input. The instant-close path (`transition == None`) was unaffected because it delegates to `updateScreenMode(previousMode)`, which has a correct `Dialog -> Dialog` branch that re-adds the dialog. Fix: the animated-close controller-restore switch now has a `case Dialog(prevDialog, ...)` arm that re-adds `prevDialog` to the dialog layer (if absent), fires its `UIEntering`/`Entering`/`LifecycleControllerStarted`, and restores it as the sole active controller — mirroring the instant path. The app-side close-first convention already avoided this; the fix protects any dialog-from-dialog callback. Regression test `ScreenManagerDialogTransitionTest.testAnimatedDialogClose_RestoresUnderlyingDialog` (opens d1, opens d2 over it, animated-closes d2, asserts d1 is re-attached and back in `activeScreenControllers`).
- **Conditionals on inline children inside a param-dependent `repeatable` were dropped in codegen** — `@(show=>true) bitmap(...)` (or any `@()`/`@else`/`@default` on a bitmap/text/ninepatch/graphics/point/mask/layers/flow child) inside a `repeatable` whose count depends on a `$param` rendered on *every* iteration in codegen, while the runtime builder evaluates conditionals per iteration via `resolveConditionalChildren`. Loop-var conditionals (`@($i => 0)`) were equally ignored. Fix: new `generateRuntimeChildrenExprs` wraps each runtime-repeat child in a chain-aware runtime `if` built from the same condition machinery the static-unroll path uses (`generateVisibilityCondition`/`condMapToExpr` — params resolve to instance fields, loop vars to their runtime idents). Builder-forwarded kinds (interactive, dynamicRef, …) gain correct `@else`/`@default` chain gating too — previously they always built because `shouldBuildInFullMode` alone has no sibling-chain context. Conditional-only params inside the body remain untracked for `setParameter` (existing pinned policy — the gate re-evaluates on body rebuild). New fixture `test/examples/131-codegenRepeatConditional/` + `CodegenRepeatConditionalTest` (builder baseline + codegen, param and loop-var conditionals).
- **Codegen statically-resolved repeat counts rounded instead of truncating** — `tryResolveStaticInt` used `Math.round` for float literals and `OpDiv`, so `repeatable($i, step(7/2, …))` unrolled 4 iterations in codegen where the builder (`resolveAsInteger` → `Std.int`) runs 3. The param-dependent count path already truncated (`Std.int(countExpr)`) — only the static-resolve path diverged. Fix: `Std.int` in both `tryResolveStaticInt` arms. Fixture `test/examples/132-codegenIntTruncation/` + `CodegenIntTruncationParityTest.testStepCount_*`.
- **Codegen `$grid.pos(...)` kept fractional cell coordinates** — the builder resolves grid cell coordinates through `resolveAsInteger` before `resolveAsGrid`, so `$grid.pos($col/2, 0)` with `col=3` lands on cell 1 (x = 1·spacing). Codegen emitted raw float math (`$gx * spacing`), landing at 1.5·spacing — for element positions, named grid systems, pixels/graphics coordinates, and `$grid.pos(...).x/.y` value extraction (static and runtime, including the 4-arg offset form whose offsets the builder also truncates). Fix: `Std.int(...)` wrap at all eight grid codegen sites. Same fixture + `CodegenIntTruncationParityTest.testGridCoord_*`.
- **Codegen `getDynamicRef` silently returned the last-built site when unnamed sibling `dynamicRef($X)` sites collide** — the codegen key registry was a plain last-writer-wins `Map`, so two (or sixteen) unnamed siblings targeting the same programmable shared one key and one result field; `getDynamicRef("X")` handed back whichever site was built last with no signal — while `BuilderResult.getDynamicRef` throws on ambiguity to force `#name` disambiguation. Fix: the registry now counts writers per key; collided keys get a generated `getDynamicRef` arm that throws `BuilderError` with the builder's `#name`-disambiguation message (`hasDynamicRef` stays presence-only/never-throws, matching the builder). A second *explicit* `#name` on an occupied key is now a macro-time compile error, mirroring the builder's build-time throw. The three `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testDynamicRefCodegen*` tests that pinned the old silent behavior on fixture 73's 16-way-collided `statusBar` key now use the fixture's `#probe` handle (added for exactly this) and assert the collision throw. New fixture `test/examples/133-codegenDynRefKeyContract/` + `CodegenDynRefKeyContractTest.testUnnamedCollision_*`.
- **Codegen `#name dynamicRef($param)` was keyed by the live template name instead of the explicit `#name`** — the literal-target variant was fixed in rc.5 (see the `#name dynamicRef(...)` entry below), but the dynamic-name branch (template named by a `$param`) still dropped the explicit name: the generated dispatcher compared against the current template name, so `getDynamicRef("panel")` returned null and the working key changed on every template swap — the documented contract is the opposite (`#foo dynamicRef($template)` stays keyed as `"foo"` across swaps, per `resolveDynamicRefKey`). Fix: dynamic-name entries carry the explicit name; `getDynamicRef`/`hasDynamicRef` match it when present (live-template-name matching remains for unnamed sites). Explicit-name collisions against any other dynamicRef key are a macro-time compile error. Same fixture + `CodegenDynRefKeyContractTest.testNamedDynamicTarget_*` (builder baseline + codegen, before/after template swap).
- **`@switch` active arm was needlessly torn down + rebuilt when a param referenced only in an *inactive* sibling arm changed** — the rebuild gate was the union of param refs across *all* arms (builder: `switchParamRefs` collected over every arm; codegen: `_applyVisibility`'s union gate). So changing a param used only in an inactive arm fired the active arm's full teardown (`cleanupDestroyedSubtree` + `removeChildren` + rebuild), restarting any stateanim playhead and re-seeding any particles inside the active arm even though nothing in it depended on the changed param. Fix: collect param refs *per arm*, track the currently-active arm index, and only rebuild when the build is initial, the matched arm flipped, the changed param is the switch key, or the changed param is referenced by the *active* arm's own refs. Applied symmetrically in `MultiAnimBuilder` (per-arm `trackExpression` gate, new `IncrementalUpdateContext.isParamDirty`) and `ProgrammableCodeGen` (inner active-arm relevance switch in the generated update block). In-arm `$param` interpolation in the *active* arm still rebuilds as before (unchanged, still tested). Two regression tests: `BuilderUnitTest.testSwitchActiveArmSurvivesInactiveArmParamChange` (runtime builder) and `CodegenSwitchInactiveArmTest.testCodegenSwitchActiveArmSurvivesInactiveArmParamChange` (codegen, fixture `test/examples/130-switchInactiveArmRebuild/`), both asserting active-arm object identity is preserved across an inactive-arm-only param change.
- **Bracket multi-value conditionals on non-enum discrete params silently diverged between builder and codegen** — `@(p => [a, b])` / `@(p != [a, b])` unconditionally built raw string-keyed `CoEnums` and read values via `expectIdentifierOrString`, diverging from the single-value and `@switch` pipe-arm paths. Symptoms: `bool` brackets never matched at runtime (`Value(1)` vs the strings `"true"`/`"false"`); `int` collapsed to `_level == 0` in codegen (`enumValueToIndex` → 0) and integer literals like `[1, 3]` failed to parse at all; `string` brackets emitted `String == 0` in codegen (compile error). Fix: new `MacroManimParser.makeBracketMultiValue` reads values via `parseConditionalValue`, validates enum members, then routes enum params to `CoEnums` (raw-string, as codegen's `enumValueToIndex` expects) and every other discrete type — string, int/uint, color, bool, flags, and undefined loop vars — through `stringToConditional`/`stringToConditionalGeneric` OR'd via `CoAnyOf`. New fixture `test/examples/130-bracketMultiValueConditional/` + `BracketMultiValueConditionalTest` (4 tests: runtime + codegen, affirmative + negated membership across int/bool/string arms).
- **Codegen `staticRef($param)` with a param-driven name built a nonexistent programmable** — `staticRef($which)` where `$which` is a string parameter naming the target collapsed `RVReference` to the literal string `"which"` in `ProgrammableCodeGen` STATIC_REF dispatch, so it built a programmable literally named after the parameter (an empty object). The runtime builder resolves `$which` through `indexedParams` (`resolveRefName`); the sibling dynamicRef path already routed param-named refs to `generateDynamicNameRefCreate`. Fix: STATIC_REF now resolves the ref name to a runtime expression (loop-var substitution, param resolution via the extracted `resolveProgrammableNameExpr` helper shared with the dynamic-name dynamicRef path, or legacy literal fallback). New fixture `test/examples/128-codegenStaticRefDynamicName/` + `CodegenStaticRefDynamicNameTest` (builder baseline + codegen, default and overridden name).
- **Codegen `#name dynamicRef(...)` was keyed by its target programmable name instead of the explicit `#name`** — the codegen dynamicRef tracking ignored `node.updatableName`, so `getDynamicRef("name")` returned null while `BuilderResult` (which keys by the explicit `#name` via `resolveDynamicRefKey`) resolved it. Fix: an explicit `#name` prefix is now the lookup key in codegen, matching the builder. New assertion in `CodegenHasSlotDynRefTest.testCodegenNamedDynamicRefKeyedByExplicitName` (fixture gains `#leafRef dynamicRef($leaf, w=>12)`).
- **Codegen indexed `#name[$i] dynamicRef(...)` inside a static-count repeatable was unaddressable** — codegen unrolls a literal-count `repeatable` at macro time, but each iteration keyed its dynamicRef under the shared `_comp_<prog>` field (last-writer-wins), so only the final iteration was reachable. The builder keys each iteration `"seg 0"`, `"seg 1"`, … via `resolveDynamicRefKey`. Fix: new `indexedDynamicRefFields` map registers each resolved `"base idx"` key against a per-site result field (`_dynref_lit_<fieldName>`), folded into the `getDynamicRef`/`hasDynamicRef` dispatchers; indexed staticRef/dynamicRef sites always get their own per-site field regardless of forwarded refs. Added `getDynamicRefByIndex(name, index)` convenience to both `BuilderResult` and codegen instances. New fixture `test/examples/129-codegenIndexedDynamicRef/` + `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testCodegenIndexedDynamicRefInStaticRepeatIsAddressable` (asserts `"seg 0".."seg 2"` resolve, are distinct, and match `getDynamicRefByIndex`).
- **`BuilderResult.getInteractives()` exposed detached conditional-arm interactives as ghost click targets** — a runtime-builder conditional (`@(open=>true) interactive(...)`) eagerly builds its interactive and detaches the arm subtree (`removeChild`) when the condition flips false, but the registration stayed in the static `interactives` list at stale coords, so `getInteractives()` (and thus the screen's `syncInteractivesFrom` diff) left a ghost click target. Fix: `getInteractives()` now returns only interactives whose parent chain still reaches the result root (a reachability walk, so interactives nested below a detached arm container — whose immediate parent stays non-null — are also dropped), matching codegen which walks the live scene graph. Two regression tests in `InteractiveEventTest`: `testHiddenConditionalInteractiveIsNotExposed` and `testHiddenConditionalNestedInteractiveIsNotExposed`.
- **`buildWithParameters` missing-element error read "could find element"** — typo fixed to "could not find element" (`BuilderUnitTest` assertion updated to match).
- **Codegen `hasDynamicRef` disagreed with `getDynamicRef` for a dynamicRef declared inside a `@switch` arm** — the generated `hasDynamicRef` presence companion iterated only `repeatSinkFields` when consulting runtime sinks, while the `getDynamicRef` dispatcher (fixed in the entry below) iterates all `sinkFields`. So a dynamicRef whose only declaration lived in a `@switch` arm was *reachable* via `getDynamicRef("embed")` but reported absent by `hasDynamicRef("embed")` — breaking the documented guard-before-access pattern and diverging from `BuilderResult`, where both methods read the same map and can never disagree. Fix: `hasDynamicRef`'s sink-fallthrough loop now iterates `sinkFields` (switch arms + param-dependent repeats), mirroring the dispatcher. New assertion in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testCodegenSwitchArmDynamicRefIsReachable` (`hasDynamicRef("embed")` must return true for the switch-arm dynamicRef).
- **Unknown enum member in a single-value conditional silently diverged between builder and codegen** — the follow-up to the multi-value fix below. Single-value forms — `@(p => hbover)`, `@(p != hbover)`, and `@switch` single-value arms (`hbover: ...`) — routed a typo'd enum member through `stringToConditional` without validating it against the parameter's declared `PPTEnum` members. The builder then silently never matched while codegen turned it into an `Int == String` compile error (the same divergence the multi-value forms guard). Fix: `validateConditionalEnumValues` is now also called on all three single-value paths (negated `@(p != value)`, the `CoNot` backward-compat negate, and the `@switch` single-arm `stringToConditional` path). Three regression tests in `ParserErrorTest`: `testSingleValueMatchConditionalRejectsUnknownEnumValue`, `testSingleValueNegatedConditionalRejectsUnknownEnumValue`, `testSwitchSingleArmRejectsUnknownEnumValue`.
- **Codegen: a `#name dynamicRef(...)` declared inside a `@switch` arm was unreachable via `getDynamicRef` when the programmable had no other static dynamicRef** — the `getDynamicRef` dispatcher was generated only when `hasDynamicRefs || repeatSinkFields.length > 0`, but `@switch` arms register their dynamicRefs into a separate switch sink (not `repeatSinkFields`). A programmable whose *only* dynamicRef lived in a switch arm got no `getDynamicRef` method at all, while the runtime builder resolved it (arm children build into the parent IR). Fix: the dispatcher is now generated when `hasDynamicRefs || sinkFields.length > 0` (covering both switch arms and param-dependent repeats), and its sink-fallthrough loop iterates all `sinkFields` rather than just `repeatSinkFields` — mirroring the `getSlot` / `getUpdatable*` dispatchers. New `#switchArmDynamicRef` programmable in `test/examples/106-switchArmNames/switchArmNames.manim` + `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testCodegenSwitchArmDynamicRefIsReachable` (asserts `getDynamicRef("embed")` resolves on a codegen instance whose only dynamicRef is in the active `@switch` arm).
- **Unknown enum member in a multi-value / pipe conditional silently diverged between builder and codegen** — `@(p => [a, hbover])`, `@(p != [a, hbover])`, and `@switch` pipe arms (`a | hbover { ... }`) build `CoEnums` from raw lexemes without validating them against the parameter's declared enum. A typo'd member then never matched in the builder (silent) but matched enum index 0 in codegen — a silent behavioral divergence. Fix: new `MacroManimParser.validateConditionalEnumValues` rejects any value not in the parameter's `PPTEnum` members at parse time (string params accept any value; loop vars have no type to validate). Three regression tests in `ParserErrorTest`: `testMultiValueMatchConditionalRejectsUnknownEnumValue`, `testMultiValueNegatedConditionalRejectsUnknownEnumValue`, `testSwitchPipeArmRejectsUnknownEnumValue`.
- **`tint_requires_drawable` error message crashed on non-class targets** — the fail-fast for root-level/container `tint:` formatted the offending object via `Type.getClassName(Type.getClass(object))`, which returns null (and the interpolation reads "null") for objects without a concrete class. Switched to `Std.string(Type.typeof(object))` for a robust type description in the error message.
- **`TweenManager.clear()` invoked from a tween's `onComplete` callback double-released the still-completing tween into the shared pool** — `TweenManager.update()` fires `onComplete` *before* its own cleanup releases the tween. A realistic teardown callback (`screenManager.tweens.clear()` from inside `onComplete`, e.g. a screen transition cleaning up the last tween of a sequence) recycled the completing handle into `_pool`, control returned to `update()`'s own cleanup loop, and `Tween.release` ran again on the same instance — pushing it onto `_pool` a second time. Two later `acquire()` calls then handed the same `Tween` to two logical tweens, silently aliasing them on `target` / `elapsed` / `entries` (configuring the second tween clobbered the first). Fix: `Tween` gained a private `pooled:Bool` flag; `release()` is now idempotent — it early-returns when `pooled` is already set, sets the flag before recycling, and the flag is cleared in `acquire()` so reused handles look fresh. One regression test in `TweenManagerTest` (`testClearFromOnCompleteDoesNotDoubleReleaseTween`): tween with `setOnComplete(() -> mgr.clear())` completes, then two fresh tweens are acquired on distinct targets — asserts the two tween instances are distinct and that creating the second did not clobber the first's `target`.
- **`apply { pos: dx, dy }` in codegen overwrote the parent's existing placement instead of composing additively** — `apply { pos: ... }` is supposed to ADD the offset to the parent's base position (mirroring the builder's `addPosition`); the prior codegen path emitted an absolute `setPosition`, so an `@(cond) apply { pos: 5, 5 }` whose parent was a `#box point { pos: 100, 50 ... }` jumped to `(5, 5)` instead of `(105, 55)` and ignored the base placement of any non-root positioned container (root pos is unaffected — it lives in a separate holder). The save/revert path correctly snapshotted the pre-apply `parent.x`/`y`, so an unmatched arm restored the base — but the matched arm's "apply" body wiped it. Fix: both the base position emitter (constructor / param-driven re-fire) and the `apply` entry now use the `coordsToXYExprs` delta form: ctor captures `_applyBaseX/Y` and writes `parent.x = base + delta`; `apply` body writes `parent.x += delta` (revert restores the saved x/y). The `_applyVisibility` reset pass already reverts every entry to its captured base before replaying matched entries, so `+=` composes overlapping pos applies in declaration order without accumulating across rebuilds. New fixture `test/examples/126-codegenApplyPosAdditive/` + `CodegenApplyPosAdditiveTest` with builder AND codegen tests asserting a `#box point { pos: 100, 50 ... @(hover=>true) apply { pos: 5, 5 } }` round-trips `(100, 50) → (105, 55) → (100, 50)` across hover-on / hover-off.
- **Codegen `staticRef($child, p => $parentEnum)` forwarded the parent enum's raw Int index instead of its name, so `create()` threw** — `generateStaticRefCreate` built the forwarded-param map via `rvToExpr(val)`, which emits an enum-typed reference as its raw `Int` storage index. That index flowed into `_pb.buildStaticRef` → `buildWithParameters` → `dynamicValueToIndex`, which stringifies the int (`"0"`) and rejects it against the child's `[a,b,c]` value list — codegen `create()` threw "is not a value" while the runtime builder (which resolves the reference in context) succeeded. Sibling `dynamicRef` sites already forwarded enums by name via `dynamicRefForwardValueExpr`. Fix: `generateStaticRefCreate` now routes the forwarded value through `dynamicRefForwardValueExpr` too, so a parent enum is emitted as its name string and accepted by the child's `setParameter` enum path. New fixture `test/examples/124-codegenStaticRefEnumForwarding/` + `CodegenStaticRefForwardingTest` (builder baseline + codegen) asserting `staticRef($cgStaticFwdEnumChild, mode => $parentMode)` resolves to the right child arm (width 11/22) for both the default `parentMode=a` and an overridden `parentMode=b`.
- **Grid hover hit-test regression test was tied to a voluntary allocation counter that could be silently removed** — the prior fix (Int-packed cell keys) added a `cellKeyStringAllocCount` watchdog and two regression tests that read it. The counter and the increment are entirely under author control, so a future refactor that reverts `cells` to a `StringMap` could survive simply by also dropping the counter (and the tests would fall back to delta-of-zero against a counter that no longer increments). Fix: dropped the `cellKeyStringAllocCount` counter and consolidated the two regression tests into one (`testCellHoverHitTestUsesIntKeyedMapNotStringKey`) that asserts the concrete map type directly — `Std.isOfType(rect.cells, haxe.ds.IntMap)`, the matching `hex.cells` check, and the `layerEntries` inner-map check — an active guard that fails the instant the map type is reverted to String, independent of any voluntary counter. The collision-free packing test (`testCellKeysAreCollisionFreeIncludingNegativeCoords`) is unchanged.
- **Slots / dynamicRefs / indexed names inside a param-dependent repeatable were unreachable on codegen instances** — when a `repeatable`'s count depends on a settable parameter (`repeatable($i, range(0, $rows)) { #cell[$i] slot: ...; #tag[$i] bitmap(...); #embed[$i] dynamicRef(...) }`), codegen builds the body through the runtime-rebuild path (`rebuildRepeatChildren` → `generateRuntimeChildExprs` → `buildNodeByUniqueNameWithParams`). That path built each forwarded child into a throwaway `InternalBuilderResults` and returned only the `h2d.Object`, discarding the slot/dynamicRef/indexed-name registrations — so `instance.getSlot("cell", i)` / `getDynamicRef("embed i")` / `getUpdatableByIndex("tag", i)` returned null or threw on a codegen instance while the equivalent `BuilderResult` resolved them. (Interactives were unaffected — codegen's `getInteractives` walks the live scene graph.) Fix mirrors the existing `@switch` arm sink: each param-dependent repeat (1D and 2D) now allocates a persistent `SwitchArmResults` sink in the ctor; `buildSingleNodeWithParams` gained an optional `sink` param so forwarded children register into it; `_rebuildRepeat_X` evicts the previous count's registrations (`resetRepeatSink` → `removeRegistrationsUnder`, before `removeChildren`) and repopulates on each count change; and the instance `getSlot` / `getUpdatable*` / `getDynamicRef` dispatchers fall through to the repeat sinks. Addressable-named inline children (`#name[$i] bitmap`/`text`/…) are now routed through the builder fallback so their name registration survives. New fixture `test/examples/125-codegenRepeatSlot/` + `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testCodegenParamDepRepeatSlotReachable` (builder-vs-codegen parity for slot, indexed name, and dynamicRef inside a `range(0, $rows)` repeat).
- **Grid hover hit-test allocated a String on every mouse move** — `UIMultiAnimGrid.cells` was keyed by a `'${col}_${row}'` String built by `cellKey`, which runs on the per-move hover path (`onMouseMove → cellAtPointInto → hitTestRectInto`/`hitTestHexInto → cells.exists(cellKey(...))`). So a fresh String was allocated (and String-hashed) on every move even when the cursor stayed in the same cell — invisible to the `CellCoord` allocation watchdog, which only counts `CellCoord`. Fix: `cellKey(col, row)` now returns an `Int` packed as `((col & 0xFFFF) << 16) | (row & 0xFFFF)` (collision-free for col/row in `[-32768, 32767]`, covering negative hex axial coords), and `cells` + the `layerEntries` inner map are now `Int`-keyed. Removes the per-move String allocation and cheapens all ~35 cell lookups (Int hashing). New `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`-gated `UIMultiAnimGrid.cellKeyStringAllocCount` watchdog pins the gap shut. Three regression tests in `UIMultiAnimGridTest`: `testCellHoverHitTestAllocatesNoCellKeyStringPerMove` (rect, 0 cell-key Strings across 20 same-cell moves), `testHexCellHoverHitTestAllocatesNoCellKeyStringPerMove` (hex), and `testCellKeysAreCollisionFreeIncludingNegativeCoords` (packing stays collision-free for negative/swapped coords).
- **Root-level / `apply { }` `tint:` on a non-Drawable target was a silent no-op** — `tint:` maps to `h2d.Drawable.color`, but the programmable root is an `h2d.Layers` (non-Drawable) and so are container elements (`flow`/`layers`/`mask`). A root-level `tint:` on a `programmable()`, or an `apply { tint: }` whose parent is a container, simply did nothing — no error, no visual — hiding the authoring mistake. Fix: `MultiAnimBuilder.applyExtendedFormProperties` and the five codegen tint emitters (ctor + apply + revert, both the child-element and `apply`-entry paths) now throw a `BuilderError` with `code="tint_requires_drawable"` ("tint requires a Drawable target (bitmap/text/...); cannot apply to <class>") instead of silently dropping the tint, matching across builder and codegen. **Migration:** move any root-level/container `tint:` onto a Drawable child (`bitmap`/`text`/…) or `apply { tint: }` onto one. New fixture `test/examples/123-codegenRootTint/` + `CodegenRootTintTest` (codegen `create()` throws a `BuilderError`); two `BuilderUnitTest` cases — `testRootTintOnProgrammableThrows` (builder throws) and `testTintOnDrawableElementStillBuilds` (regression guard: `@tint` on a `bitmap` still builds).
- **Conditional `apply { blendMode: ... }` / `apply { tint: ... }` did not revert on un-match in the builder** — `IncrementalUpdateContext`'s apply baseline captured `filter`/`alpha`/`scale`/`rotation`/`pos` but not `color` or `blendMode`, so a `@(cond) apply { blendMode: add }` (or `{ tint: ... }`) stayed stuck on its applied value after the condition flipped false — the builder diverged from codegen, which saves and reverts both. Fix: `captureApplyBaseline` now snapshots `color` (when the parent is an `h2d.Drawable`) and `blendMode`; `reconcileApplyParent` restores both during the reset pass (tint only on Drawable parents, matching `applyExtendedFormProperties`' guard). One regression test in `GridCellHoverRegressionTest` (`testApplyBlendModeRevertsWhenConditionFlipsFalse`): a `@(status=>hover) apply { blendMode: add }` cell toggles hover twice, asserting `blendMode` round-trips Alpha→Add→Alpha across both cycles.
- **Codegen overlapping `apply` entries on the same parent clobbered each other** — codegen emitted each conditional `apply` as an independent `if (cond) <apply> else <revert>`, so two `@(cond) apply { ... }` blocks targeting the same parent fought: a non-matching later entry's else-revert wiped an earlier matching entry's effect. Fix: codegen now mirrors the builder's `reconcileApplyParent` reset-and-replay — pass 1 runs every entry's revert unconditionally (resetting touched properties to the constructor-captured baseline), pass 2 replays only the matched entries in declaration order (`if (cond) <apply>`, no else). New fixture `test/examples/122-codegenOverlappingApply/` + `CodegenOverlappingApplyTest` with builder AND codegen tests asserting two overlapping `apply { alpha: }` compose correctly (first wins when only it matches; last-in-declaration-order wins when both match; baseline restored when neither matches).
- **Enum-typed `dynamicRef` parameter forwarding threw / failed to update the child** — forwarding a parent *enum* param into a child enum param (`dynamicRef($inner, mode=>$parentMode)`) routed the forwarded value through `resolveAsInteger` (builder) / `rvToExpr` emitting the enum field's raw Int index (codegen). Enum-valued references are stored as `Index(...)` and the child's `setParameter` enum path accepts the value's NAME, not the index — so the builder threw "is not a value but Index(...)" and codegen's forwarded int was rejected by `dynamicValueToIndex`. Fix: both paths now forward enum params by NAME — builder adds a `PPTEnum(_)` case routing through `resolveAsString`; codegen adds `dynamicRefForwardValueExpr` which emits the enum name string for enum-typed parent refs (every other value type keeps its raw representation to avoid a string round-trip on the forwarding hot path). New fixture `test/examples/122-codegenDynamicRefEnumForwarding/` and tests: `DynamicRefTest.testDynamicRefForwardingEnumParamUpdatesChildOnSetParameter` (builder), `CodegenDynamicRefForwardingTest.testEnumForwarding_Builder_ForwardsParentEnumToChild` + `…_Codegen_…` — each asserts the child re-renders to the parent's enum value after `setParameter` on the parent.
- **`UICardHandHelper` allocated a `TargetingResult` every frame while dragging over a target** — `updateDrag`'s per-frame `canPlayCard` feedback check rebuilt `TargetZone(currentTargetId)` each call; `TargetZone` carries a String arg, so a fresh enum was allocated every frame even when the hovered target id was unchanged. Fix: cache the `TargetingResult` (`cachedTargetResult` + `cachedTargetResultId`) and reuse it while `currentTargetId` is stable (`NoTarget` is a parameterless singleton, no allocation). Regression test in `CardHandOrchestratorTest` (`testDragReusesTargetingResultAcrossFramesForSameTarget`): three `updateDrag` calls with an unchanged target id must hand `canPlayCard` the same instance (HL reference identity), not three allocations.
- **`ParticleGroup.matchesTrigger` allocated a 2-element array per sub-emitter event check** — the `switch [configured, actual]` form built a temporary array on this sub-emitter-event hot path just to compare two enum constructors (`OnInterval` ignored its payload). Replaced with `Type.enumIndex(configured) == Type.enumIndex(actual)` — constructor-identity comparison with no allocation, same matching semantics.
- **Mid-life particle anim override (`onBounce`) froze long-lived particles on the impact frame forever (refines "Particle `applyAnimEventOverride` was clobbered by the next-tick natural-advance loop" below)** — the prior fix added a `Particle.animOverrideActive` pin that gates the natural-advance loop so an override survives until the particle re-initialises. That is correct for `onDeath` (the particle recycles/frees right after) and for a deliberate late-life override into an earlier state (the flicker-fix scenario), but wrong for a mid-life trigger like `onBounce → impact`: the pin held for the rest of the particle's life, so a long-lived bouncing particle stayed stuck on the impact state and never advanced to its dying state (`localT` clamps to 1.0). Fix: the pin is now classified at fire time as either *releasable* (play-through) or *sticky*. New `Particle.animOverrideReleasable` flag, set by `applyAnimEventOverride`: true when the override target's window is still current/ahead at fire time (`life/maxLife < overrideEnd`), false when it targets a state whose window is already in the past (a deliberate late-life override that must persist). A releasable pin is one-shot — `Particle.update()` clears `animOverrideActive` once lifetime progression passes the END of the pinned state's window, so the impact anim plays through once and then resumes lifetime-driven advance; a sticky pin holds until reinit (original flicker-fix behaviour). The flicker fix (override not clobbered on the very next tick) is preserved either way — the pin holds at least until the window ends. `onDeath` needs no release (the particle recycles/frees). `animOverrideReleasable` is reset in `ParticleGroup.init`. One new regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testOnBounceAnimOverrideResumesLifetimeAdvanceAfterImpactWindow`): a non-looping particle with fly/impact/dying states, injected `onBounce` override mid-fly, asserts the impact tile holds across the impact window and then advances to the dying tile once life passes the dying state's `startLifeRate`. The pre-existing `testAnimEventOverridePersistsAcrossNextUpdate` (late-life override into state 0) still passes — it now exercises the sticky path.
- **Codegen baked `0xFF000000` opaque alpha onto child `tint:` and `pixels` colors, diverging from the builder and from strict-D (refines "BREAKING: Strict-D color semantics" above)** — the strict-D migration deleted the `if (c >>> 24 == 0) c |= 0xFF000000` opaque-alpha bake from the runtime builder and from codegen's root-tint / `SolidColor` / `Cross` emitters, but five sites in `ProgrammableCodeGen` still carried it: child-element `tint:` updates (ctor + apply + revert paths) and the `pixels` line/rect/pixel emitters (both the direct `_rt_pl` path and the bounds-computation path). So a child `tint: 0xFF0000` or `pixels(filledRect ..., 0xFF0000)` — transparent red under strict-D — rendered as opaque red in codegen while the builder (and codegen's own root tint) correctly stored it verbatim with alpha 0. Fix: removed the five remaining bakes; codegen now stores every Int color verbatim, matching the builder and the "Int is final" strict-D rule. New fixture `test/examples/121-strictDColorVerbatim/` + `StrictDColorVerbatimTest` with two tests asserting builder AND codegen both store `0x00FF0000` (transparent red) verbatim — `testTintWithZeroAlphaIsStoredVerbatimInBuilderAndCodegen` (child `tint:`) and `testPixelWithZeroAlphaIsStoredVerbatimInBuilderAndCodegen` (`pixels`).
- **Codegen `$param` references inside `particles` / `tileGroup` blocks resolved against an empty/default scope instead of the instance's parameters** — `@:manim` codegen built particle systems via `ProgrammableBuilder.buildParticles(name, index)` and tile groups via `buildTileGroupFromProgrammable(name, index)` with no parameter map, so a `count: $particleCount` inside a `particles {}` block or a `bitmap(...): $tileX, 0` inside a `tileGroup {}` block resolved `$particleCount` / `$tileX` against the parameter *defaults* (or an empty scope), never the values passed to `create(...)`. The runtime builder path resolved these correctly; only codegen diverged. Fix: both `ProgrammableBuilder` methods now take an optional `params:Map<String, Dynamic>`; `buildParticles` routes through a new `MultiAnimBuilder.buildParticleWithParams` that pushes the resolved instance params into scope (via `resolveProgrammableParamsToScope`, which fills unsupplied params from their defaults) before `createParticleImpl`, and `buildTileGroupFromProgrammable` forwards the params to `buildWithParameters`. Codegen emits a `Map` literal populated from the instance's `_<param>` fields at each particles/tileGroup create site. New fixture `test/examples/120-codegenParamScope/` + two visual-runner tests in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` — `testCodegenParticlesResolveParamScope` (asserts `group.nparts == 99` from `create(99)`, not the default 11) and `testCodegenTileGroupResolveParamScope` (asserts the baked bitmap offset reflects `create(250)`, not the default 7).
- **Root-level extended-form properties (`scale:` / `alpha:` / `rotate:` / `tint:` / `filter:`) on `programmable()` did not re-fire on `setParameter` in the builder** — `MultiAnimBuilder.startBuild` applied `applyExtendedFormProperties(root, rootNode)` once at build time but, unlike child elements (`trackIncrementalExpressions`), never registered a tracked expression for the root, so a `$param`-driven `scale: $s` / `alpha: $a` at the programmable body root stayed frozen at its build-time value across `setParameter`. Codegen had already been fixed to honour these (see the strict-D entry above); this brings the runtime builder in line. Fix: extracted the ext-form tracking into a shared `trackExtendedFormExpressions(node, object, gateVisibility)` helper (used by both `trackIncrementalExpressions` and the root path). The programmable and non-programmable root branches call it with `gateVisibility=false` — the root has no parent, so the normal `isEffectivelyVisible` gate (which returns false for a parentless object) would otherwise skip it, and re-applying scale/alpha to the root is a harmless field write that should always reflect current params. Root **position** is intentionally NOT tracked (it composes additively with runtime `setPosition`, so re-applying would clobber a caller's offset). One regression test in `ApplyParamUpdateTest` (`testRootLevelExtendedProps_RefireOnSetParameter_Builder`): a programmable with root `scale: $s` / `alpha: $a`, asserts `setParameter("s", 2.0)` / `setParameter("a", 0.25)` update `root.scaleX` / `root.alpha`.
- **Constant-count `repeatable` with a loop-var-only conditional hid surviving iterations in incremental mode** — a `repeatable($i, step(3, ...)) { @($i >= 1) bitmap(...) }` in an incremental build has no settable-param dependency (the loop count is constant), so it took the full-build repeat path while `incrementalMode` stayed true. Each per-iteration conditional was then registered as an incremental conditional entry — but all N entries collapse onto the single parse-time template `uniqueNodeName`, and the later `applyConditionalChains` re-evaluated the condition with the loop var (`$i`) no longer in scope, hiding iterations that had legitimately been built. Fix: new `suppressConditionalTracking` flag on `MultiAnimBuilder`, set while iterating a constant-count repeatable body in incremental mode. While set, loop-var conditionals are resolved at build time (full-mode chain filtering) and NOT registered as incremental entries; expression tracking stays active. Gates the four `incrementalMode` conditional-tracking sites (`resolveConditionalChildren`, the deferred-build sentinel, the not-visible early return, and the conditional sentinel insertion). One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testRepeatableVarConditionalIncrementalSurvivesUnrelatedSetParameter`): builds a 3-iteration repeatable with `@($i >= 1)`, asserts 2 visible bitmaps, fires an unrelated `setParameter`, asserts the count stays 2.
- **Batched multi-param `setParameter` fired tracked expressions grouped-by-param instead of in declaration order (refines "applyUpdates now uses a reverse index keyed by paramName" above)** — the reverse index groups trackeds by the param they reference, so firing them inline during the per-param walk ran them grouped-by-param for a batched multi-param update (`beginUpdate` + several `setParameter` + `endUpdate`). Two tracked expressions that write overlapping properties of the same object compose by firing order (last write wins), so a stable build/declaration order is the contract — grouped-by-param firing silently reordered them whenever they referenced different params. Fix: new `declOrder:Int` field on `TrackedExpression`, assigned from a monotonic `trackedSeq` in `trackExpression()` (never reset across the context's life, so `cleanupDestroyedSubtree` splices preserve survivors' relative order). `applyUpdates` now collects the relevant trackeds into a reusable `trackedFireScratch` (gen-deduped), sorts by `declOrder` when more than one was collected (single-param hot path skips the sort), then fires them — restoring stable declaration-order composition. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testBatchedMultiParamUpdateFiresTrackedExpressionsInDeclarationOrder`): three recorder trackeds declared interleaved across two params (pb, pa, pb), batched update touches both, asserts firing order is the declaration order `1,2,3`.
- **`UIScreenBase.clear()` left pushed interaction controllers on the stack instead of restoring the default controller** — `clear()` walked `controllersStack` and called `clearState()` on every entry, then `removeChildren()` on the scene root. The walk did NOT pop the stack, so any `UIInteractionController` pushed via `pushController(ctrl)` (used by `UISelectFromHandController.start`, `UIPickTargetController.start`, etc.) survived the clear — but the scene graph it referenced was gone. The next `get_controller()` call returned the dead controller, future input events routed into a controller whose interactive references had been destroyed, and the default-controller invariant ("the bottom-most entry on the stack is `UIDefaultController`") was silently violated. The deactivate hooks (`onDeactivate`) were never fired either, so controllers that registered listeners on game state didn't get a chance to unsubscribe. Fix: `clear()` now pops every controller above the bottom-most one via `controllersStack.pop()` and fires `LifecycleControllerFinished` on each so deactivate hooks (e.g. `UIInteractionController.onDeactivate`) run; the surviving bottom controller (`UIDefaultController`) gets its previous `clearState()` call. Result: the default-controller invariant holds across `clear()`, deactivate hooks run, and dead interaction controllers no longer leak across screen transitions. Three regression tests in `InteractionControllerTest`: `testClearRestoresDefaultControllerOnTopOfStack` (single pushed controller), `testClearFiresLifecycleFinishedOnPushedControllers` (via a `TestableInteractionController` instrumenting activate/deactivate flags), `testClearWithNestedControllersRestoresDefault` (two stacked controllers).
- **Particle `applyAnimEventOverride` was clobbered by the next-tick natural-advance loop** — `Particle.update()` walks the `animStates` array forward whenever `timeNormalized >= animStates[i+1].startLifeRate`, monotonically advancing through lifetime-driven animation states. `applyAnimEventOverride(p, name)` (used to switch tile sets on `onBounce`, `onDeath`, and game-emitted custom events) set `currentAnimStateIndex` directly to the override target and swapped the tile immediately. Whenever the override target was *earlier* in the array than the natural-by-time index would be (typical: an "impact" anim fired mid-lifetime points at state 1 while time-driven advance has already walked to state 2), the very next tick's `while (stateIdx + 1 < animStates.length && timeNormalized >= animStates[stateIdx + 1].startLifeRate) stateIdx++;` walked from the override target back up past it, clobbering the override for every subsequent frame — the visual impact / bounce / death anim flickered for one frame at most. Fix: new `Particle.animOverrideActive:Bool` flag, set by `applyAnimEventOverride` and cleared by `ParticleGroup.init(p)` (so a recycled or freshly-spawned particle starts unpinned). `Particle.update()`'s natural-advance loop is gated `if (!animOverrideActive)`, so an override survives until the particle re-initialises. Two regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testAnimEventOverridePersistsAcrossNextUpdate` (override targets state 0 from natural-state-2 mid-late lifetime, asserts the index stays at 0 across the next `update`); `testOnDeathAnimOverrideSwitchesParticleTileAtLifecycleDeath` (verifies the OnDeath sub-emitter trigger now also fires `applyAnimEventOverride("onDeath")` so death anims actually swap their tile at lifecycle expiry — the OnDeath site in `Particle.update`'s death branch was extended with a matching `group.applyAnimEventOverride(this, "onDeath")` next to the existing `group.triggerSubEmitters(this, OnDeath)`).
- **Particle filter-rejected deferred-free tick ran a full physics step on an already-dead particle** — `init()` rejection marks the particle as dead in place (`life = maxLife + 1`, `rejected = true`, `visible = false`) but the particle stays in the `h2d.SpriteBatch` until the next `update()` reaps it. The reap path went through the bottom-of-`Particle.update` lifecycle branch — which the prior `Particle emitFilter sub-emitter cascade` fix had already taught to skip `triggerSubEmitters(OnDeath)`. But the *physics* block (gravity, force fields, velocity integration, position step, life increment, rotation, size, alpha, color, sprite anim, bounds) still ran first on every dead-on-arrival particle, since the rejection guard was only checked at the death branch at the very end. A heavy `emitFilter` that discards 80%+ of spawns (point-of-impact reject zones, region masks) paid the full physics cost for every rejected particle for one tick. Fix: new short-circuit at the top of `Particle.update()` — if `rejected`, run the same `emitLoop` recycle / non-loop free logic as the lifecycle branch and return immediately, skipping the entire physics block. The `emitBurstAt` post-batch comment was rewritten to document that the next-update rejection short-circuit (not the lifecycle branch) performs the matching `liveCount--`. One regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testRejectedParticleSkipsPhysicsTickBeforeFree`) — emit one rejected particle with gravity=1000, asserts `life`, `vy`, and `y` are all unchanged across the deferred-free tick.
- **`ParticleGroup.emitBurstAt` applied position/velocity offsets to filter-rejected particles** — the `for (i in 0...count)` loop unconditionally wrote `p.x += atX; p.y += atY; p.vx += inheritVx; p.vy += inheritVy` after calling `init(p)`, even when `init()` had marked the particle dead via `emitFilter`. The new rejection short-circuit at the top of `Particle.update()` (entry above) observes the particle's position/velocity to detect the rejection state, and the offset write would have moved the dead particle to the burst location — clobbering the pre-rejection zero-init that the short-circuit relies on observing. Fix: move the four `p.* +=` writes plus `p.visible = true` and `triggerSubEmitters(p, OnBirth)` inside the `if (accepted)` branch. The `liveCount++` and `batch.add(p)` stay outside (rejected particles still need a batch slot for the next-update reap) — long-standing comment on `liveCount++` is updated to point at the new short-circuit instead of the death-branch decrement. One regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testEmitBurstAtDoesNotApplyOffsetToRejectedParticle`) — calls `emitBurstAt(500, 700, 50, 80, 1)` with `emitFilter = (_, _) -> false`, asserts the rejected particle's `x`, `y`, `vx`, `vy` are all 0.
- **`Particles` container self-removed on the first `sync()` tick before any group had emitted** — `Particles.sync()` initialises `isDone = true` and only flips it to false when at least one group has `batch.first != null`. That conflated two unrelated states — "system has never emitted yet" and "system has finished emitting" — into the same signal. The default `onEnd()` body calls `this.remove()`, so the natural construction sequence `new Particles(parent); configure(); addGroup(g);` lost the container if the scene ticked between construction and `addGroup`.
