Same trap for the worldAnchor trail-emitter pattern: a burst-only group (`nparts == 0`, `emitLoop = false`) waiting for an external `emitBurstAt()` trigger never populates its batch via `start()`, so `batch.first` stays null on the first sync and the container is removed before the caller can ever fire a burst. Fix: new `hasEmittedAny:Bool` field latched true once any group's batch has held at least one live particle; `onEnd()` now only fires when `isDone && hasEmittedAny`, so a freshly-created or burst-pending container is no longer auto-removed on the first sync. Two regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testEmptyParticlesContainerDoesNotFireOnEndOnFirstSync` (constructs a `Particles` with no groups, sets a counting `onEnd`, syncs once, asserts zero fires); `testParticlesWithBurstOnlyGroupDoesNotFireOnEndBeforeFirstBurst` (one burst-only group with `nparts = 0`, syncs once, asserts zero fires).
- **`UIMultiAnimGrid` swap-on-drop path threw NRE when an external draggable had no source cell** — `acceptDrops(draggable)` registers any `UIMultiAnimDraggable` as a drop target across the grid's cells. When `swapEnabled = true` and the target cell is occupied, the drop handler routed into `handleSwapDrop(binding, coord, srcGrid, srcCell)` — but the swap path dereferences `srcCell` (in animation start-position math and in the `CellSwap` event ctx). For draggables built via `makeDraggableFromCell(col, row)` the source cell is auto-populated, but external draggables (created directly via `UIMultiAnimDraggable.create(obj)` for off-grid sources — an inventory list panel, a card hand, an external pickup) have `sourceCellCoord = null` by design. Dropping one of those on a swap-enabled grid's occupied cell threw a null-reference exception instead of falling through to the normal `CellDrop` event. Fix: the swap-guard in the `onDragDrop` handler was tightened to `if (swapEnabled && srcCell != null && (swapAccepts != null ? swapAccepts(coord, binding.draggable) : isOccupied(coord.col, coord.row)))`, so a null `srcCell` falls through to the standard `CellDrop` path. One regression test in `UIMultiAnimGridTest` (`testAcceptDropsFallsBackToCellDropWhenSwapHasNoSourceCell`) — registers an external draggable with no `sourceCellCoord` on a swap-enabled grid's occupied cell, invokes the `onDragDrop` callback wired by `acceptDrops`, asserts no throw, `CellDrop` fires and `CellSwap` does not.
- **`UICardHandHelper` default `interactivePrefix` collided across helpers sharing one screen** — `interactivePrefix` defaulted to the fixed string `"card"` and `nextCardSeq` was a per-helper field starting at 0, so two card hands on one screen both produced ids `"card_0"`, `"card_1"`, … Every card's screen-level wrapper id then collided in `UIScreen.interactiveMap` (silent `Map.set` overwrite at the registration site), so the second helper's cards won the entry and the first helper's interactives stopped receiving routed events / autoStatus updates entirely. Fix: new `static var helperInstanceCounter:Int` on `UICardHandHelper`, mirroring the `UIMultiAnimGrid.cardTargetCounter` pattern; each helper captures `helperInstanceCounter++` into a `final helperInstanceId:Int` in the constructor, and the default `interactivePrefix` is built as `'card${helperInstanceId}'` instead of the fixed string. Explicit user-supplied `interactivePrefix` is still honoured verbatim (collisions there are the caller's contract). One regression test in `CardHandIntegrationTest` (`testTwoDefaultHelpersOnSameScreenProduceDistinctInteractiveIds`) builds two helpers on one `UITestScreen` with default config, deals one card to each, asserts the two helpers' `interactiveId`s differ AND that the screen returns distinct wrappers for `'<id1>.card'` vs `'<id2>.card'` — the latter would catch any regression where the prefix changes but the wrapper map still overwrites.
- **`reconcileApplyParent` clobbered externally-set parent `x`/`y` on every `@(cond) apply { filter: ... }` flip (refines "apply: unconditional $param tracking + interleaved same-property fix" 2b26e5f)** — the rc.5 commit replaced per-entry apply save/restore with per-parent baseline-replay (`captureApplyBaseline` + `reconcileApplyParent`) so overlapping `@(c1) apply { alpha: 0.5 }` / `@(c2) apply { alpha: 0.8 }` on the same parent compose correctly. The baseline was captured at build time and held the parent's `filter, alpha, scaleX, scaleY, rotation, x, y`. On every match-state flip, reconcile unconditionally reset ALL of those properties to baseline, then replayed currently-matched entries. This broke any external code that mutates the parent's position after build: `UIMultiAnimGrid` builds a cell at the origin then calls `cell.object.setPosition(hexX, hexY)`, and the cell's `.manim` has `@(status=>hover) apply { filter: glow(...) }`. Every mouse hover/leave fired reconcile → reset `parent.x, parent.y` to the build-time baseline (`0, 0`) → the grid cell snapped back to the grid origin, stacking all hovered/unhovered cells on top of each other. The playground symptom is "previously-hovered empty cells disappear" — they didn't disappear, they all overlapped at (0,0). Fix: property-scoped reset. Pass 1 walks `conditionalApplyEntries` for this parent and tags which properties any entry on this parent touches (`anyTouchesFilter`/`Alpha`/`Scale`/`Rotation`/`Pos`); Pass 2 resets ONLY those properties to baseline; Pass 3 replays matched entries unchanged. `node.pos` is a `Coordinates` enum (never null) — `ZERO` is treated as "does not touch position" so an `apply { filter: ... }` with no explicit `pos:` correctly opts out of the position reset. The matching `applyExtendedFormProperties` replay site also gates `addPosition()` on `hasPos` so a `pos:`-less apply does not re-add (0,0) on every replay. Matches the property-scoped approach in commit 8762218 (transition cancel baseline). Tint baseline is intentionally not restored (consistent with pre-fix behavior — `applyExtendedFormProperties` only writes tint when `node.tint != null`, and tint baselines on `h2d.Drawable.color` were never restored). 14 regression tests in new `GridCellHoverRegressionTest` covering: minimal hex/rect-cell hover→leave cycles (×2 to surface incremental-cache regressions), external-position preservation across apply toggle, two-cell sweep (mouse moves from A to B), 10-cell sequential sweep, real `grid-demo.manim` `hexCell`/`rectCell` sweeps with `applyCellTargetFeedback`-shape begin/setStatus/setHighlight/end batches, and full `UIMultiAnimGrid` widget mouse-sweep tests asserting `countVisibleDescendants` and `totalGraphicsArea` (sum of `h2d.Graphics` bounds) stay unchanged across the sweep — the latter would catch any regression where `h2d.Graphics` draw commands cleared without re-fire. Multi-grid test exercises four grids sharing one `MultiAnimBuilder` (rect/hex/storage/loadout), mirroring the real playground `GridDemoScreen`.

## [1.0.0-rc.5] - 2026-05-15

*Note: 1.0.0-rc.4 was published to haxelib without its own CHANGELOG section — its entries are folded into this one.*

### Breaking
- **`.manim` expression precedence: comparison operators now bind below additive (C-like)** — the parser was rewritten to a Pratt loop with explicit precedence (`*` `/` `%` `div` at lbp 30, `+` `-` at lbp 20, `==` `!=` `<` `>` `<=` `>=` at lbp 10, all left-associative). Any `.manim` expression that mixed arithmetic and comparison left-of-operator (`$a + $b < $c`, `$a + $b == $c + $d`) previously parsed as `$a + ($b < $c)` and now parses as `($a + $b) < $c`. Right-of-comparison shapes (`$a < $b + $c`) parsed correctly under the old code and still do. **Migration:** grep your `.manim` files for arithmetic-then-comparison expressions and add explicit parens around the intended grouping if you were relying on the old binding. See the detailed entry under `### Changed` below.

### Added
- **`BuilderResolvedSettings.getColorOrException(key)` / `getColorOrDefault(key, default)`** — the Settings API previously had no dedicated color accessor: callers reading a `:color` setting (`overlay.color`, `overlayColor:color => $overlayColor`, etc.) routed through `getIntOrDefault` and got the raw `RSVColor` integer back via a silent fallthrough arm. That fallthrough also leaked the inverse direction — `getIntOrDefault("alpha", 0)` against a `:color` setting silently returned the color int, masking type mismatches at the call site (the actual symptom on the modal-overlay path: a typo'd `overlay.color` value of type `:int` sneaking through). New `getColorOrException` / `getColorOrDefault` accept `RSVColor` (the raw `0xAARRGGBB` Heaps int) and `RSVInt` (so users who write the literal as `:int` or `=> 0xFF000000` are not punished); reject `RSVFloat` / `RSVString` / `RSVBool`. Symmetric to the existing `AnimMetadata.getColorOrException` / `getColorOrDefault` shape. The `getIntOrException` / `getIntOrDefault` / `getFloatOrException` / `getFloatOrDefault` color-fallthrough arms now throw with an actionable `'use getColorOrException for color settings'` message. `UIScreenBase.parseOverlaySettings` migrated to `getColorOrDefault`; no other in-tree consumers were affected. Seven regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testColorSettingRejectedByGetIntOrException`, `testColorSettingRejectedByGetIntOrDefault`, `testColorSettingRejectedByGetFloatOrException`, `testColorSettingRejectedByGetFloatOrDefault`, `testGetColorOrDefaultAcceptsColorAndInt`, `testGetColorOrExceptionAcceptsColorAndInt`, `testGetColorOrDefaultRejectsStringAndFloat`).
- **`pixelOutline(...)` trailing `smoothColor` keyword now documented and parser-tested** — the parser, builder, and codegen all already supported the trailing `, smoothColor` flag (matching the existing `dropShadow` / `glow` conventions) but the docs only listed the positional signature without it, so the feature was effectively undiscoverable. `docs/manim.md` and `docs/manim-reference.md` updated to spell out `pixelOutline(knockout, color, knockoutStrength [, smoothColor])` / `pixelOutline(inlineColor, outlineColor, fillColor [, smoothColor])` and clarify the third positional argument's role on the `inlineColor` mode (inner fill color drawn on top of the source). Three regression tests in `ParserErrorTest` (`testPixelOutlineKnockoutWithoutSmoothColor`, `testPixelOutlineKnockoutWithSmoothColor`, `testPixelOutlineInlineColorWithSmoothColor`) walk the AST via a recursive `findFirstPixelOutlineFilter` helper and assert the `smoothColor:Bool` defaults to `false` and flips to `true` only when the keyword is present.
- **`Particles.worldAnchor:Null<h2d.Object>` for world-space trail emission** — `relative: false` particle groups previously had only one bake target: the full absolute scene-space transform of their `Particles` container. Trails parented to a moving sprite (missile exhaust, plasma trail, railgun streak) emitted into screen pixels and visibly slid against the world whenever the camera panned or zoomed — and the workarounds were ugly: either keep one shared emitter at world origin and call `emitBurstAt(currentWorldX, currentWorldY)` every frame (loses the per-shot lifetime needed for `shutdown()`/fade/GC), or parent the emitter to the moving sprite and accept the slide bug. New runtime-only field on `bh.base.Particles`. When set (`particles.worldAnchor = worldRoot`), `ParticleGroup.init`'s `!isRelative` branch bakes emit position/velocity/scale/rotation through the relative transform `R = worldAnchor⁻¹ ∘ parts.abs` — parts' absolute transform expressed in worldAnchor's local frame — and `Particles.draw`'s `!isRelative` branch renders through `worldAnchor`'s absolute transform. Together those keep the trail glued to the world points where it was emitted, while still letting the emitter container live as a child of the moving sprite. Null default preserves legacy screen-space baking and identity draw (back-compat). No DSL surface — the anchor is a live `h2d.Object` reference, configured from runtime code (`trailEmitter.worldAnchor = worldRoot`). Three regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testWorldAnchorBakesParticlesIntoAnchorLocalFrame` (anchor's translation is removed from the bake — emit at parts-local `(0,0)` with worldRoot at `(50,60)` and parts at `(10,20)` produces baked `(10,20)`, not `(60,80)`), `testWorldAnchorTrailStaysAnchoredAcrossAnchorTranslation` (two emits separated by an anchor pan from `(0,0)` to `(500,600)` land at the same anchor-local coords), `testNullWorldAnchorPreservesScreenSpaceBaking` (regression guard — explicit-null path still produces full scene-space bake).
- **`BuilderResult.hasSlot(name, ?index, ?indexY):Bool` and `BuilderResult.hasDynamicRef(name):Bool`** — companions to the throwing `getSlot` / `getDynamicRef`. Lets callers query for a slot or dynamicRef without wrapping the get in try/catch. Primary use case: indexed slot iteration count shrinking under `setParameter` (a `repeatable($i, step($count, …)) { #button[$i] slot { … } }` with `count` dropped from 5 to 3 leaves no `Indexed("button", 4)` entry — `hasSlot("button", 4)` reports false instead of throwing). Secondary: any code path that conditionally fetches a `dynamicRef` whose presence depends on a `@switch` arm or visibility flip. `hasDynamicRef` reports presence in the dynamicRefs map only — `getDynamicRef` may still throw at the call site if multiple unnamed sites collide on the same key. Two unit tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testHasSlotReportsExistenceWithoutThrowing` (covers Named, Indexed, missing-name, missing-index, no-slots-block, and the iteration-shrinkage case) and `testHasDynamicRefReportsExistenceWithoutThrowing`.
- **Allocation watchdog counters extended to `Hex`, `FractionalHex`, `TweenPropertyEntry`, and `AnimatedPathState`** — prior coverage only spanned `FPoint`, `CardLayoutPosition`, and `UICardHandLayout.scratchArrayAllocationCount` (all card-hand related). Four new counters fill in the rest of the per-frame hot surface: `Hex.creationCount` and `FractionalHex.creationCount` (every `pixelToHex` + `.round()` round-trip on hex grids and every `Hex.add`/`subtract`/`scale`/`rotate*` allocates a fresh instance — both currently pinned at known counts in tests, awaiting `*Into` variants); `TweenPropertyEntry.creationCount` (1–7 entries per `Tween` construction; tweens are created continuously by UI fades, card moves, transitions — counter pinned at current churn so a future pool can flip the assertion); `AnimatedPathState.creationCount` (should stay at 0 per `update`/`seek` — `currentState` is mutated in place — pinned at 0). All counters gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK` so the increments and field declarations vanish from production builds, same as the existing three. `TweenPropertyEntry` was promoted from `private class` to module-public so tests can read the static counter (instance fields remain private to the Tween implementation). `AnimatedPathState` gained an explicit `new(...)` constructor (the synthetic `@:structInit` ctor cannot run extra code) — the existing `{position: ..., angle: ...}` literal call site is unchanged. Documentation in `.claude/rules/testing-and-debugging.md` covers the counter table, the convention, the test pattern, and gotchas (warm-up, `@:structInit` interaction, accessor visibility).
- **End-to-end allocation smoke test (`AllocationSmokeTest.hx`)** — a single integration test that runs a representative scene (hex grid + 5-card hand + tween manager with two long-running tweens + animated path) for 60 ticks and asserts a total per-counter budget across the whole run. Catches per-frame regressions that slip past subsystem-specific watchdog tests because the subsystem is exercised outside its dedicated test path. Per-counter expectations are commented inline alongside the asserts so the rationale is visible at the failure site.
- **Particle step allocation regression test (`ParticleRuntimeTest.testParticleStepDoesNotAllocateFPoint`)** — pins the contract that `Particle.update` + `applyForceFields` is FPoint-allocation-free even with three force fields active (Wind, Vortex, Attractor). Force fields are stored as enum cases (immutable, allocated once at config time) and PathGuide already uses `_scratch:FPoint`; the test makes those guarantees executable.

- **DevBridge custom game ops — register queries/commands, emit typed events** — three new MCP RPCs plus a game-side registration API let a game expose domain-specific debug affordances without editing `DevBridge.hx`. `screenManager.devBridge.registerQuery(op, description, params, handler)` exposes a read-only handler; `registerCommand(op, description, params, handler)` exposes a mutating handler; `registerEvent(name, description, payload)` declares a game-emitted event type for discovery. Handlers run with a `Dynamic` params object and return any JSON-serializable value; throwing `haxe.Exception` surfaces as an MCP `internal` error with the thrown message preserved. RPCs: `list_game_ops` returns `{queries, commands, events}` metadata (for MCP client discovery), `game_op` invokes a registered handler by name (auto-detects query vs command, returns `{kind, op, result}`, throws `not_found` for unknown ops and `invalid_params` when `op` is missing), `get_game_events` polls the event ring buffer (params: `clear`, `limit` ≤ 200, `since_id` cursor, `types[]` filter; returns `{events, total, dropped, lastId}`). `screenManager.devBridge.emitEvent(name, data)` pushes into the ring buffer and broadcasts as SSE `game_event` — safe to call without prior `registerEvent` (prints a DEV-mode warning if the name is unknown). Duplicate op names (query↔command collision or same-kind duplicate) throw at registration time. Only active with `-D MULTIANIM_DEV`. 15 regression tests in `DevBridgeTest` covering empty registry, registration + dispatch (query/command), collision rejection, handler exception wrapping, unknown-op `not_found`, missing-`op` `invalid_params`, emit + poll, unregistered event warning path, `since_id` cursor, `types[]` filter, `limit`, `clear`, and ring-buffer drop behavior.
- **`LifecycleMode` test hook for `simpleMacroTest`** — new optional last-arg on `VisualTestBase.simpleMacroTest` / `builderAndMacroScreenshotAndCompare` (`bh.test.LifecycleMode`) that lets a visual test mutate the built root between scene-add and screenshot. Variants: `None` (default, no-op), `DetachReattach` (`root.remove()` + `addChildAt(root, originalIdx)` — z-order preserving so title overlays stay on top), `Custom(fn:(root:h2d.Object) -> Void)` (escape hatch). Applied symmetrically to builder AND macro phases so a reference image captures post-lifecycle state and either-side regressions surface as a similarity mismatch. Use to catch bugs that pure render tests miss because they don't exercise `onRemove`/`onAdd` chains — filter rebind, vanilla `h2d.Graphics` vertex wipe, tilegroup re-bake, particle shutdown/resume. Consumers: `test113_CodegenGraphicsReattach` (first), `test14_TileGroupDemo` (tileGroup re-bake), `test68_FiltersAdvanced` (filter rebind).
- **`ColorUtils` RGB/RGBA helpers** — `ColorUtils.rgb(0x55AA88)` returns `0xFF55AA88` (opaque from bare RGB); `ColorUtils.rgba(0x55AA88, 0.5)` returns `0x8055AA88` (RGB with 0..1 alpha, clamped and rounded). Plus `withAlpha(argb, alpha)` to swap the alpha byte on an existing ARGB, and `getRgb(argb)` to strip it. Use from game code at the extraParams / setParameter boundary so bare-RGB Haxe ints don't render invisible under strict-D (parser literal `#RRGGBB` semantics are unchanged). 10 unit tests in `ColorUtilsTest`.
- **`BuilderError` typed runtime exception + structured error surfaces** — new `bh.multianim.BuilderError extends haxe.Exception` carries the offending `Node` (for source position via `parsedPos():{file, line, col}`) and an optional `code:String` tag for programmatic filtering. Three call shapes: (1) `builderError(msg, ?code)` member helper on `MultiAnimBuilder` — auto-attaches `currentNode`; (2) `builderErrorAt(node, msg, ?code)` member helper — for sites where an explicit node is in scope (replaces `throw '...' + MacroUtils.nodePos(node)`); (3) `BuilderError.of(msg, ?code)` static factory — for helper classes (`BuilderResult`, `SlotHandle`, `BuilderResolvedSettings`) that have no `currentNode`. All 262 throws in `MultiAnimBuilder.hx` migrated (was 256 plain-string throws + 6 added by Phase 1/2/3 work). Error-surface catch sites in `ScreenManager.rebuildAll()`, `ScreenManager.makeHotReloadFailError()` (powers DevBridge `hot_reload` + SSE notifications), `ScreenManager.strictFail()` (under `-D MULTIANIM_STRICT`), and `DevBridge.handleEvalManim()` (MCP `eval_manim`) now branch on `BuilderError` and return `{file, line, col, code}` to clients. MCP consumers can now render clickable diagnostics for runtime `.manim` errors. Established codes: `"not_a_number"` (expected numeric but got string/color/callback-string), `"missing_ref"` (reference/array/animation not found). Consumer-side `catch (e)` / `catch (e:Dynamic)` / `catch (e:haxe.Exception)` still match (via `haxe.Exception` inheritance); `'$e'` formatting preserved through `toString()` override. **Sibling runtime builder files migrated**: `ProgrammableBuilder.hx` (11 throws; particle/tileGroup lookups attach `progNode` for parserPos), `layouts/MultiAnimLayouts.hx` (6 throws), `ProgrammableUpdatable.hx` (4 throws) — all now route through `BuilderError.of` (or `new BuilderError(msg, node)` where a node is in scope), so their errors also reach the structured-diagnostic path. Parse-time lexer errors in `MacroManimParser.hx` and runtime-injected throws emitted by `ProgrammableCodeGen.hx` into user code remain plain strings (out of scope — they run before or outside the builder error surface).
- **`HeapsUtils.solidTile` / `solidBitmap` helpers** — single shared path for solid-color `h2d.Tile` / `h2d.Bitmap` creation. `solidTile(color, w, h)` returns a 1×1 GPU texture stretched to `(w, h)`; `solidBitmap(color, w, h, ?parent)` is the Bitmap convenience. Color is strict-D `0xAARRGGBB` — top byte is alpha (e.g. `0x80FF8000` = 50%-alpha orange). Top-byte=0 is treated as opaque to match Heaps' `Tile.fromColor` default and keep bare `0xRRGGBB` callers working; for fully transparent, call `Tile.fromColor` directly. Migrated call sites: `MAObject` debug hitbox overlays, `MultiAnimBuilder.generatePlaceholderBitmap` (`SolidColor` generated tile), text tile background, the incremental `SolidColor` update path, `ScreenManager` modal overlay, and the `ProgrammableCodeGen` `SolidColor`/`Cross` emitters (the legacy inline `if (c >>> 24 == 0) c |= 0xFF000000` bake — a no-op once Heaps masks the top byte — is deleted).
- **DevBridge `debugger()` API and `get_debugger_hits` RPC** — JS-`debugger;`-style breakpoint for game code. `screenManager.devBridge.debugger(data, pause=true)` snapshots `data`, auto-captures file/line/method via `haxe.PosInfos`, pushes an SSE `debugger` event, appends the hit to a ring buffer (capacity 100), and optionally pauses the game loop (shared `setPaused()` helper extracted from `handlePause`). Poll via new `get_debugger_hits` RPC (params: `clear`, `limit` ≤ 100, `since_id` cursor; returns `hits[]`, `total`, `dropped`, `lastId`). Compiled out in release — only active with `-D MULTIANIM_DEV`. 9 regression tests in `DevBridgeTest` covering empty buffer, capture without pause, id increment, `since_id` cursor (including past-latest), `limit`, `clear`, ring-buffer drop behavior, and paused-flag side-effect.
- **`UIInteractiveSource` interface** (`bh.ui.UIInteractiveSource`) — abstraction over anything that can feed interactive elements to `UIScreen.addInteractives` / `UIRichInteractiveHelper.register`. Four methods: `getInteractives():Array<MAObject>`, `addRebuildListener(fn)`, `removeRebuildListener(fn)`, `setParameter(name, value)`. Implemented by both `BuilderResult` (runtime path) and every `@:manim` codegen-generated instance class, so both paths are interchangeable at call sites. `UIScreen.addInteractives` and `UIRichInteractiveHelper.register`/`resync`/`bind` now accept `UIInteractiveSource` instead of `BuilderResult` — existing `BuilderResult` calls still work via polymorphism.
- **Codegen instances expose their interactives** — `@:manim` instance classes now generate `getInteractives():Array<MAObject>` (walks the instance's scene graph via `ProgrammableBuilder.collectInteractives`), `addRebuildListener`/`removeRebuildListener` (append/remove from internal `_rebuildListeners` array), a private `_fireRebuildListeners()` helper fired at the end of every typed setter (after `_applyVisibility` / `_updateExpressions`), and a generic `setParameter(name:String, value:Dynamic)` dispatcher that routes to the right typed setter (`setStatus` etc.). The enum dispatcher accepts either an Int index or a String enum value (e.g. `instance.setParameter("status", "hover")` looks up `"hover"` in the param's enum values at codegen time). Instance classes now declare `implements bh.ui.UIInteractiveSource` so `screen.addInteractives(codegenInstance, prefix)` works symmetrically to the runtime path. Switch arm flips inside codegen programmables auto-resync the screen's wrapper map through the same rebuild listener mechanism used by the runtime path. 7 integration tests in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest` covering `getInteractives`, direct screen wiring, string-dispatched `setParameter`, full `UIRichInteractiveHelper` end-to-end cycle, autoStatus end-to-end, and `@switch` arm resync on `setMode()`.
- **`BuilderResult.addRebuildListener(fn)` / `removeRebuildListener(fn)`** — register a callback that fires once per `applyUpdates()` cycle in which any parameter changed. Use to resync external state (e.g. screen-side `UIInteractiveWrapper` maps) after a `@switch` arm flip or param-dependent `repeatable` count change. The listener fires AFTER all conditional/tracked updates and dynamic ref propagation, so the BuilderResult collections are coherent when it runs. Re-entrant `setParameter()` from inside a listener is supported (enters a fresh applyUpdates cycle). No-op on non-incremental results (static snapshots never rebuild), so callers can install listeners without branching on incremental mode.
- **`UIRichInteractiveHelper.resync(result, ?prefix, ?metadataKey)`** — resync bindings against the BuilderResult's current `interactives` list. Removes bindings whose ids no longer exist (filtered by prefix), adds bindings for new ids, and *preserves* existing bindings' state (does not reset `currentState` to Normal). Use after a SWITCH arm flip / REPEAT rebuild changes which interactives are present. The screen's auto-status helper is now resynced automatically (see "Fixed" below).
- **`@switch` block** — `@switch(param) { value: ...; default: ... }` groups multiple conditions on a single parameter. Supports single-value arms (`idle: ...`), multi-value with pipe (`hover | pressed: ...`), comparisons (`<= 10:`, `> 90:`), ranges (`0..100:`), `default:` fallback, and block arms (`value { ... }`) for multiple elements per case. Block arms can contain nested `@()` conditionals. Implemented as `SWITCH` NodeType with O(1) Haxe `switch` dispatch in codegen for pure-enum arms. No unnecessary scene graph wrappers — single-child arms toggle visibility directly on the child.
- **Conditional blocks** — `@(cond) { ... }`, `@else { ... }`, `@else(cond) { ... }`, `@default { ... }` — group multiple elements under a single condition without repeating the `@` prefix on each element. Wraps children in a POINT node. Supports nesting (block-in-block).
- **`@any`/`@all` conditional keywords** — `@any(p1=>v1, p2=>v2)` matches when ANY listed condition matches (OR logic). `@all(p1=>v1, p2=>v2)` matches when ALL listed conditions match (AND logic, same as `@()`/`@if()`). Unlisted parameters are ignored in both modes. Stacked conditionals (`@(a=>1) @(b=>2)`) now produce a clear error directing users to `@all()` or `@any()`.

- **Tile source `center()`/`pivot()` modifiers** — wrap any tile source to set the tile's origin point. `center(source)` is shorthand for `pivot(0.5, 0.5, source)`. `pivot(x, y, source)` sets an arbitrary pivot using 0–1 ratio coordinates. Works everywhere tile sources are used: `bitmap()`, particle `tiles:`, etc. When used inside `bitmap()`, overrides the bitmap's hAlign/vAlign. Implemented as `TSPivot` enum variant in `TileSource`, handled in builder, codegen, and all three bitmap creation paths.

- **Dynamic programmable references in `dynamicRef`** — `dynamicRef($template, val=>$hp)` where `$template` is a `string` or enum parameter of the enclosing programmable. The resolved parameter value names the target programmable. When the template parameter changes, the old visual is torn down and the new programmable is fully rebuilt. Forwarded parameters (`val=>$hp`) propagate incrementally without rebuild. Backward compatible: `$progName` where `progName` is not a parameter falls back to literal name. Strict validation: forwarded parameters must exist on the target programmable (RTE if missing, allowed if target has a default). Works in both builder (via `IncrementalUpdateContext.trackDynamicName`) and codegen (via `generateDynamicNameRefCreate` with container/name/result fields).

- **`UIEventPriority` constants and overlay priority** — `UIEventPriority` class with `Content` (0), `Overlay` (100), `Modal` (200) tiers for event priority. Dropdown (`UIStandardMultiAnimDropdown`) now implements `UIElementPriority` — sets `Overlay` when panel opens, `Content` when closed. `UIPanelHelper` sets `Overlay` priority on all panel interactives. `UIInteractiveWrapper.eventPriority` is now publicly writable for programmatic override. Fixes the dropdown click-through bug where background interactives received events through open overlays.
- **`ScreenShakeHelper`** — lightweight additive screen shake helper for impact feedback. Supports basic shake with linear decay, directional shake (horizontal recoil, vertical landing), and custom decay curves. Multiple concurrent shakes stack naturally. Non-destructive: applies offsets as per-frame deltas relative to the previous frame, so gameplay can freely move the target (camera scroll, layout, animation) without the shake fighting back to a captured baseline. Uniform jitter via `hxd.Rand`.
- **Particle `emitFilter`** — optional callback on `ParticleGroup` to filter particles by spawn position. Called after world position is computed; return `false` to discard the particle. Works for both relative and non-relative groups.
- **Particle `externallyDriven`** — when `externallyDriven: true`, particles do not auto-update from the render loop. Game code drives the simulation via `group.advanceTime(dt)` or `particles.advanceTime(dt)`. Follows the same pattern as `AnimationSM.externallyDriven`. Both group-level time (shutdown, attached paths, spawn curves) and per-particle physics are suppressed until manually advanced.
- **Grid `sceneToHex()`** — convert scene coordinates to hex grid coordinates. Returns the nearest hex `CellCoord` regardless of whether a cell exists. Works for hex grids only (returns null for rect).
- **`.anim` `flipX` / `flipY`** — flip all frames of an animation horizontally/vertically *in place*. The sprite keeps the same untrimmed screen footprint regardless of where the pivot sits within it (tiles are mirrored around the untrimmed center, trim offsets are recomputed, extrapoints auto-mirror around the center). Usable at the file level (`flipX: yes` before any animation), inside `animation { ... }` blocks, and in the `anim name(flipX:yes): "sheet"` shorthand. Animation-level setting overrides file-level default. Shared atlas tiles are cloned before flipping to avoid cross-instance mutation.
- **Particle `shutdown` block and API** — graceful particle stop for looping emitters. `shutdown: { duration: 1.0, curve: easeOutQuad, alphaCurve: ..., sizeCurve: ..., speedCurve: ... }` configures wind-down behavior in `.manim`. At runtime, `particles.shutdown()` or `group.shutdown(?duration, ?curve)` starts a timed shutdown that curves particle count (selectively stops recycling dead particles) and optionally applies global alpha/size/speed multipliers. All curves use progress convention (`mult = 1.0 - curve(t)`). Query state via `isShuttingDown()` and `getShutdownRate()`. `emitBurstAt()` still works during shutdown. Existing `onEnd()` fires when the last particle dies.

- **`@switch` incremental mode** — `setParameter()` on a `@switch`-controlled parameter now triggers full arm rebuild (teardown + rebuild). Arm content is built non-incrementally; all param refs across all arms are collected so that changing any referenced param (switch param, inner conditionals, repeatable counts, `$param` expressions, positions) triggers the rebuild. Supports nested `@switch`, `@()` conditionals, and repeatables inside arms.
- **`@switch` codegen lazy rebuild** — codegen no longer pre-builds all arms and toggles visibility. Instead, uses ordinal-based arm lookup (`switchCounter` at compile time, DFS walk at runtime) to rebuild only the active arm via `ProgrammableBuilder.rebuildSwitchArm()`. Always rebuilds on any parameter change, forwarding current param values to the builder for correct expression evaluation.
- **`@switch` duplicate default rejection** — parser now rejects `@switch` blocks with multiple `default` arms at parse time.

### Changed
- **`.manim` expression parser converted to Pratt; comparison operators now have standard C-like precedence below additive** — `MacroManimParser` previously parsed binary operators right-recursively and re-associated the AST in a post-hoc `binop()` rotation switch that only knew about `*`/`/`/`%`/`div` vs `+`/`-`. Comparison operators (`==`, `!=`, `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=`) had no rotation cases, so they fell into the default arm and effectively bound *tighter* than arithmetic — `$a + $b < $c` parsed as `$a + ($b < $c)`, never `($a + $b) < $c`. The asymmetry was load-bearing only by accident: most call sites that mix arithmetic and comparison (typically inside ternary conditions) put comparison on the right and stayed correct. The new parser is a textbook Pratt loop driven by an `infixBp(token)` precedence table: `*`/`/`/`%`/`div` at lbp 30, `+`/`-` at lbp 20, comparisons at lbp 10, all left-associative (rbp = lbp + 1). The four type-tagged entry points (`parseIntegerOrReference`, `parseFloatOrReference`, `parseStringOrReference`, `parseAnything`) each become a one-liner that delegates to `parseExpression(0, t)`; the atom code (literals, ternary, unary minus, parens, callbacks, `$ref` chains, array literals) was extracted into four `parseAtom_<type>` helpers dispatched via a single `parseAtom(t)` switch. The `binop()` rotation function and `parseNextExpression` are gone. Behaviour change visible to users: any `.manim` expression that mixed arithmetic and comparison left-of-operator (`$a + $b < $c`, `$a + $b == $c + $d`) now parses with the standard C-like grouping. Right-of-comparison shapes (`$a < $b + $c`, `$a == $b * $c`) parsed correctly under the old code by accident and still do. AST shape (`EBinop`/`EUnaryOp`) and downstream consumers (`ProgrammableCodeGen.rvToExpr`, `MultiAnimBuilder.resolveAs*`) are unchanged. Four regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testExprLeftAssocSubtraction` (`$x - 3 - 2 == 5`, would be 9 if right-associated), `testExprLeftAssocDivMul` (`$x / 4 * 3` left-associates across same-precedence multiplicative ops), `testExprParensOverridePrecedence` (paren respect + precedence in one expression), `testExprComparisonBelowAdditive` (the new precedence: `?($a + $b < $c)` takes the false branch when `(a+b) < c` is false, where the legacy `a + (b < c)` reading would take the true branch for the same inputs).

- **`applyConditionalChains` caches its `entryMap` / `applyMap` lookups across `setParameter` calls** — the per-call rebuild allocated 2 `haxe.ds.StringMap` instances + N inserts (one per `conditionalEntries` / `conditionalApplyEntries` entry, keyed by `uniqueNodeName`) on every `applyUpdates` cycle, even when the source arrays were unchanged. `setParameter` fires on every slider drag, hover tracker, and gameplay tick — for any non-trivial UI tree this was 2 `StringMap` + dozens of inserts per frame for nothing. Source arrays only mutate during build and structural rebuild (`trackConditional`, `trackDeferredConditional`, `trackConditionalApply`, `cleanupDestroyedSubtree`'s splice loops over both arrays). Fix: new `cachedEntryMap` / `cachedApplyMap` fields on `IncrementalUpdateContext`, populated lazily in `applyConditionalChains` and invalidated (`field = null`) at every site that mutates the source arrays. New `conditionalMapRebuildCount:Int` instrumentation field counts rebuilds — the regression test `BuilderUnitTest.testIncrementalApplyConditionalChainsCachesMaps` asserts the counter advances by exactly 1 across the warm-up `setParameter` and stays flat across 5 follow-up steady-state `setParameter` calls.
- **`UICardHandLayout` reuses static scratch arrays across `computePathLayout` calls** — `computePathLayout` and `computeEvenArcLengthRates` allocated four fresh `Array<Float>` instances per call (`sampleRates`, `sampleLengths`, `rates`, `adjustedRates`), totalling ~200 Float entries every layout pass. Layout fires on every hover transition, drag step, draw, discard, and rearrange — a steady stream of GC pressure for pure work buffers callers never see. Fix: four static `_scratchSampleRates`/`_scratchSampleLengths`/`_scratchRates`/`_scratchAdjustedRates` reused across calls and cleared with `resize(0)` on entry, matching the existing `_scratch:FPoint` pattern (HL is single-threaded so static sharing is safe). Capacity is retained across calls, so steady-state allocation is zero. New `scratchArrayAllocationCount:Int` instrumentation counter on the class — the regression test `CardHandOrchestratorTest.testPathLayoutReusesScratchArraysAcrossCalls` asserts it stays flat across 4 follow-up calls after a warm-up.
- **Card hand hot paths now avoid per-event and per-frame allocations** — two micro-optimizations on paths that run on every mouse event / every frame. (1) `UICardHandHelper` cached `scratchPoint:h2d.col.Point` once at construction; `onMouseMove`/`onMouseRelease`/`getCardIdAtPosition` reuse it for `globalToLocal` instead of allocating a fresh `Point` per call (`globalToLocal` mutates its input in place, so sharing is safe on the single UI thread). (2) `UICardHandLayout.computePathLayout` and `UICardHandTargeting.updateTargetingLine` switched from `path.getPoint(rate)` (returns a fresh `FPoint`) to `path.getPointInto(rate, scratch)` — saves ~101 `FPoint` allocations per layout call in `EvenArcLength` distribution mode (one per arc-length sample + one per card) and up to `MAX_SEGMENTS + 1` per targeting-line update. Three regression tests: `CardHandIntegrationTest.testMouseHandlersReuseCachedScratchPoint` (asserts the cached `scratchPoint` field identity is preserved across move/release/hit-test and that coords reflect the last call), `CardHandOrchestratorTest.testPathLayoutUsesAllocationFreePointLookup` (EvenRate), `CardHandOrchestratorTest.testPathLayoutEvenArcLengthUsesAllocationFreePointLookup` (EvenArcLength) — both backed by a new `CountingPath` subclass that counts `getPoint` vs `getPointInto` dispatches and asserts `getPoint` was called zero times. (A third item that previously appeared here — replacing `splice(count, 1)` with `pop()` in `FloatingTextHelper.update` — was a regression and has been reverted; see the Fixed entry above.)
- **`UICardHandHelper.animateCardTo` no longer allocates `FPoint` per call** — every layout pass (`applyLayout(true)` for hover/drag/draw, `rearrangeCards` for animated rearrange) called `animateCardTo(entry, new FPoint(...), new FPoint(...), ...)` once per `InHand` card, allocating two FPoints per card per layout — pure waste, since `animateCardTo` only reads `from.x/y` and `to.x/y` and then forwards to `createProjectilePath` (whose `applyStretch` in turn reads-and-forgets). Five cards × multiple layout passes per hover transition = 10s of FPoints per interaction. Fix: `animateCardTo` signature changed from `(entry, from:FPoint, to:FPoint, ...)` to `(entry, fromX:Float, fromY:Float, toX:Float, toY:Float, ...)`. The single `createProjectilePath(pathName, from, to)` call inside the body uses two per-instance scratch FPoints (`scratchFromFPoint` / `scratchToFPoint`) populated from the primitives, since `createProjectilePath` requires `FPoint` arguments. Helper is single-threaded so the per-instance scratch is safe across calls. New `FPoint.creationCount:Int` static counter (one increment per constructor) instruments the assertion; regression test `CardHandIntegrationTest.testApplyLayoutAnimatedDoesNotAllocateFPointsPerCard` builds a 5-card hand, zeroes the counter, calls `applyLayout(true)`, and asserts `creationCount == 0`. Allocation count is currently 0 across all `animateCardTo` callers (`applyLayout`, `rearrangeCards`, `discardCard`, `playCardWithoutTarget`, `cancelDrag`).
- **`ParticleGroup` recycles dead `Particle` instances via a free-list pool** — each `emitBurstAt(...)` previously did `var p = new Particle(this)` per spawn. With sub-emitters, manual bursts, or `spawnCurve`-driven emission, sustained churn allocated a fresh `Particle` (extends `h2d.SpriteBatch.BatchElement`, ~20 fields including `prev`/`next`/`batch` linked-list pointers) for every spawn, even though dead particles had just been removed from the batch and were eligible for reuse. Fix: new `freeParticles:Array<Particle>` field on `ParticleGroup`, populated at every `liveCount--` site in `Particle.update()` (non-loop death, bounds kill, shutdown trim — looping in-place recycle via `init(this)` does NOT enter the pool, since the particle stays in the batch). New `allocParticle()` helper pops from the pool first, falls back to `new Particle(this)`. `start()` and `emitBurstAt()` route through it. Reuse is safe because `SpriteBatch.sync()` calls `e.remove()` (→ `batch.delete(this)` clears `e.batch` and unlinks prev/next from neighbors) the same frame `update()` returns false, so by the time `allocParticle()` pops a recycled particle, it is no longer in the batch and can be re-added cleanly via `batch.add(p)`. `init(p)` resets the per-lifetime fields (life, position, velocity, color, anim/segment indices, `rejected`); pool growth is naturally bounded by peak-concurrent particle count. Two regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testEmitBurstReusesParticleInstancesAfterDeath` (asserts the second 3-particle burst reuses all three of the first burst's `Particle` instances after natural death), `testSustainedBurstHasBoundedParticleAllocations` (asserts unique `Particle` instance count stays at ~burst count over 10 emit-die-emit cycles, instead of growing linearly to `burst × cycles`). Visual tests: 101/101 pass — confirms the pool does not cause stale-tile or batch-relinking crashes in the rendering path.
- **Particle color-curve evaluation is now O(1) per frame per particle** — `Particle.update()` previously called `ParticleGroup.evaluateColorCurve(rate)`, which scanned from index 0 to find the active segment on every frame for every particle (`for (i in 0...colorCurveSegments.length) if (segs[i].startRate <= rate) activeIndex = i;`). With N particles × M color segments × 60 fps the cost scaled as N*M/s with no upper bound. Rate is monotonic inside a particle's lifetime, so the active segment index can only advance forward — caching it gives O(1) amortized per frame. Fix: new `Particle.currentColorSegmentIndex:Int` field (mirrors `currentAnimStateIndex`), advanced via a `while (segIdx + 1 < segCount && segs[segIdx + 1].startRate <= timeNormalized) segIdx++;` loop directly inside `Particle.update()` (no helper call). Reset to 0 in `ParticleGroup.init(p)` (same site as `currentAnimStateIndex`) so recycled particles restart at segment 0 instead of getting stuck on the last segment across an emit-loop cycle. `evaluateColorCurve()` is still present for external callers but no longer on the hot path. Three regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testColorCurveSegments_ColorCorrectAcrossLifetime` (asserts mid-segment colors at boundaries), `testColorCurveSegments_ResetsOnEmitLoopRecycle` (asserts a looping particle restarts at segment 0 after life overflow), `testColorCurveSegments_CacheAdvancesMonotonically` (asserts the cached index never moves backwards within a lifetime and advances across a 4-segment curve).
- **`setParameter()` on a param referenced only in incremental-unsupported slots now throws** — previously, calling `setParameter()` / typed setter (`setFoo`) on a `$param` that appeared only in `interactive` `id`/metadata, `stateanim` selector values, or `stateanim_construct` `animName`/`fps` silently updated the internal field but had no visual effect — the rendered state diverged from the param map with no diagnostic. Both paths now reject up front: builder's `IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` throws `BuilderError.of(..., "untracked_param")` with a message naming the rejected param and the slot reason (e.g. `stateanim selector "direction"`); codegen's typed setter prepends an unconditional `throw '...'` when the param is registered as untracked — generic `setParameter(name, value)` inherits the throw through the typed-setter call. Registration happens during build: builder's `trackIncrementalExpressions` walks INTERACTIVE id/metadata key+value and STATEANIM selectors / STATEANIM_CONSTRUCT animName+fps and calls `ctx.markParamUntracked(name, reason)`; codegen's `generateCreateExpr` / `generateStateAnimCreate` / `generateStateAnimConstructCreate` call the static `recordUntrackedParams(refs, reason)` helper (backed by `untrackedParamRefs:Map<String, Array<String>>`, reset in `resetState`). Error message: `setParameter("<name>", ...) rejected: this param is referenced in incremental-unsupported slot(s) [<reasons>]. Changing it would leave the rendered state inconsistent. Either rebuild the programmable or avoid runtime mutation of this param.` Params fully covered by tracked slots (e.g. only used in `w`/`h`, `pos`, `scale`, `filter`, conditional visibility, graphics color, …) are unaffected and continue to work. Six unit tests in `CodegenIncrementalInteractiveStateanimTest` cover id/metadata/selector rejection in both paths and assert the exact message shape (`setParameter("<name>", ...) rejected` prefix + reason substring); the builder test additionally asserts `BuilderError.code == "untracked_param"`.
- **`MAObject.multiAnimType` is now `public var` (was `public final`)** — required to let both builder and codegen rewrite `MAInteractive(w, h, id, meta)` when a `$param`-driven size changes. Consumers (`UIInteractiveWrapper.containsPoint`, `MAObject.getBoundsRec`, `UICardHandTargeting`, `UIMultiAnimScrollableList`, `UIRichInteractiveHelper`) already re-read on every call — no caller treated the field as stable across calls. Not expected to affect any user code.
- **BREAKING: Strict-D color semantics — `0x` literals are Heaps AARRGGBB, preserved verbatim** — `addAlphaIfNotPresent` is deleted. `ColorArg` abstract is deleted; `PPTColor` storage is a plain `Int`. The parser bakes alpha only for CSS `#` forms (3/6-digit shorthand); `0x...` literals pass through unchanged regardless of length, and every Int-consumer path (`setColor`, `setParameter`, `createFrom`, hot-reload, node tint, graphics shapes, atlas2 autotile, `PixelOutline.inlineColor`, `ReplacePaletteShader`) trusts its input. This fixes the BUG-3 footgun where `setColor(0)` was silently clobbered to `0xFF000000` (opaque black) and `#00000000` / `0x00000000` transparent was unreachable from runtime callers — transparent and opaque-black are now distinguishable everywhere. **Migration:** any `.manim` source using `0xRRGGBB` (6-hex Heaps literal) to mean "opaque RRGGBB" must change to `#RRGGBB` or `0xFFRRGGBB`. Automated sed: `sed -i -E 's/0x([0-9A-Fa-f]{6})\b/#\1/g' *.manim`. The 154 occurrences in this repo's test `.manim` files are migrated; cross-repo consumers (`hx-multianim-playground`, `hx-easy-game`) still have unmigrated literals and will render them transparent until their own sed passes run. Any Haxe caller passing `0xFF0000` to a color-accepting API expecting opaque red must change to `0xFFFF0000`. Existing Fixed entries on `setParameter` / `0xRRGGBB` alpha baking (below) documented the old compensation that is now replaced by this simpler "Int is final" rule. The `testCodegenColorParamTransparentFootgun` test is now `testCodegenColorParamTransparentPreserved` and asserts the fixed behavior.
- **`@ifstrict` removed** — replaced by `@all()` (AND on listed params, unlisted ignored). The old `@ifstrict` behavior (requiring ALL programmable params to be mentioned) is no longer available.
- **`layout(name)` single-argument form** — `repeatable($i, layout("name"))` is now the only accepted form. The legacy two-argument `layout("group", "name")` (where the first argument was a label-only group identifier) is rejected at parse time with a clear error directing users to drop the first argument. All bundled examples, tests, cookbook, and reference docs migrated.
- **Parser cleanup — hxparse removal** — `MultiAnimParser` is now a thin facade that delegates to `MacroManimParser`. The old hxparse-based lexer/parser, the `MPToken`/`MPKeywords`/`LayoutsParsingState`/`MPInterpolationEnum`/`IdentifierType`/`NumberType`/`ValueType` enums and associated machinery are deleted. A single hand-written recursive-descent parser now covers both compile-time (macro codegen) and runtime paths. Public API (`MultiAnimParser.parseFile()`, `ParametersDefinitions`, `PlaceholderTypes`, etc.) is unchanged.
- **`Particles.addGroup(g)` signature** — removed the unused optional `index` parameter (it was never honoured inside the method). Callers passing a second argument must drop it.
- **Hyperlink callback error re-throw in DEV mode** — rich text hyperlink callback errors are now re-thrown after tracing when `MULTIANIM_DEV` is defined, making callback bugs visible during development.
- **`@switch` parameter type support** — `@switch` single-value arms now route through the same type-aware converter as `@(param => value)`. `@switch` on `color` params accepts `#RRGGBB` literals, on `string` params accepts `"quoted string"` literals, on `bool` params accepts `true`/`false`, and on `int` params accepts bare/negative integers. Previously these either failed to tokenize (strings/colors) or silently produced `CoEnums` string-matches that never matched integer-backed values (bool, color) at runtime. Range/comparison arms (`<= N`, `N..M`, `> N`, etc.) are now rejected at parse time on non-numeric parameters with a clear error. `@switch` on `float`, `tile`, and `flags` parameters is rejected at parse time (use `@(p => bit[N])` for flag tests). Pipe-separated multi-value arms (`v1 | v2 | v3`) are type-aware for discrete non-enum/non-string params (color, int, uint, bool) via a new `CoAnyOf` conditional — each arm value is converted through `stringToConditional` and OR'd at match time. Enum/string pipe arms keep the existing `CoEnums` path.
- **`crossfade` transition semantics** — `crossfade(duration, ?easing)` is now a sequential blend-through-zero instead of a simultaneous two-sided fade. The outgoing element fades fully to alpha 0 over `duration`, then the incoming element waits and fades from 0 to its target alpha over another `duration`. Total wall-clock duration is `2 × duration`. Previously both states animated in parallel over a single `duration`, producing a noticeable mid-point alpha spike when old + new were both ~50% visible. Applies to all three transition paths (incremental builder, codegen no-parent, codegen parent-rewrite). Reference image for `95-transition` regenerated.
- **`UIPanelHelper` / `UITooltipHelper` host parameter is now `UIComponentHost`** — the constructor `screen` parameter's type changed from the concrete `UIScreenBase` to the `UIComponentHost` interface, completing the same migration previously done on `UICardHandHelper`. `UIComponentHost` gains an `onScreenEvent(event, source)` method (needed by `UIPanelHelper` to push its `EVENT_PANEL_CLOSE` notification). All existing construction sites continue to work unchanged since `UIScreenBase` implements `UIComponentHost`. Enables unit-testing these helpers against a mock host without standing up a full screen.

### Fixed
- **Transition cancellation left animated property at interpolated value, poisoning the next transition's baseline (refines "Transition cancellation overwrote user-set transform" below)** — the earlier rc.5 fix made cancel paths "stop the tween; saved fields are intentionally not reapplied" because the prior restore-everything behavior clobbered user-set X/Y/scale on a fade. That fix introduced a new bug: a hide tween animating alpha 1.0 → 0.0, cancelled mid-flight, left `obj.alpha` at e.g. 0.6. The next show then ran `final preAlpha = obj.alpha` (0.6) and tweened 0 → 0.6, leaving the element permanently dim. Symmetric story for `TransFlipX` / `TransFlipY` (scaleX/scaleY baseline) and `TransSlide` ({x, y, alpha} baselines). Fix: per-transition `restore:Null<Void -> Void>` closure stored on each `activeTransitionTweens` entry, snapping ONLY the properties this transition was writing back to their captured pre-transition values — `TransFade` restores alpha; `TransFlipX`/`Y` restores scaleX/Y; `TransSlide` restores {x, y, alpha}. User mutations on properties this transition wasn't animating still survive cancel (the rc.5 contract is preserved per-property). Called from both `cancelActiveTransition` / `cancelAllTransitions` and the natural `tween.onComplete` so cancel and completion converge on the same property snap. Mirrored across `IncrementalUpdateContext` (runtime builder path) and `CodegenTransitionHelper` (codegen path). Four regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalTransitionFadeRecoversAlphaBaselineAfterMidFlightCancel` (runtime fade), `testCodegenTransitionFadeRecoversAlphaBaselineAfterMidFlightCancel`, `testCodegenTransitionFlipXRecoversScaleBaselineAfterMidFlightCancel`, `testCodegenTransitionSlideRecoversPositionBaselineAfterMidFlightCancel`.
- **Codegen presence-transition hide branch clobbered user-reparented objects on completion** — `CodegenTransitionHelper.executePresenceTransition`'s hide branches capture the wrapper's parent and on tween completion call `capturedParent.removeChild(capturedObj)` and restore animated properties to their pre-transition baseline. If game code reparented the conditional element mid-transition (a common pattern when bubbling a card from a hand into a slot or moving a tooltip into a different overlay), the `removeChild` silently no-oped on the wrong parent but the property restores still fired — slamming the user-owned object back to its pre-fade x/y/alpha and breaking the new layout. The `cancelActiveTransition` path was unaffected (does not touch parent ownership). Fix: every property-restore closure in `setPresenceWithTransition` now opens with `if (capturedObj.parent != capturedParent) return;` — the helper still owns the object only when it's still under the same parent it tracked. One regression test in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` (`testCodegenSlidePresenceTransitionLeavesReparentedObjectAlone`) — slide-left hide partway, reparent the conditional element to a user-owned container, advance past completion, assert the user reparent survives and x reflects the slide tween's end state (preX - slideOffset), not the restored preX.
- **`UIMultiAnimGrid.cellDragUpdateMove` allocated 1–2 `FPoint`s per cursor event during a cell drag** — built on the rc.5 `_scratchPoint` / `_scratchFPoint` scaffolding for the stationary hover path (`cellAtPoint` / `sceneToHex` / `hitTestHex`), but the drag-tick path went through a separate `sceneToLocal` helper that still did `new FPoint(local.x, local.y)` and was called 1–2× per `cellDragUpdateMove`. With `cellDragFindTarget` also routing through `cellAtPoint` per move (writing through `_scratchFPoint`), naively reusing `_scratchFPoint` for the drag-target local point would let the find-target call clobber the drag-target value mid-routine — an aliasing hazard, not just an allocation one. Fix, two parts: (1) new `sceneToLocalInto(parent, sceneX, sceneY, out:FPoint)` writes the local coords into a caller-owned FPoint; the existing allocating `sceneToLocal` stays for callers that legitimately retain the result (e.g. `createProjectilePath`'s Stretch endpoint). (2) New class-level `static final _scratchSceneToLocalFP:FPoint` deliberately separate from `_scratchFPoint` so cellAtPoint can write its own scratch without disturbing the drag-target local point. Comment on the new field documents the aliasing rationale. Net: 0 allocations per drag-move event. One regression test in `UIMultiAnimGridTest` (`testCellDragMoveDoesNotAllocateFPoint`) — start a drag via press, warm one move, then 20 follow-up moves; asserts `FPoint.creationCount` delta is 0.
- **`getElements` removed from `UIScreen` and `UIControllerScreenIntegration` interfaces; production code must use `forEachElement` (refines line 45 below)** — the rc.5 entry introduced `forEachElement` as a streaming alternative to `getElements` but kept `getElements` on the interfaces "for external callers". With `UIDefaultController.update` and `getEventElements` now both routed through `forEachElement`, leaving `getElements` on the interfaces was an attractive nuisance — any future production hot-path caller could reach for it and silently re-introduce the per-event Array + concat allocation shape. Fix: removed the method from `UIScreen` (`src/bh/ui/screens/UIScreen.hx`) and `UIControllerScreenIntegration` (`src/bh/ui/controllers/UIController.hx`). Kept on `UIScreenBase` as `@:noCompletion public function getElements(...)` for utest assertions only — comment notes that it allocates and production code paths must use `forEachElement`. One regression test in `EventPriorityTest`: `testUIDefaultControllerHotPathNeverCallsGetElements` injects a `ThrowingGetElementsIntegration` that throws on `getElements(...)` and exercises the full input hot path — `handleMove`, push/release `handleClick`, `handleMouseWheel`, key down/up `handleKey` — asserting zero `getElements` calls.
- **Scratch-state reentrancy hardening (audit follow-up)** — four pre-emptive hardenings to scratch/shared-state usage flagged by an audit pass. None of these were currently triggerable bugs; the goal is to prevent silent state corruption the moment a plausible-looking callback is wired up. All changes zero-allocation on the per-frame / per-call hot path. (1) `AnimatedPath.seek` / `update` / `getState` doc — the three methods return the same mutable `currentState` reference; the class-level "Mutated in-place on each update" contract at `AnimatedPathState:22` is now also called out at each return-site so callers can't accidentally stash the reference across frames. Doc-only. (2) `UICardHandHelper._scratchPositions` static → instance — was `static final` (shared across all helper instances); a future scene with two card hands could collide on the buffer if any code path ever interleaved their `getCardAtBasePosition` hit-tests. Per-instance now; one `Array` allocation per helper at construction, zero per-frame impact. (3) `IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` re-entry guard — new `applyingUpdates:Bool` field throws `reentrant_apply_updates` BuilderError if the pass-2 forwarding loop re-enters via a child rebuild listener that calls `setParameter` back into the parent. `fwdCtxScratch.resize(0)` at the entry would otherwise clobber the outer iteration. The post-cleanup rebuild-listener dispatch (intentionally re-entry-safe per the comment at that site) unsets the flag before firing so the architected re-entry path is preserved. Today no listener triggers this; if any future cross-programmable wire-up does, the throw catches the bug instead of silently dropping forwardings. Zero allocation. (4) `HotReload.restoreParams` conditional batch — `result.beginUpdate()` was called unconditionally; if a dev callback fires hot reload from inside an active batch on the same result, the nested `beginUpdate` throws `nested_begin_update` and the dev session crashes. Now checks `result.batchMode` and piggybacks on the existing batch if one is open. `IncrementalUpdateContext.batchMode` exposed as `(default, null)` (public getter, direct internal writes preserved); `BuilderResult.batchMode` added as a `(get, never)` proxy. Dev-only file (`#if MULTIANIM_DEV`).
- **Codegen `get_name(index)` / `get_name(x, y)` returned dangling refs to detached `h2d.Object`s when an inner visibility conditional hid that index** — `ProgrammableCodeGen` populates `indexedNamedElements` (and the 2D variant) during the static-unroll path: each iteration of `unrollRepeatChildren` registers a `_eN` field at a literal index. The generated `get_<name>(index)` switch / `get_<name>(x, y)` if-chain returned that field unconditionally, but a sibling `@($i < $count) #cell[$i] ...` conditional only detaches `_eN` from the scene graph via `_applyVisibility` — never destroys it, never unregisters from the accessor. So `get_cell(3)` after `setCount(2)` returned a live `h2d.Object` reference whose `.parent == null`; callers reading or mutating `alpha`/`x`/`y`/`addChild` on it silently wrote dead state until/unless the conditional flipped back to visible. Divergent from the runtime `BuilderResult.getUpdatableByIndex` path, which only registers initially-visible entries (deferred conditionals materialize lazily) so initially-hidden indices return null. Fix: both accessor shapes now gate each generated case body on `_eN.parent != null` (`return _eN.parent != null ? _eN : null`). Single null check per accessor call; no extra fields, no extra storage. Hidden indices return null on both the static unroll path and after a runtime visibility flip. Two regression tests in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` against new fixture `test/examples/119-codegenRepeatShrink/`: `testCodegenIndexedNamedHiddenReturnsNull` (1D — repeatable + `@($i < $count)` conditional) and `testCodegenIndexed2DNamedHiddenReturnsNull` (2D — repeatable2d + `@all($x < $cols, $y < $rows)` conditional), both covering baseline visible / shrink / regrow cycles.
- **`Tween.step` propagated NaN into target properties when `duration <= 0`** — zero (or negative) duration is a legitimate "skip" intent — `crossfade(0)` / `slide(left, 0)` in a `.manim` transition, programmatic `mgr.tween(obj, 0, [...])` for instant-snap fallbacks, or any caller deriving `duration` from a runtime expression that lands on 0. The hot loop computed `t = clamp(elapsed / duration, 0, 1)` and fed that into `lerp(from, to, t)`. With `duration = 0` and a first `step(0)` call (no time has passed yet), `elapsed / duration = 0 / 0 = NaN`; `FloatTools.clamp` is not NaN-aware (all NaN comparisons return false), and `lerp(from, to, NaN)` returns NaN, which propagated into `obj.alpha` / `obj.x` / `obj.y` / `obj.scaleX/Y` / `obj.rotation`. The target object then silently disappeared from the scene (NaN alpha clamps to fully transparent on render, NaN positions are nonsensical to the renderer). Fix: `Tween.step` now early-returns with a snap-to-final-state when `duration <= 0` — every entry is written to its `.to` value, `elapsed = duration`, returns `true` so the tween completes on the same step. Bypasses the lerp + clamp entirely. Two regression tests in `TweenManagerTest`: `testZeroDurationTweenDoesNotProduceNaN` (assert no NaN written to any of alpha/x/y/scale/rotation after `mgr.update(0)` on a 0-duration tween) and `testZeroDurationTweenSnapsToFinalValue` (assert properties reach target and `onComplete` fires on first step).
- **Codegen literal-name `dynamicRef($X, p => $outer)` did not forward parent param changes into the child** — the runtime builder wires `incrementalContext.trackDynamicRef(...)` for forwarded params unconditionally, so `parentResult.setParameter("parentVal", N)` propagates into the child's `val`. The matching codegen path (`generateDynamicRefCreate`) built `_refParams` once at construction and returned `exprUpdates: []` — nothing pushed to `expressionUpdates`, so subsequent parent setters left the child silently stuck at the construction value. The dynamic-name variant (`generateDynamicNameRefCreate`) was already correct. Compounding factor: `_comp_<programmableRef>` is shared by all sibling sites that reference the same programmable (last-writer-wins), so the forwarded-param updater could not just key off the shared field — it would target whichever sibling happened to be built last. Fix, two parts: (1) new per-site `_dynref_lit_<fieldName>:BuilderResult` field on the parent (allocated only when forwarded refs exist) so the updater targets THIS site's child; the site-build write fills both `_comp_<programmableRef>` (legacy lookup) and `_dynref_lit_<fieldName>` (per-site updater target). (2) the codegen emits an `expressionUpdates` entry with `paramRefs = collectRVParamRefs(values)` and an updater body that rebuilds `_refParams` from the current parent fields, then runs `child.beginUpdate(); setParameter(k, v); child.endUpdate();` with the same `try/cancelUpdate/throw` shape introduced for the dynamic-name path. Three regression tests in new `CodegenDynamicRefForwardingTest` against new fixture `test/examples/118-codegenDynamicRefForwarding/`: `testLiteralNameDynamicRef_Builder_ForwardsParentParamToChild` (runtime baseline), `_Codegen_TypedSetterForwardsParentParamToChild` (typed setter route), `_Codegen_SetParameterForwardsParentParamToChild` (dispatcher route).
- **`SlotHandle.setContent(null)` reached `container.addChild(null)` and surfaced as an opaque h2d failure** — the public type signature is `setContent(obj:h2d.Object)` (non-null at the type level), but Haxe does not enforce nullability at runtime and a caller passing `null` (typically by mistake, or by routing a `Null<h2d.Object>` value through an inferred-type call site) reached the `contentTarget.addChild(obj)` path with a confusing h2d-internal error. `setContent(null)` is also semantically ambiguous — the natural reading is "clear the slot," but `clear()` is the documented API for that. Fix: explicit `null` guard at the top of `SlotHandle.setContent` throws `BuilderError.of('SlotHandle.setContent received null — use clear() to empty the slot', "slot_null_content")` before any partial mutation. The slot stays usable after the rejection (no state changes; the next `setContent(validObj)` works normally). One regression test in `ParameterizedSlotTest` (`testSlotSetContentRejectsNullWithBuilderError`) — asserts the throw is a `BuilderError`, `code == "slot_null_content"`, slot stays empty after the rejection, and a follow-up `setContent(validObj)` succeeds.

- **`Tween.removeTargetOnComplete` was silently dropped for tweens wrapped in `TweenSequence` / `TweenGroup`** — the flag (introduced in the rc.5 entry below for `fadeOut(removeOnComplete=true)`) was only honored in the bare-tween arm of `TweenManager.update` (`HTween` case). The `HSequence`/`HGroup` arms only fired the outer `seq.onComplete`/`group.onComplete`, and the per-child completion sites inside `TweenSequence.step` / `TweenSequence.finish` / `TweenGroup.finish` only fired the per-tween `onComplete` — `removeTargetOnComplete` was never checked. So a caller that did `var t = mgr.createTween(obj, 0.1, [Alpha(0.0)]); t.removeTargetOnComplete = true; mgr.sequence([t]);` got a completed fade with `obj` still parented to the scene, silently diverging from the bare-tween contract. `TweenGroup.step` also had no per-tween completion site at all (groups intentionally fire per-tween `onComplete` only in `finish()`), so `removeTargetOnComplete` could not fire even if the test were added. Fix: new `Tween.runCompletionHooks()` consolidates the "fire `onComplete` then apply `removeTargetOnComplete`" sequence; called from the `HTween` arm of `TweenManager.update`, both `TweenSequence.step` / `finish`, and `TweenGroup.finish`. `TweenGroup.step` gains a `removeTargetOnComplete`-only branch at per-tween completion (the `onComplete` asymmetry is preserved — pre-existing intentional behavior). Two regression tests in `TweenManagerTest`: `testRemoveTargetOnCompleteFiresForTweenInsideSequence` and `testRemoveTargetOnCompleteFiresForTweenInsideGroup` — parent `addChild(obj)`, attach a tween with `removeTargetOnComplete = true`, wrap in `sequence`/`group`, advance past completion, assert `obj.parent == null`.
- **`UIMultiAnimDropdown` panel rendered behind host programmable siblings when the dropdown was embedded after `customAddToLayer` ran** — `customAddToLayer` ran during initial element add and placed the panel at `getHigherLayer(originalLayer)`, but a common embedding pattern is to add the dropdown to a host programmable's slot or layer *after* the dropdown was registered with the screen. The host's actual scene-graph layer is then higher than `originalLayer`, so the panel landed on a lower layer than the host and got occluded by any of the host's later siblings (a panel positioned over a card, list item, or popup). Fix: new `ensurePanelAboveHostLayer()` called from `startOpen` walks the parent chain from the dropdown root, finds the first ancestor that is a direct child of a registered screen layer, and re-parents the panel to one layer above that ancestor's layer. No-op when the panel is already on the correct layer or when the dropdown is not embedded under a registered layer (legacy direct-on-screen case).
- **`UIPanelHelper.dispose()` left fading-out named panels parented to the scene root** — the single-panel branch already tracked `fadingOutObj` and called `obj.remove()` during `dispose()`, but the named-panel branch only tracked the in-flight `Tween` (via `namedFadeOutTweens`). `dispose()` called `tween.cancel()` to flush the tween, but `TweenManager.cancel` intentionally suppresses `onComplete` — which is the only path that normally calls `obj.remove()` on the panel object. So named panels closed mid fade-out and then disposed stayed parented to the scene root forever (silent leak — visible as a stuck panel after rapid open/close cycles). Fix: parallel `namedFadeOutObjs:Map<String, h2d.Object>` mirrors the tween map, populated alongside `namedFadeOutTweens` in `closeNamedImpl` (and cleared by the same removal sites + the fade-out `onComplete`). `dispose()` now detaches the objects via `obj.remove()` *before* cancelling the tweens, mirroring the single-panel branch. The unused per-state `fadeOutTween:Null<Tween>` field was removed from `PanelState` — the named-panel fade-out is and was tracked solely through the helper-level maps. One regression test in `UIPanelHelperTest` (`testDisposeRemovesFadingOutNamedPanelFromScene`) opens a named panel, closes it (starts fade-out), advances 0.1s into the fade, calls `dispose()`, and asserts the panel's `h2d.Object.parent == null` immediately and after further updates.

- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` leaked `builder.stateStack` frames and dirty `changedParams`/`hasChanges` on any mid-update throw** — `applyUpdates` pushes a builder-state frame at the top, sets `builder.indexedParams`/`builderParams`, then runs four phases that can throw — `applyConditionalChains` (chain resolution), tracked `updateFn` callbacks (tracked-expression closures), the dynamicRef forwarding loop (already guarded for the child batch in rc.5; can still rethrow into the parent), and `rebuildDynamicNameRef` (template-rename rebuild). On any throw the bottom `builder.popBuilderState()` was skipped, so every later build/update on the same builder ran against the failed call's stale `indexedParams`/`builderParams` and the parent context's `changedParams`/`hasChanges` stayed flagged — every subsequent `setParameter` then re-evaluated tracked expressions and re-fired dynamicRef forwarding for params that did not actually change in the new call. Compounded across hot-reload eval-manim loops and screen rebuilds that catch-and-continue. Fix: wrap the four phases in `try/caught:Null<Dynamic>`; on catch, capture and skip downstream phases. `popBuilderState`, `changedParams.clear()`, `hasChanges = false` now run unconditionally before the rethrow. Rebuild listeners are suppressed on the throw path (a partially-applied update is not a rebuild; firing listeners against inconsistent visibility/expression state would invite re-entrant `setParameter` calls). One regression test in `DynamicRefTest` (`testForwardingFailureRestoresParentBuilderStateAndIncrementalState`) — parent forwards a uint into a child bool, the throw unwinds, asserts `builder.stateStack.length` is unchanged, `hasChanges == false`, and `changedParams` is empty.
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` left `batchMode = true` on any rejection thrown inside a `beginUpdate` batch** — the rc.5 entry below ("Forwarded `setParameter` failure mid-batch leaked `batchMode = true` into the child") fixed the parent's forwarding loop, but `setParameter` itself has four rejection paths — `untracked_param`, `unknown_param`, `invalid_param_value`, and the typeDesc fallback — that all throw straight out without unwinding the caller's own batchMode. A user batch wrapped in their own try/catch then trapped the BuilderError, but the next `result.beginUpdate()` wedged with `nested_begin_update` until the result was rebuilt. Symmetric problem to the forwarding fix; same recovery shape. Fix: `setParameter` extracted into `setParameterImpl`; the public method wraps it in `try { ... } catch (e:Dynamic) { if (batchMode) cancelUpdate(); throw e; }`. `cancelUpdate` is now also a user-facing recovery primitive — the docstring updated to note callers wrapping in try/catch do not need to call `cancelUpdate` explicitly. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testSetParameterThrowInsideBatchDoesNotWedgeBatchMode`) opens a batch, catches an `unknown_param` throw, asserts the next `beginUpdate()` succeeds, then exercises the same recovery for `invalid_param_value`.
- **`ProgrammableCodeGen` dynamicRef forwarded-param update did not unwind the child `batchMode` on a mid-batch throw** — the codegen path emits `child.incrementalContext.beginUpdate(); for (k => v in _refParams) { child.setParameter(k, v); } child.endUpdate();` to forward parent param changes into a dynamicRef child. The matching runtime path in `MultiAnimBuilder.applyUpdates` was hardened in rc.5 with `try { ... } catch (e) { child.cancelUpdate(); throw e; }`, but the codegen path remained naked — any `setParameter` rejection (e.g. a parent uint forwarding `42` into a child bool, or any forwarded value the slow path rejects) left the child stuck inside `batchMode = true` and the next `child.beginUpdate()` wedged with `nested_begin_update`. Fix: codegen now emits the same `try/cancelUpdate/throw` shape so runtime and codegen forwarding paths recover identically. One regression test in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` (`testCodegenDynamicRefForwardingFailureLeavesChildUsable`) using the new fixture `test/examples/117-codegenForwardingFailure` — parent uint `p` forwards into child bool `c`, `instance.setP(42)` throws on bool coercion, asserts the child is still usable for a fresh `beginUpdate`/`setParameter`/`endUpdate` cycle.
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.cleanupDestroyedSubtree` leaked conditional entries whose object was already detached from the scene graph** — when a top-level `@()` conditional's body is initially-unmatched it enters the deferred path with `entry.object.parent == null` (the anchored sentinel is parented under the programmable root, not the wrapper); a `setPresenceWithTransition` mid fade-out also temporarily detaches the object. The cleanup's `isDescendantOf` short-circuit (`obj.parent != null && isDescendantOf(obj, container)`) treated parentless wrappers as "not under container" and skipped them, so destroying the enclosing subtree (SWITCH arm flip, REPEAT shrink, dynamicRef template rebuild) leaked entries indefinitely. Same shape on `deferredEntries` (wrapper detached the same way) and `activeTransitionTweens` (fade-out completion detaches the target before the cleanup runs). Fix: cleanup now probes the anchored sentinel as a second route (`sentinel == container || isDescendantOf(sentinel, container)`) for both `conditionalEntries` and `deferredEntries`, and drops the live-parent short-circuit on `conditionalApplyEntries`/`activeTransitionTweens` so detached-but-still-rooted-under-container objects are still reclaimed via the ancestor walk. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testCleanupDestroyedSubtreeReclaimsDetachedConditionalEntries`) — a `flag:bool=true` programmable with two top-level `@()` arms ends initial build with 2 conditional entries (one visible + one deferred-hidden), `cleanupDestroyedSubtree` reclaims both.
- **Param-dep `repeatable` fallback silently no-opped `setParameter` on dynamicRef/staticRef refs, child conditional gates, and per-node `pos`/`scale`/`rotation`/`alpha`/`tint`/`filter` refs** — the rc.5 entry below ("setParameter on a param referenced inside a param-dependent repeatable body silently no-opped") wired up untracked-param rejection for INTERACTIVE id/metadata and STATEANIM selectors via `markUntrackedParamsInSubtree` (runtime) and `recordUntrackedParamsInSubtree` (codegen). Both helpers switched on only three node kinds, so every other ref shape in the body remained silently stale: `dynamicRef($tpl, p=>$x)` target + forwarded params, `staticRef($name, …)` symmetric, `@(p=>v)` / `@else` conditionals on children, and the per-node decorations `pos` / `scale` / `rotation` / `alpha` / `tint` / `filter`. Conditional gates are a special case — the runtime path already folds them into `repeatParamRefs` via `collectChildConditionalParamRefs`, so a setParameter on a conditional gate triggers a full body rebuild and must NOT be marked untracked; the codegen path does not, so codegen rejects them. Fix, both helpers: (1) extended the switch to handle `DYNAMIC_REF` and `STATIC_REF`, marking refs in target + parameter map values; (2) per-node `pos` (via `collectCoordinateParamRefs`), `scale`, `rotation`, `alpha`, `tint`, `filter` (via `collectFilterParamRefs`) walked after the kind switch; (3) helpers take a `rebuildRefs`/`countParamRefs` arg listing refs already wired to trigger a body rebuild (iterator-spec count + child conditional refs on the runtime path; iterator-spec count on the codegen path), and refs present in that list are excluded from the untracked-marking pass; (4) codegen `recordUntrackedParamsInSubtree` walks `node.conditionals` and marks conditional-gate refs as untracked (no rebuild wiring there), while the runtime fold via `collectChildConditionalParamRefs` keeps the runtime path's rebuild-on-conditional contract. Six regression tests added to `CodegenRepeatFallbackUntrackedTest` against three new fixtures in `test/examples/114-codegenRepeatFallbackUntracked/`: `testParamDepRepeat_DynRefTarget_Builder_Throws` + `_Codegen_Throws` (dynamicRef target), `testParamDepRepeat_ConditionalRef_Builder_TriggersRebuild` (conditional ref ON the runtime path must rebuild — no throw) + `_Codegen_Throws` (codegen must reject), `testParamDepRepeat_ChildPos_Builder_Throws` + `_Codegen_Throws` (child position). The existing `testParamDepRepeatRebuildDynamicRef` test in `BuilderUnitTest` was updated to reflect the new contract — `setParameter("value", 25)` on a dynamicRef parameter inside a param-dep repeat now throws `untracked_param` (was previously a silent no-op the test treated as expected behavior).
