- **`ParticleGroup` rejected-particle bookkeeping leaked across four hot-path sites — `liveCount` drift, recycle never-frees, same-frame physics + lifecycle on rejection, OnInterval misfire** — `emitFilter` rejection in `ParticleGroup.init` (legitimate "spawn nothing in this region" pattern, e.g. spatial culling, line-of-sight gates) flips the particle to `life = maxLife + 1`, sets `rejected = true`, and returns false. Four downstream sites mishandled this: (1) `emitBurstAt` only incremented `liveCount` inside the `if (accepted)` branch, but the death branches in `Particle.update` decrement unconditionally — including stillborn rejected particles. Repeated rejected bursts (line-of-sight gating during a sweep, an animation that hides every spawn) drove `liveCount` negative, which then poisoned `shutdownTargetCount = liveCount` at `shutdown()` time. Fix: count every batched particle in `emitBurstAt`; the symmetry restored against the death-branch decrement. (2) The delayed-init branch of `Particle.update` (where `delay > 0` defers `init()` past the burst) had no `else` for the `init() returned false` path: rejected particles fell through into the physics block (gravity, force fields, position update) and the lifecycle branch, getting freed in the same tick that rejected them. Burst path already handled this — delayed-init now mirrors with an explicit `return true` after the failed init. (3) Both recycle sites in `Particle.update` (the out-of-bounds branch and the end-of-life branch in looping groups) called `group.init(this)` and ignored the return value. When `init()` rejected the recycle, the particle stayed in the batch with `rejected = true`, `life = maxLife + 1`, ready to cycle again next frame, forever. Fix: both recycle sites now check the return value and free via `liveCount-- + freeParticles.push(this)` when init rejects, matching the non-loop branch. (4) `checkIntervalSubEmitters` at line 291 had no `!rejected` guard — a rejected particle has `life = maxLife + 1` and `lastSubEmitTime = 0`, so the `life - lastSubEmitTime >= interval` test fires for any reasonable interval, spawning sub-emitter bursts (sparks, smoke trails) from particles the filter explicitly rejected. The OnBirth/OnDeath triggers below already gate on `!rejected`; the interval trigger now does too. Four regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testEmitBurstWithRejectionKeepsLiveCountFromDriftingNegative` (3 rejected bursts, assert `liveCount == 0`), `testDelayedInitRejectionSkipsSameFramePhysics` (rejected delayed-init particles must remain in batch one frame, not be freed in the rejection tick), `testRejectedRecycleInLoopGroupFreesParticles` (looping group with all-rejecting filter must free particles after first recycle reject, not cycle forever), `testRejectedParticleDoesNotFireIntervalSubEmitters` (rejected particle with OnInterval sub-emitter must not spawn sparks).
- **Hyperlink callback exception policy traced unconditionally and rethrew under the wrong build flag** — `MultiAnimBuilder`'s `richText` `onHyperlink` handler ran the user callback inside `try { ... } catch (e:Dynamic) { trace('Hyperlink callback error: $e'); #if MULTIANIM_DEV throw e; #end }`. Two problems: production builds emitted a `trace(...)` for every callback exception (wrong: the rest of the codebase silences error spam in production, see `ScreenManager.rebuildAll`/`loadScreen`, `MacroManimParser` flow warnings — all gated on `MULTIANIM_DEV`), and `MULTIANIM_DEV` rethrew the exception (wrong flag: the rest of the codebase uses `MULTIANIM_DEV` for tracing/visibility and `MULTIANIM_STRICT` for rethrow / fail-fast — see the documented strict-mode contract in `CLAUDE.md`). Fix: matched the established three-tier policy — production silently swallows, `-D MULTIANIM_DEV` traces the error and swallows, `-D MULTIANIM_STRICT` rethrows. One regression test in `RichTextTest` (`testHyperlinkCallbackErrorGatingMatchesBuildFlags`) installs a callback that throws on `link:foo`, captures `haxe.Log.trace`, and asserts: no rethrow without strict, exactly one trace under `MULTIANIM_DEV` mentioning "Hyperlink callback error", zero traces in plain production builds.
- **Codegen `setParameter` dispatcher and typed setters silently accepted bad values for `tile` / array params** — every other primitive arm in the codegen `setParameter` dispatcher (Bool/Int/Color/Float/String) calls `Std.isOfType` and throws with `setParameter("<name>", ...) requires <Type>, got <value>` before the field is mutated and before any rebuild fires. The two `Dynamic`-typed arms — `PPTTile` and `PPTArray` — just did `cast _value` and let HashLink's runtime cast either no-op or fail later. A `setParameter("icon", "oops")` call against a tile param wrote `"oops"` into `_icon`; the failure surfaced deep inside the bitmap rebuild as a confusing `Null access` / no-such-field from `tile.sub(...)`, by which point the field was already corrupted. The matching typed setter (e.g. `setIcon(...)`) had its public arg type pinned to `Dynamic` by `publicParamType` (so default-arg / initial-construction sites stay flexible — tile params have no constexpr literal form), so the typed path was the looser of the two routes and undermined any dispatcher fix. The runtime builder `dynamicToResolvedWithDef`'s `PPTArray` arm had the same shape — non-Array fell through `cast value` into `ArrayString(...)` and HashLink threw an opaque `'Can't cast String to hl.types.ArrayObj'` from inside the resolver, with no mention of the param name or `setParameter` source. Fix, three sites: (1) codegen dispatcher PPTTile / PPTArray arms now do up-front `Std.isOfType` validation matching the other primitive arms' diagnostic shape; (2) the typed setter for PPTTile params (`setIcon`, ...) now validates at the top of the body so the typed and dispatcher routes throw with the same shape; (3) `MultiAnimBuilder.dynamicToResolvedWithDef`'s `PPTArray` arm now throws `BuilderError.of('PPTArray parameter requires Array, got: <value>')` instead of falling through to the unsafe cast (mirrors the existing PPTTile arm). Four regression tests: `BuilderUnitTest.testDynamicToResolvedWithDefRejectsNonArrayForPPTArray` (runtime path), `ProgrammableCodeGenTest.testCodegenTileParamSetParameterRejectsNonTile`, `testCodegenTileTypedSetterRejectsNonTile` (both probe field-corruption preservation: re-applying the original tile after a rejected call must hit the identity-guard no-op path, proving the rejected value never clobbered the backing field), `testCodegenArrayParamSetParameterRejectsNonArray`.
- **`ParticleGroup.shutdown(duration)` curve was a no-op for burst-only and burst-overflowed groups** — the gradual count curve in `updateTime` computed `shutdownTargetCount = round(nparts * aliveFraction)`, baselined on the steady-state cap `nparts`. For looping groups whose live population came from `emitBurst()` rather than the cap (the natural pattern is `emitLoop = true` + `nparts = 0` + per-shot bursts, e.g. trail emitters that fire from sub-emitters or `spawnCurve`), `nparts == 0` collapsed `shutdownTargetCount` to `0` on the very first `updateTime` after `shutdown(duration)`. The death/recycle gate at the end-of-life and out-of-bounds branches then refused every recycle, so the configured shutdown duration was effectively ignored — particles died in roughly one lifetime regardless of the requested `duration`, and a synchronized burst with short `life` looked like an instant flush. The same hazard hit groups whose bursts pushed `liveCount > nparts` at shutdown time: only the `nparts` baseline survived, the overflow was killed in one lifetime. Fix: capture a separate `shutdownInitialCount = max(nparts, liveCount)` at `shutdown()` time and use it as the curve baseline in `updateTime` instead of `nparts`. Steady-state behavior is unchanged (when `liveCount == nparts`, `max(nparts, liveCount) == nparts`); the fix only kicks in when bursts have populated above the cap, which is the case the original formula never accounted for. Two regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testShutdownDurationShapesBurstOnlyGroupWithZeroNparts` (nparts=0, life=0.2, burst=20, shutdown(2.0), assert > 5 alive at 25% through), `testShutdownDurationShapesGroupWithBurstOverflow` (nparts=5, burst pushes to 20, shutdown(2.0), assert > 8 alive at 25% through).
- **`ParticleGroup.init` non-relative bake produced NaN velocity/rotation when `size: 0` (or container `scaleX: 0`)** — the corrected column-norm derivation (Fixed entry below) computed the absolute rotation as `Math.atan2(rB / scX, rA / scX)`. `atan2` is magnitude-invariant under positive scaling, so dividing both arguments by the same `scX` was a no-op in the common case but turned into `0/0` when `scX = 0` — i.e. whenever the per-particle `size` was zero (a legitimate "fade in from nothing" pattern) or any ancestor carried `scaleX: 0`. `atan2(NaN, NaN)` returns NaN; the resulting `cos(absRot)` / `sin(absRot)` poisoned `p.rotation`, `p.vx`, and `p.vy`, and the same NaN flowed through the `worldAnchor` opt-in path because it reuses the same column-axis-angle extraction. Particles emitted at this configuration were invisible (NaN positions) and could leak NaN into downstream ForceFields. Fix: drop the unnecessary divisions — `Math.atan2(rB, rA)`. The column-1 norm `scX` is still used for the actual scaleX bake; only the rotation extraction is changed. Two regression tests in `ParticleRuntimeTest`: `testNonRelativeBakeWithZeroSizeKeepsVelocityAndRotationFinite` (size=0 + identity matrix), `testNonRelativeBakeWithZeroSizeAndWorldAnchorKeepsVelocityFinite` (size=0 under the worldAnchor-composed transform path).
- **`UIScreenBase.getElements(type)` and `forEachElement(type)` ignored `type` for locally-registered elements** — the type filter only narrowed the sub-element provider stream (`UIMultiAnimDropdown`/`Tabs`/`RadioButtons`); for elements added directly via `addElement(...)`, both methods returned/visited the entire `elements` array regardless of `SubElementsType`. `SETReceiveEvents` consumers would still see update-only `UIElementUpdatable` elements and vice versa — silent contract violation that worked by accident because the in-tree consumers never registered an element that implemented exactly one of the two roles. Fix: new private `elementMatchesType(e, type)` filters local elements with the same `Std.isOfType` tests `addElement` uses; `getElements` builds a filtered array, `forEachElement` skips non-matching entries before invoking the callback. Provider streaming is unchanged (already type-filtered). The matching `parseOverlaySettings` color read also moved from `getIntOrDefault` to `getColorOrDefault` to match the stricter Settings API (Changed entry below). Two regression tests in `UIComponentTest` (`testGetElementsFiltersLocalElementsByType`, `testForEachElementFiltersLocalElementsByType`) — register one `EventsOnlyElement` (events but no update) and one `UpdateOnlyElement` (update but no events), assert `SETReceiveEvents` returns/visits exactly the events one and `SETReceiveUpdates` exactly the update one.

- **Transition cancellation overwrote user-set transform on the live `h2d.Object`** — `BuilderResult` exposes the conditional wrapper objects, and game code routinely mutates `obj.x`/`obj.y`/`obj.alpha`/`obj.scale` on them (a dialog that follows a target during slide-in, per-frame layout adjustment, etc.). When a transition was cancelled or rolled into the next presence flip, both `IncrementalUpdateContext.cancelActiveTransition` / `replaceActiveTransition` and the codegen `CodegenTransitionHelper` mirror methods unconditionally restored every captured field (`savedAlpha`, `savedScaleX/Y`, `savedX/Y`) — clobbering any axis the user wrote even though the in-flight tween had never touched it (e.g. fade only animates alpha, but X/Y were rewritten anyway). Reverse-direction flips (show→hide mid-fade) also produced a visible jump back to alpha=1 before the new fade-out started. Fix: cancel paths now only stop the tween; saved fields are intentionally not reapplied. The next transition's `executePresenceTransition` recaptures `preX`/`preAlpha`/... from the live `obj`, so user mutations propagate into the new transition's baseline, and reverse-direction blends are smooth. The `target:Bool` field that already had to be added for direction-aware skip detection (entry below) makes it possible to discriminate same-direction re-entries from genuine cancellations, but the cancellation path itself stays restore-free regardless. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testTransitionCancellationPreservesUserSetTransform`) writes `wrapper.x = 123.4`, `wrapper.y = 67.8` mid-fade, flips visibility, and asserts both axes survive the cancellation.
- **`activeTransitionTweens` entries and `trackTransition*` signatures carried five dead `saved*` floats** — the entry above removed every read site of the captured baseline (`savedAlpha`, `savedScaleX`, `savedScaleY`, `savedX`, `savedY`), with explicit `// Don't restore …` comments at the three cancel paths documenting the intent. The storage was left behind: the entry struct in both `IncrementalUpdateContext` and `CodegenTransitionHelper` still declared the five fields, `trackTransitionTween` / `trackTransitionSequence` still took them as parameters, and ~29 call sites across the two backends still threaded `preAlpha, preScaleX, preScaleY, preX, preY` through them. Pure storage cruft — five `Float` fields per tracked transition entry + five stack args per push. The per-Trans*-case `preAlpha`/`preScaleX/Y`/`preX/Y` locals stay where they are; they still seed each tween's "from" baseline and the hide-branch `onComplete` restoration of `obj.alpha`/`obj.x`/`obj.y` after fade/slide-out. No behavior change. No new test — the existing transition test suite serves as regression coverage; the cleanup is a follow-on to the entry above.
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` allocated 1 outer Array + per-context `{ctx, items}` struct + per-binding `{param, value}` entries on every dynamicRef forwarding fire** — the prior shape walked `dynamicRefBindings`, found relevant entries (those whose `referencedParams` intersect `changedParams`), looked up or appended a `forwardGroups` entry keyed by child context, and pushed a `{param, value}` item onto it. Steady-state shape with two dynamicRefs both forwarding the same parent param (the natural `dynamicRef($widget, val=>$hp)` pattern, repeated for a left/right pair, an inventory row, etc.) was 1 outer Array + 2 group structs + 2 items arrays + 2 entry structs = 7 transient allocations per `setParameter` call. Forwarding fires per UI hover/press transition; on a typical card with two decoration dynamicRefs that's ~7 allocations per mouse move — pure GC churn during hover sweeps. Fix: dispatch rewritten to a two-pass shape over a single reusable `fwdCtxScratch:Array<IncrementalUpdateContext>` field. Pass 1 walks `dynamicRefBindings` once and pushes each *unique* relevant child context onto the scratch (linear `indexOf` dedup — K is typically 1–2). Pass 2 iterates unique contexts and for each one re-walks bindings to find peers, applying them under a single `beginUpdate()`/`endUpdate()` — same batching guarantee as before, plus the same `try/cancelUpdate/throw` recovery shape. Scratch is `resize(0)`'d on entry; capacity persists across calls. Net steady-state allocations on the forwarding hot path: 0. One regression test in `DynamicRefTest` (`testApplyUpdatesDoesNotAllocatePerForwardingCall`) — parent with two dynamicRef children both forwarding `val=>$hp`, 20 successive `setParameter("hp", N)` calls after warm-up, currently pinned at delta=0 via the `dynamicRefForwardAllocCount` watchdog (counter declared but intentionally never incremented at the new zero-alloc sites — flipped on if a future refactor re-introduces per-call alloc and the increment moves with it).
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` allocated an empty `Map<String, ResolvedValue>` and a six-field anonymous struct via `builder.pushBuilderState()` on every call** — `applyUpdates` runs on every non-batched `setParameter` (every mouse move with hover bindings, every `setParameter` from auto-status, every dynamicRef forwarding fire). The resetting `pushBuilderState` snapshots the six builder-state fields onto `stateStack` *and then* writes fresh empty `indexedParams = []` / `builderParams = {}` / `currentNode = null` — but the very next four lines in `applyUpdates` overwrite all three from the context's own fields. The empty Map and Array allocated by the reset were dead-on-arrival. Fix: new `pushBuilderStateNoReset()` snapshots without the reset writes; `applyUpdates` calls the new variant. The resetting variant remains for the other call sites that legitimately depend on the reset (general builder reentry). Watchdog `pushBuilderStateResetCount` (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`) increments only on the resetting path — the test asserts `setParameter` does not invoke it. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testSetParameterDoesNotAllocateEmptyParamContainersInApplyUpdates`) — 10 successive `setParameter` calls after warm-up, asserts delta == 0 against the resetting counter.
- **`AnimationSM.loadState` and `play` allocated a throwaway `[]:Array<AnimationFrame>` every time they cleared the clip's playlist** — the existing `setSingleFrame` helper had reduced the steady-state path to zero allocations (reuses a `_singleFrameBuf:Array<AnimationFrame>` with a single slot), but the matching "clear the playlist" pattern still went through `clip.setFrames([])`, which materializes the wrapper Array and hands it to the clip as the new frames source. `play()` fires per state advance and `loadState()` fires per `addAnimationState` callsite — over a sustained playback session those `[]` allocs are observable in heap captures. Fix: new `AnimationClip.clearFrames()` that `resize(0)`'s the same `_singleFrameBuf` and assigns it back as `this.frames` — same shape as `setSingleFrame`, just empty. `AnimationSM.loadState` and `AnimationSM.play` migrated. `setSingleFrame`'s `_singleFrameBuf[0] = frame` continues to work after a prior `clearFrames()` because Haxe's `arr[i] = v` auto-extends the array — documented with an inline comment at the slot-0 write site. One regression test in `AnimFilterRuntimeTest` (`testPlayDoesNotAllocateEmptyArray`) — 50 `play()` invocations after warm-up assert `AnimationClip.setFramesCallCount` delta is 0.
- **`UICardHandHelper.rearrangeCards` allocated a per-card closure on every draw/discard for every non-skipped card in the hand** — the rearrange call passed an explicit `onComplete = () -> { resolveAnimationComplete(entry); entry.container.scaleX = pos.scale; entry.container.scaleY = pos.scale; }` capturing both `entry` and `pos`. With a 7-card hand and one card drawn, that's 6 throwaway closures per draw — and every drawCard/discardCard fires `rearrangeCards`, so on a sustained card-shuffling session the closure churn adds up. Inspection: `pos.scale` is always `1.0` for rearrange call sites because `computeLayout(-1)` builds positions at base scale, and the path's `state.scale` already settles to 1.0 on the final tick — the explicit scale write was redundant. The only load-bearing side effect of the closure is `resolveAnimationComplete(entry)`, which matters for Disabled cards with a pending re-enable. Fix: new `ActiveAnimation.resolveOnComplete:Bool` flag (defaults false). `animateCardTo` accepts a trailing `resolveOnComplete = false` parameter and stamps the flag onto the queued animation. The three completion sites (per-frame `update` loop, `clear()` drain, `removeAnimationsForEntry` displacement) check the flag and call `resolveAnimationComplete(entry)` before the `onComplete` closure (now nullable, null at the rearrange call site). `animateCardTo`'s snap-if-close (`dx²+dy² < 1`) and no-path early-return paths also honor the flag. Net: rearrange's per-card alloc drops from 1 closure to 0. One regression test in `CardHandIntegrationTest` (`testRearrangeCardsStoresNullOnCompleteInsteadOfPerCardClosure`) — 4-card hand with perturbed positions so the snap-if-close fast path doesn't fire, asserts all three queued ActiveAnimations have `onComplete == null`.
- **`UIDefaultController.getEventElements` allocated a fresh `Array<UIElement>` on every mouse move / click / wheel / key event, plus a fresh sort comparator closure when multiple hits collided** — `getEventElements` is on the hottest UI input path: it runs once per dispatched input event, even for events that hit nothing. The prior shape created a local `var hits:Array<UIElement> = []`, streamed elements through `forEachElement` to populate it, and (if `>1` hit) called `haxe.ds.ArraySort.sort(hits, (a, b) -> { ... })` — the sort comparator was a fresh closure per call, capturing nothing but still an allocation. The capture-target short-circuit at the top (drag in progress) returned `[controllable.captureEvents.target]` — also a fresh single-element Array. Fix: (1) `_hits:Array<UIElement>` instance scratch field (`final`, `resize(0)` on entry); both the capture-target arm and the streaming arm push into it. (2) `_priorityComparator` hoisted to a `static final` field so the closure is allocated once per program rather than per call. Net steady-state per-event allocations on `getEventElements`: 0. Two regression tests in `EventPriorityTest`: `testGetEventElementsReusesScratchAcrossMouseMoves` (50 follow-up moves over two overlapping interactives — sort path) and `testGetEventElementsCaptureTargetShortCircuitReusesScratch` (50 follow-up moves with `captureEvents.target` forced — short-circuit path). Both pin `UIDefaultController.hitsCreationCount` delta to 0; the counter currently tracks controller construction (proxy for the instance-scratch allocation), so a future refactor that re-introduces a `var hits = []` inside the method would need to add a matching increment to make the test re-fire.
- **In-flight presence transitions were cancelled by every unrelated `setParameter`** — `applyConditionalChains()` runs unconditionally on every `setParameter`, walks all conditional entries, and calls `setPresenceOrMaterialize(entry, matched)` even when `matched` is unchanged. The early-return guard `inGraph == newVisible && !hasActiveTransition(obj)` then mistook "transition active" for "transition needs replacing": when no transition spec applied to the changed param, it fell into the cancel-and-restart branch and tore down the live tween (a fade-in mid-flight froze at whatever alpha had been reached, replaced with no replacement tween). Same shape in `CodegenTransitionHelper.setVisibilityWithTransition` / `setPresenceWithTransition`. Repro: `transition { paramA: fade(0.5) }` on a programmable, fade in via `setParameter("paramA", true)`, then call any unrelated `setParameter("paramB", value)` — the fade visibly stalls. Fix, both backends: each tracked `activeTransitionTween` entry now carries the visibility `target:Bool` it animates toward; new `getActiveTransitionTarget(obj)` returns the active target (or null). The early-return guard becomes direction-aware: when `inGraph == newVisible` and the active transition's target equals `newVisible`, the in-flight tween already converges to the right state and is left alone; only mismatched targets (genuine reverse) take the cancel-and-restart path. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testInFlightTransitionContinuesAcrossUnrelatedSetParameter`) starts a fade-in, advances it to ~40% alpha, fires an unrelated `setParameter`, asserts tweens still active on the same target, advances to completion, asserts alpha reaches ~1.0 on the original schedule.
- **Forwarded `setParameter` failure mid-batch leaked `batchMode = true` into the child `IncrementalUpdateContext`** — `IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates`'s `forwardGroups` loop opened `child.beginUpdate()`, drove a sequence of `child.setParameter(item.param, item.value)`, and called `child.endUpdate()` at the bottom. If one of the forwarded `setParameter` calls threw (`unknown_param` / `invalid_param_value` — e.g. a child param vocabulary that diverged from this `resolveFn` after hot reload, or a `PPTBool` child fed an `Int` like `42`), the exception unwound past `child.endUpdate()` and the child stayed inside the batch forever. The next caller of `child.beginUpdate()` then tripped `nested_begin_update` and the child became permanently unusable. The committed `indexedParams` entries from the failed batch are also incoherent — partial state half-applied against unchanged conditional chains. Fix: new `IncrementalUpdateContext.cancelUpdate()` discards `batchMode`/`changedParams`/`hasChanges` without firing `applyUpdates`; the forwarding loop wraps each group in `try/catch`, calls `cancelUpdate()` on the child before rethrowing, so the parent's exception path leaves the child cleanly closed. Committed `indexedParams` entries from the failed batch stay put (the next clean `setParameter` resyncs); the rebuild listeners and tracked expressions never fire on the half-applied state. One regression test in `DynamicRefTest` (`testForwardingFailureLeavesChildUsable`) — parent forwards a uint into a child bool, the child rejects `42` mid-batch, the test asserts the child is still usable for a fresh `beginUpdate`/`setParameter`/`endUpdate` cycle without `nested_begin_update`.
- **`SlotHandle` had no API path to interactives and named elements declared inside a parameterized slot's decoration body** — `MultiAnimBuilder.buildSlotContent` (the codegen-side parameterized-slot construction site) builds the slot decoration into a fresh `InternalBuilderResults` (interactives, named elements, sub-slots, dynamicRefs, htmlTextsWithLinks), threads it through `build()`, and then discarded it — the returned `SlotHandle` carried no reference. Codegen consumers building a parameterized slot via the `buildSlotContent` path (typed metadata wiring, screen interactive registration, decoration-body named-element lookups) had no way to reach those registrations through the handle. Symmetric problem to the one `SwitchArmResults` already solves for `@switch` arms. Fix: `SlotHandle` gains a `@:allow(bh.multianim.MultiAnimBuilder)` `ir:Null<InternalBuilderResults>` field and two public methods — `getInteractives()` (returns a fresh copy of decoration-body interactives) and `getUpdatable(name, ?index, ?indexY)` (named-element lookup, mirrors `BuilderResult.getUpdatable` and `SwitchArmResults.getUpdatable`). `buildSlotContent` now persists its `InternalBuilderResults` onto the handle. The runtime `BuilderResult` slot path (which routes decoration registrations into the parent's IR instead) leaves `ir = null` and the new methods return empty / null. Two regression tests in `ParameterizedSlotTest`: `testSlotHandleExposesDecorationInteractives` (interactive count flips with a `setParameter` toggling a conditional in the decoration body), `testSlotHandleExposesDecorationNamedElement` (named `#frame` element retrievable through the handle).
- **Mixed enum + numeric conditionals on the same param diverged across builder and codegen for unknown enum values** — `setParameter("status", "<unknown enum value>")` is a documented runtime contract: UI widgets (Button, Checkbox, Tabs) call `setParameter("status", "disabled")` on every client template, expecting templates whose `status` enum doesn't list `"disabled"` to leave all `@(status=>v)` arms unmatched while a parallel `disabled:bool` drives visuals. Templates that mixed the enum match with numeric/range comparisons on the same param (`@(status=>normal) … @(status >= 0) … @(status => 0..2) …`) broke this contract two different ways: the runtime stored unknown enum strings as `StringValue(s)`, and `matchSingleCondition`'s `CoRange` branch hit the `default: throw` arm, aborting the whole rebuild; codegen flattened unknown enum strings to `Int(-1)`, which silently lit `@(status < 0)` arms (visibly wrong arm). Fix, both backends: (1) Runtime — `matchSingleCondition`'s `CoRange` branch now handles `Index(idx, _)` (treats enum index as the numeric comparand, symmetric with the codegen path's `_status` field comparison) and `StringValue(_)` (silent no-match, symmetric with `@(status=>v)` arm semantics). (2) Codegen — the unknown-enum sentinel changed from `-1` to `-2147483648` (Int.MIN); each numeric comparison emitted from `condMapToExpr` for an enum-typed param now ANDs in `(_paramExpr != -2147483648)`, so numeric/range arms silently fail to match when the sentinel is set. New fixture `test/examples/116-enumNumericConditional/enumNumericConditional.manim` with five regression tests in `EnumNumericConditionalTest`: runtime no-throw + no-match + restore-after-unknown, codegen no-throw + no-match + restore-after-unknown.
- **`collectCoordinateParamRefs` skipped `LAYOUT($idx)`, `EXTRA_POINT_REF`, `EXTRA_POINT_ANIM`, `SELECTED_HEX_CELL_*`, and `ZERO` variants — incremental position tracking silently no-opped** — both the runtime (`MultiAnimBuilder`) and codegen (`ProgrammableCodeGen`) paths re-collect param refs for a node's `pos` to install a `trackExpression` closure that recomputes position when those params change. The collector's switch was non-exhaustive (`default:` arm), so positions referencing newer coordinate variants — `layout(name, $idx)`, `extraPoint(...)`, `$ref.extraPoint(...)`, `$hex.cellCorner(...)`, `$hex.cellEdge(...)`, and the `ZERO` no-op — collected zero refs. `setParameter("idx", 1)` against a `bitmap(...): layout(base, $idx)` left the bitmap pinned at layout point 0 forever. Fix, both backends: every coordinate variant is now exhaustively handled in `collectCoordinateParamRefs` (runtime), `collectCoordParamRefs` / `collectPositionParamRefs` (codegen). The runtime path also collapsed two duplicated, drifted switches in `applyTrackedPosition` into a single delegation to `collectCoordinateParamRefs`. The runtime closure additionally pushes/pops `currentNode` so `NAMED_COORD` / `EXTRA_POINT_REF` resolution finds the right coordinate-system parent chain on re-fire. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testIncrementalLayoutWithParamIndex`) — three layout points, `setParameter("idx", N)` for N=1, 2, asserts the bitmap reaches each point in turn.

- **`ParticleGroup.init` non-relative branch derived particle scale and rotation from matrix rows instead of columns** — the bake formulas `scX = √(matA² + matC²)` / `scY = √(matB² + matD²)` and `absRot = atan2(matB / scY, matA / scX)` happen to coincide with the correct values for pure rotation or uniform scale, which is why no test caught it, but they diverge as soon as the `Particles` container (or any ancestor) carries non-uniform scale. For h2d's affine layout (column 1 = `(matA, matB)` is the world image of the local x-axis, column 2 = `(matC, matD)` is the world image of the local y-axis), the axis lengths are `√(matA² + matB²)` and `√(matC² + matD²)` — column norms. With `scaleX=2, scaleY=0.5, rotation=π/3` the row formulas give `scX≈1.09, scY≈1.75` (swapped and wrong relative to the intended `2.0` / `0.5`) and rotation `≈ 0.82 rad` instead of `π/3 ≈ 1.047`. The visible symptom was non-relative trail particles emitting at the wrong size and rotation under non-uniform container scale (camera with separate horizontal/vertical zoom, stretched HUD layer, cinematic squash). Fix substitutes column norms in both scale and rotation derivations and uses the column-1 norm consistently for both `atan2` arguments; the existing per-particle `size` multiplier is preserved. The corrected derivation is now also used for the `worldAnchor` opt-in path (it operates on the same effective transform). One regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testNonRelativeBakedScaleAndRotationUseContainerAxisLengths`) pins the corrected formulas at the `scaleX=2, scaleY=0.5, rotation=π/3` test case, where row and column formulas disagree on all three values.
- **`Particles.sync`/`draw` and `UIScreenBase.getElements` allocated on every frame** — two related per-frame allocation hotspots surfaced by a downstream alloc capture (proto-game `ShmupCombatScreen`): together ~10 allocations / ~1.5 KB per frame on an idle scene with one Particles instance and one active controller. (1) `Particles.sync()` runs once and `Particles.draw()` runs twice per frame; each iterated `groups:Map<String, ParticleGroup>` directly via `for (g in groups)`. On HL each `for`-in over a Map allocates a fresh hashmap iterator (backing array + iterator struct + iterator object) — ~3 allocs × 3 hot loops = ~9 allocs/frame per Particles instance even when nothing is happening. (2) `UIDefaultController.update` called `integration.getElements(SETReceiveUpdates)` every frame, which `elements.copy()`s the Array and `concat`s every provider's sub-element list — at minimum one array allocation per frame, more whenever a sub-element provider (Dropdown, Tabs, RadioButtons) was active (each `getSubElements` builds a fresh Array). The same defensive-copy hit `getEventElements` per dispatched event. Fix, three parts: (1) `Particles` keeps a parallel `groupList:Array<ParticleGroup>` synced from `addGroup`/`removeGroup` and iterates it in all six hot/cold paths (`sync`, `draw` ×2, `setRandomFunc`, `shutdown`, `advanceTime`); the Map remains for O(1) `getGroup(id)` lookup. `getGroups()` now returns `groupList.iterator()` — insertion order, where it was previously the undefined `Map.iterator()` order. (2) New `UIElementSubElements.forEachSubElement(type, fn)` interface method with alloc-free implementations on `UIMultiAnimDropdown` / `UIMultiAnimTabs` / `UIMultiAnimRadioButtons`; `UIScreen.forEachElement(type, fn)` (and matching `UIControllerScreenIntegration.forEachElement`) streams elements + provider sub-elements through the callback without building an intermediate Array. `getElements` stays for external callers. (3) `UIDefaultController.update` caches its update callback in a `final _updateCallback` field (with state threaded through a `_updateDt` field) so the per-frame call does not allocate a fresh closure on HL; `getEventElements` also routes through `forEachElement`. Three regression tests: `ParticleRuntimeTest.testAddGroupAppendsToGroupList` and `testRemoveGroupRemovesFromGroupListPreservingOrder` pin the `groupList` mirror contract via `@:access(bh.base.Particles)`; `EventPriorityTest.testIntegrationForEachElementVisitsAllElementsInOrder` pins the new `forEachElement` callback contract through `MockIntegration`.
- **`ParticleGroup` with an `attachedPath` advanced the path while the group was disabled** — `start()` and `draw()` already short-circuit on `enabled == false`, but `updateTime(dt)` (which `Particles.sync()` calls every frame regardless of visibility) ignored the flag and ran `attachedPath.update(dt)` unconditionally. Side effects: the path's `rate` advanced past disabled spans, timed `event(name)` callbacks fired (so `pathStart` / mid-rate user events were observed by listeners that thought the group was inert), and any `spawnCurve` accumulated emission credit was paid back as a burst the moment the group flipped enabled. Fix: gate the `attachedPath != null` arm on `enabled` so the path stalls in lock-step with start/draw — re-enabling the group resumes the path from its frozen rate. One regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testAttachedPathDoesNotAdvanceWhenGroupDisabled`) attaches a 1-second time-mode path with a mid-rate event, disables the group, ticks `updateTime(0.5)` past the event, and asserts both `getState().rate == 0` and zero events fired.
- **`TweenSequence.finish()` and `TweenGroup.finish()` ignored the `cancelled` flag, firing `onComplete` callbacks on cancelled tweens and writing their final values** — both `finish()` methods unconditionally walked their child tweens, called `tween.init()` + `tween.finish()` (which sets every tracked property to its target), and fired `tween.onComplete()`, regardless of whether the container or any individual child had been cancelled. This violated the documented contract "Cancelled tweens do not fire onComplete" (`.claude/rules/runtime-systems.md`). Production callers like the `closeNamed` interrupt path (`UIPanelHelper.closeNamed` calls `prevFadeOut.finish()` to flush a still-running fade-out) and `ScreenManager.finalizeTransition()` are not directly hit today, but anyone holding a `TweenSequence`/`TweenGroup` reference and calling `cancel()` then `finish()` (the natural takeover pattern) saw spurious callbacks fire and properties jumped past the cancellation point. The bug compounded with `TweenSequence.cancel()` only marking the *current* tween as cancelled — queued tweens stayed alive and would fully run if `finish()` was later invoked. Fix, three parts: (1) `TweenSequence.cancel()` now propagates to all queued tweens, mirroring `TweenGroup.cancel()`'s contract; (2) `TweenSequence.finish()` early-returns on `cancelled` and skips per-iteration tweens that were individually cancelled (handles the `cancelAll(target)` selective-cancel path that leaves the sequence alive); (3) `TweenGroup.finish()` early-returns on `cancelled` and skips per-tween cancelled (same per-tween rationale). Eight regression tests in `TweenManagerTest`: `testCancelledSequenceFinishDoesNotFireSequenceOnComplete`, `testCancelledSequenceFinishDoesNotFirePerTweenOnComplete`, `testSequenceFinishSkipsIndividuallyCancelledCurrentTween`, `testCancelledGroupFinishDoesNotFireGroupOnComplete`, `testCancelledGroupFinishDoesNotFirePerTweenOnComplete`, `testGroupFinishSkipsIndividuallyCancelledTween`, `testSequenceCancelMarksAllQueuedTweensAsCancelled`, `testSequenceFinishAfterCancelDoesNotFirePerTweenCallbacks`, `testSequenceFinishAfterCancelDoesNotResumeQueuedTweenSideEffects`.
- **`UICardHandHelper` started drag on the wrong card when the hovered card had popped up out from under the cursor** — hover detection (`onMouseMove` → `getCardAtBasePosition`) intentionally uses the base layout (no hover pop) so the popped card cannot mask its neighbors during hit-test, but Heaps routes `UIPush` to whichever `Interactive` physically contains the cursor — i.e. post-pop positions. With overlapping fan/linear layouts and a sufficient `hoverPop`, the cursor that picked card A by base layout sat in A's old shadow over B's `Interactive` after the pop animation; the helper trusted the routed id and called `startDragFromInteractive(B)`, so the visually-highlighted card stayed in place while a neighbor was dragged. Click and hover hit-test must agree. Fix: the `UIPush` arm now anchors to `hoveredEntry` when one is set (the card hover already selected, by the same base-layout hit-test as `getCardAtBasePosition`), falling back to the routed `entry` only when nothing is hovered (cursor outside the hand). Two regression tests in `CardHandIntegrationTest`: `testUIPushOnNeighborInteractiveStartsDragOnHoveredCard` (force-hover A, fire `UIPush` on B's interactive id, assert drag follows A and `isDragging` is true), `testUIPushFallsBackToInteractiveEntryWhenNothingHovered` (no hover state, assert routed entry still drags as before).
- **`UIMultiAnimGrid.cellAtPoint` / `sceneToHex` / `hitTestHex` allocated a fresh `h2d.col.Point` (and on hex grids an `FPoint`) on every call** — the same hover-hot-path shape as the prior `UICardHandHelper.getCardAtBasePosition` regression. Every `onMouseMove` over a grid burned at least one `Point` (rect grids) or one `Point` + one `FPoint` (hex grids); on a hex grid those allocations cascaded into `pixelToHex` → `FractionalHex` → `.round()` → `Hex` per call. `globalToLocal` mutates its argument in place, so the fix is the same scratch pattern used elsewhere. Fix: new static `_scratchPoint:h2d.col.Point` and `_scratchFPoint:FPoint` on `UIMultiAnimGrid` (HL is single-threaded so cross-instance sharing is safe). `cellAtPoint`, `sceneToHex`, and `hitTestHex` all reuse them — `Point`/`FPoint` allocation per hover hit-test drops from 1–2 to 0. The downstream `FractionalHex` + `Hex` allocations from `pixelToHex` + `.round()` remain — they need `pixelToHexInto`/`roundInto` variants which would touch codegen, so they're now instrumented (see Added) and pinned by tests for later removal. Two regression tests in `UIMultiAnimGridTest`: `testCellAtPointRectReusesScratchPointAcrossCalls` and `testCellAtPointHexReusesScratchPointAcrossCalls` — warm + 10 follow-up `cellAtPoint` calls assert `FPoint.creationCount` delta is 0.

- **`setParameter` on an unknown param name silently no-opped on the incremental BuilderResult path, diverging from initial-build and codegen** — the three setParameter entry points had inconsistent contracts: `MultiAnimBuilder.buildWithParameters` → `updateIndexedParamsFromDynamicMap` always threw `'Setting "X" does not match any parameter of programmable...'`; `ProgrammableCodeGen`-generated typed dispatchers always threw `'unknown parameter "X" on <className>'`; only `IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` returned silently when `getParamDefinition(name) == null`. The silent no-op was added to support UI widgets (Button/Checkbox/Slider/Tabs/TextInput) that fire `setParameter("disabled", ...)` against any client template — but it also masked genuine user errors (typos, renamed params, helpers wired to the wrong target). Fix, two parts: (1) `IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` now throws `BuilderError` with `code="unknown_param"` and an actionable message listing every available param name, symmetric with the other two paths. (2) New `BuilderResult.hasParameter(name):Bool` and matching codegen `instance.hasParameter(name):Bool` (added to the `UIInteractiveSource` interface so widgets can query both runtime and codegen targets uniformly); the codegen variant emits a compile-time switch over the declared param names. (3) The five standard UI widgets (`UIMultiAnimButton`, `UIMultiAnimCheckbox`, `UIMultiAnimSlider`, `UIMultiAnimTabs`, `UIMultiAnimTextInput`) now gate their opportunistic `setParameter("disabled", ...)` call on `result.hasParameter("disabled")` — templates that only model `status:[normal,hover,disabled]` (the cookbook default) keep working without declaring a parallel `disabled:bool`. The `setParameter("status", ...)` call is unconditional: every standard widget template declares `status`, and a missing `status` param is a real user error that should surface. Existing test `BuilderUnitTest.testSetParameterUnknownParamIsSilentNoOp` renamed to `testSetParameterUnknownParamThrows` and inverted to assert the new contract (`BuilderError.code == "unknown_param"`, no rebuild listener fire, follow-up valid setParameter still works).
- **Codegen `setParameter`'s `PPTColor` case rejected the same String formats the runtime path accepts** — calls like `setParameter("outlineColor", "red")` / `"#FF0000"` / `"#f00"` / `"#FF0000FF"` / `"0xFFFF0000"` accepted at runtime via `MultiAnimParser.tryStringToColor` crashed at codegen because the dispatcher's `PPTColor` branch only routed through `Std.parseInt`, which returns `null` for everything except plain decimal strings. Fix: the codegen path now resolves String values through `tryStringToColor` first, falling back to `Std.parseInt` for plain decimal strings; raw `Int`/`Float` values are unchanged. One regression test in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` (`testCodegenSetParameterAcceptsColorStringFormats`) sweeps named colors, 3-/6-/8-digit `#` shorthand, and `0x...` literals against a `:color` param.
- **`MultiAnimBuilder.buildWithParameters` leaked `stateStack` entries when an inner build threw and the caller swallowed the error** — `pushBuilderState()` plus `buildingRefs.push(name)` ran before the try block; the catch only unwound `buildingRefs` (and only when the top-of-stack matched), leaving every other transient field (`indexedParams`, `builderParams`, `currentNode`, `incrementalContext`, `programmableNode`) pinned to the failed call's state. The "missing node" early throw was even worse — its inline `popBuilderState()` only ran on the precise missing-node path, not on any other inner throw. `DevBridge.eval_manim` and `ScreenManager` hot-reload both follow the per-node-loop / catch-and-continue pattern that this hits; every per-node failure corrupted every later build on the same builder, with subsequent builds producing nodes against the failed call's stale `indexedParams`. Fix: removed the inline `popBuilderState()` from the missing-node throw; the catch now unconditionally calls `popBuilderState()` and `buildingRefs.pop()` so both stacks balance on every exit. Two regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testBuildWithParametersRestoresStateStackOnInnerThrow` (catches the missing-`staticRef` throw, asserts both stacks are empty), `testBuildWithParametersStateStackBalancedAfterCaughtErrorThenSecondBuild` (mirrors the eval_manim / hot-reload flow — bad build then good build on the same builder, both must end with empty stacks).
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.rebuildDynamicNameRef` did not maintain `dynamicRefCollisions` and skipped most bookkeeping cleanup** — when a `dynamicRef($template)` site renamed its target via `setParameter`, the rebuild path filtered `dynamicRefBindings` for the old child but left every other per-element bookkeeping array (`conditionalEntries`, `conditionalApplyEntries`, `deferredEntries`, `trackedExpressions`, `dynamicNameBindings`, `activeTransitionTweens`) and IR registration (`interactives`, `slots`, `names`, `htmlTextsWithLinks`) of the old subtree intact. Compounded across template cycles these arrays grew unboundedly. Worse, the collision counter for the OLD key was never decremented and the NEW key never incremented, so renaming an unnamed site OUT of a colliding key left `getDynamicRef(oldKey)` throwing the collision error forever, and renaming INTO a key that already had a writer silently last-writer-wins instead of raising the documented "use #name to disambiguate" error. Fix, two parts: (1) `rebuildDynamicNameRef` now calls `cleanupDestroyedSubtree(internalResults, container.getChildAt(0))` on the OLD child before `removeChildren()`, mirroring the SWITCH/REPEAT/materializeDeferred cleanup pattern (targets the child, not the container, so the binding's own slot is preserved); (2) for unnamed sites it decrements `dynamicRefCollisions` for the old key (removing the entry when the count would drop to 1, preserving the "key present ↔ currently colliding" invariant), and increments for the new key when another writer is already there. Three regression tests in `DynamicRefTest` / `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalDynamicNameRefBookkeepingCleanedUp` (snapshots `dynamicRefBindings`/`dynamicNameBindings`/`conditionalEntries`/`trackedExpressions` counts, cycles three template names 5× through a host with inner conditionals + interpolation + nested `dynamicRef`s, asserts no leak), `testDynamicNameRefRenameOutOfCollisionDecrementsCount` (two unnamed sites collide on `templateA`, rename one to `templateB`, assert collision throw goes away), `testDynamicNameRefRenameIntoCollisionIncrementsCount` (siblings start non-colliding, rename one into the other's key, assert `getDynamicRef` now throws collision error).
- **`SlotHandle` `setContent` / `clear` / `getContent` / `isEmpty` / `isOccupied` did not throw after slot disposal** — only `setParameter` had the disposed guard; the other five methods silently mutated or read state on an orphaned subtree (whose IR entries were already evicted by `removeRegistrationsUnder`). `setContent(obj)` on a disposed handle attached `obj` to a parentless container — never rendered, no error. Read methods returned stale state from the torn-down subtree. Fix: every public method now checks `disposed` and throws `BuilderError` with `code="slot_disposed"`, matching the `setParameter` shape. One regression test in `ParameterizedSlotTest` (`testSlotMethodsThrowAfterArmSwap`) — captures a `SlotHandle` from an active `@switch` arm, swaps to the inactive arm, verifies all five methods throw with the right code.
- **`Particles.removeGroup` did not drain the `ParticleGroup.freeParticles` pool** — when a sub-emitter chain or game-side handle captured a `ParticleGroup` reference and the parent then called `removeGroup(id)`, the live `Particle` instances were correctly destroyed but the free-list pool (which can grow to N=`life * spawnRate` entries during normal emission) stayed populated. The captured reference therefore pinned every pooled Particle for the lifetime of the capture — a slow leak that scaled with peak emission rate × group lifetime. Fix: `removeGroup` now calls `g.freeParticles.resize(0)` alongside `batch.clear()` + `batch.remove()`. One regression test in `ParticleRuntimeTest` (`testRemoveGroupDrainsFreeParticlePool`) — emits a burst, advances past life so all particles recycle into the pool, calls `removeGroup`, asserts `freeParticles.length == 0` afterwards.
- **`UICardHandHelper.update()` and `removeAnimationsForEntry` could null-deref or skip animations when an `onComplete` handler re-entered the helper** — both paths iterate `activeAnimations` backwards with `splice(i, 1)` then call `displaced.onComplete()` synchronously inside the loop. The `onComplete` is the user-supplied `onCardEvent` or the helper's own draw/discard cleanup, both of which can mutate `activeAnimations`: `setHand([])` from a `DiscardAnimComplete` handler routes through `clearHand()` which reassigns `activeAnimations = []`; another `discardCard()` from inside the handler appends; `applyLayout(true)` from draw cleanup ultimately calls `removeAnimationsForEntry` again. The outer iteration's `i--` then either dereferenced an out-of-bounds index on the now-empty array (null deref on the next `activeAnimations[i].entry` read) or skipped an unprocessed entry. `clearHand` already snapshots-then-fires for this exact reason; that guarantee now extends to `update(dt)` and `removeAnimationsForEntry` too. Fix: both paths collect completed/displaced animations into a local list during the iteration and fire each `onComplete` only after the outer loop returns. One regression test in `CardHandIntegrationTest` (`testRemoveAnimationsForEntryHandlesHandResetFromOnComplete`) — discards three cards, hooks the handler so `DiscardAnimComplete("c")` calls `setHand([])`, triggers the discard via `animateCardTo`, asserts every card's `DiscardAnimComplete` fires (cascading clearHand drains A and B), and asserts only the freshly-pushed C animation remains.
- **`UICardHandHelper.getCardAtBasePosition` allocated `Array<CardLayoutPosition>` plus N `CardLayoutPosition` instances on every mouse-move** — hover detection runs through `computeLayout(-1)` → `UICardHandLayout.computeFan/Linear/PathLayout`, all of which return a freshly allocated array of structInit class instances. The positions are read once for hit-test math and discarded. With `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK` the watchdog now catches it; in production the allocation cascaded through GC pressure on every cursor movement above the hand. Fix: new `Into` variants in `UICardHandLayout` (`computeFanLayoutInto`, `computeLinearLayoutInto`, `computePathLayoutInto`) take a caller-owned buffer, grow it once, and overwrite entries in place via a `writePos` helper. The non-`Into` variants now delegate to `Into` with a fresh array (no behavior change for external callers). `UICardHandHelper` adds a static `_scratchPositions:Array<CardLayoutPosition>` (single-threaded HL, same pattern as `UICardHandLayout._scratchRates`); `getCardAtBasePosition` calls a new private `computeLayoutInto(-1, _scratchPositions)` — first call allocates, subsequent calls reuse. `CardLayoutPosition` gains an explicit constructor (was `@:structInit`-only) so the buffer can be primed with `new CardLayoutPosition()`; the construction-count watchdog (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`) lives on the new ctor. One regression test in `CardHandIntegrationTest` (`testGetCardIdAtPositionReusesLayoutPositionBufferAcrossCalls`) — warms the buffer with one call, asserts 0 fresh `CardLayoutPosition` allocations across 10 follow-up `getCardIdAtPosition` calls on a 5-card hand.
- **`MacroManimParser` did not add `repeatable2d` X/Y iterator output vars to the body's scope** — `repeatable2d($x, $y, tiles($bmp, "atlas", "Arrow_dir0"), range(0, 3)) { bitmap($bmp): ... }` validated the loop vars `$x`/`$y` but never pushed iterator-produced output vars (`$bmp` from a `tiles()` X-side, `$val` from an `array()` Y-side, etc.) onto `scopeVars`. Any reference to those vars inside the body raised a parse error claiming the var was undefined, even though the same shape works on 1D `repeatable`. Root cause: the REPEAT2D arm of the scope-walker fell through to `default:` after pushing only the loop vars themselves; the 1D REPEAT arm explicitly walks each iterator type's output vars. Fix: REPEAT2D arm now mirrors REPEAT — calls the existing `iteratorOutputVars(repeatType)` helper for both X and Y iterator types and pushes each var onto `scopeVars` (with matching `loopVarsToPop` accounting). Two regression tests in `ParserErrorTest`: `testRepeatable2dIteratorOutputVarIsValid` (X-side `tiles()` → `$bmp` referenced in body), `testRepeatable2dIteratorOutputVarFromYIsValid` (Y-side `array()` → `$val` referenced in body).
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.getRelevantParamRefsForNode` allocated `StringMap<Bool> + Array<String>` on every call** — `findTransitionSpec` calls this method to determine which params drive a node's visibility, on every visibility re-evaluation triggered by `setParameter`. The result is fully derived from the node's `conditionals` + the `@()` / `@else` chain among preceding siblings — both fixed once parsing is done — yet the method recomputed and re-allocated unconditionally. On a transition-bearing programmable with N conditional siblings, every UI hover/press fired N StringMap creations + N small array allocations, all immediately garbage. Fix: `Node` typedef gains a lazily-populated `cachedRelevantParamRefs:Null<Array<String>>` field (initialized to `null` at parse time, both in `MultiAnimParser.parseFile` and the `MacroManimParser` literal Node construction). `getRelevantParamRefsForNode` returns the cached array when present, otherwise computes once and caches. New `IncrementalUpdateContext.paramRefsRebuildCount` counter (sibling of `conditionalMapRebuildCount`) for cache-miss instrumentation. Cache invalidation is tree-level — a re-parse on hot reload produces a fresh tree with fresh empty caches, no per-node bookkeeping needed. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testIncrementalCachesParamRefsForTransitions`): warms by cycling every level once on a `level:int=0` programmable with 4 conditional arms and a `transition`, snapshots `paramRefsRebuildCount`, then asserts 12 follow-up `setParameter` calls leave the counter unchanged.
- **Codegen `_applyVisibility` rebuilt every `@switch` arm on every `setParameter` call** — `ProgrammableCodeGen` pushed each switch's lazy update block into `_applyVisibility` unconditionally; setters always ran the block once their value-changed gate let them through. Inside `MultiAnimBuilder.rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal` this triggers `removeRegistrationsUnder` + `container.removeChildren()` + a full arm rebuild — and arm children build with `incrementalMode=false`, so any stateanim/particle group/interactive registration inside the arm was destroyed and re-seeded. Repro: a `card` programmable with `disabled:bool=false` and `@switch(rarity)`, `card.setParameter("disabled", true)` reset particles inside the rarity arm to frame 0 and dropped interactive registrations declared in the arm. The runtime path already gated this correctly via `trackExpression(..., paramRefs)` over the union of refs across all arms; the codegen path had no such gate. Fix: at codegen time, walk each switch's arm subtrees collecting the same union (switch param + arm conditional keys/values + RV refs in node payloads + DYNAMIC_REF/STATIC_REF/INTERACTIVE/STATEANIM/STATEANIM_CONSTRUCT payload refs + nested `@switch` controlling params), thread the set through `switchUpdateEntries`, and emit `_applyVisibility` switch entries as `if (_changedParam == null || _changedParam == ref1 || ... || _changedParam == refN) { rebuildSwitchArm(...) }`. `_applyVisibility(?_changedParam)` is now generated whenever switches OR transitions exist; setters always pass the changed name. Constructor's `_applyVisibility()` (no arg) still runs every switch as the initial-build path. Transition entries gain a parallel compile-time check (`_changedParam == "modeParam" || _changedParam == "statusParam" || ...`) so non-transition setters — now that every setter forwards `_changedParam` — cannot accidentally invoke `setPresenceWithTransition` with a non-transition param name and cancel an in-flight transition. Regression test in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest`: `testCodegenSwitchArmNotRebuiltOnUnrelatedParam` builds `switchUnrelatedParam` (params `rarity:[common,rare]=common`, `disabled:bool=false`; arm `common` declares `#core bitmap(...)`), captures `getUpdatable("core")`, calls `setDisabled(true)`, asserts the same h2d.Object reference, then asserts `setRarity(1)` produces a different reference (sanity — arm swaps still rebuild).
- **Allocation watchdog counters incremented on every construction in production builds** — three test-only counters (`FPoint.creationCount`, `CardLayoutPosition.creationCount`, `UICardHandLayout.scratchArrayAllocationCount`) were declared and incremented unconditionally, despite docstrings claiming they were "for hot-path instrumentation in tests". `FPoint` and `CardLayoutPosition` constructors are `inline`, so every construction site emitted a static-int increment in production — and these types sit on hot paths (per-particle update, animated path sampling, hex/grid math, layout passes, hover hit-test). Fix: gated the field declarations and increments behind a new `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK` build flag so production builds skip the work entirely. `-D MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK` added to `test-common.hxml` so the existing watchdog assertions in `CardHandIntegrationTest` and `CardHandOrchestratorTest` keep working. New regression test `CardHandIntegrationTest.testAllocationTrackingFlagIsDefinedInTestBuilds` documents the contract — fails compile/runtime if the flag is dropped from the test config so the next reader gets a clear remediation message instead of a confusing "unknown identifier" cascade across five test sites.
- **`UICardHandHelper` stranded `DrawAnimComplete` / `DiscardAnimComplete` events when in-flight animations were cancelled or interrupted** — three loss paths converged on the same root cause: dropping an `ActiveAnimation` from `activeAnimations` without firing its `onComplete`. (1) `clearHand()` (and therefore `setHand([])`, `dispose()`, hot-reload teardown, and the "card played" cleanup) zeroed `activeAnimations = []` directly — any card that was still mid-draw or mid-discard never received its completion event, so game-side state machines (`await DiscardAnimComplete → next phase`) stalled and the discarded card's container leaked into the scene graph because container removal lives inside the discard's `onComplete`. (2) `removeAnimationsForEntry(entry)` — called from every `animateCardTo` / `animateCardToTracking` site to displace any prior animation on the same `CardEntry` — silently spliced the previous animation out without firing its `onComplete`, so a `discardCard("a")` interrupting an in-flight `drawCard("a")` (or vice versa) lost the displaced animation's completion event AND its scene-graph cleanup. (3) Transition tweens (e.g. `transition { status: fade(0.5) }` on a card programmable) were never cancelled on `clearHand()`, so a screen rebuild could leave fade-out closures ticking against detached card subtrees. Fix: (a) `clearHand()` now snapshots `activeAnimations`, fires each entry's `onComplete()` before tearing down cards, then re-resets the list (defensive — `onComplete` can re-enter via `applyLayout`); (b) `removeAnimationsForEntry` fires the displaced animation's `onComplete` after the splice; (c) `clearHand()` calls `tweenManager.cancelAllChildren(handContainer)` and `cancelAllChildren(dragContainer)` before card containers are detached (`cancelAllChildren` walks the parent chain, so order matters). Five regression tests in `CardHandIntegrationTest`: `testClearingHandMidDiscardAnimationStillEmitsDiscardAnimComplete`, `testClearingHandMidDrawAnimationStillEmitsDrawAnimComplete`, `testDisposeMidDiscardAnimationStillEmitsDiscardAnimComplete`, `testDrawAnimCompleteFiresWhenInterruptedByDiscardOnSameCard`, `testDiscardCleanupRunsWhenAnimateCardToReplacesInFlightDiscard` — plus `testDisposeCancelsCardTransitionTweens` for the transition-tween leak.
- **Codegen `palette(external(ref), …)` always resolved against the local builder** — `RVColorXY(externalReference, name, x, y)` and `RVColor(externalReference, name, index)` are emitted by the parser when a `.manim` file references an imported palette via `palette(external(extRef), name, …)`. The runtime path (`MultiAnimBuilder.resolveAsColorInteger`) honors `externalReference` by switching builders through `getBuilderWithExternal(...)` before the palette lookup, but `ProgrammableCodeGen` dropped the `externalReference` field on the floor and emitted `_pb.getPaletteColor2D(name, x, y)` against the local builder. When the palette was only declared in the imported file, every `@:manim`-compiled `create()` threw "could not get palette node" — runtime tests hid the bug because they never went through codegen. Fix, two parts: (1) `ProgrammableBuilder.getPaletteColor2D` / `getPaletteColorByIndex` gain an optional `externalRef:String` parameter that resolves through `multiParserResult.imports.get(externalRef)` before the palette lookup, throwing a structured `BuilderError` if the import isn't registered; (2) `ProgrammableCodeGen.rvToExpr` for `RVColorXY` / `RVColor` threads `externalReference` (when non-null) into the generated call. Visual fixture `test/examples/115-codegenExternalPalette` (importer + `extPalette.manim` defining `#tones palette { … }`) plus two regression tests in new `CodegenExternalPaletteTest`: `testRuntimeBuildExternalPalette` (sanity, asserts the runtime path still works) and `testCodegenBuildExternalPalette` (asserts `mp.codegenExternalPalette.create()` no longer throws and produces a non-empty subtree).
- **Codegen `setParameter` generic dispatcher threw on unknown enum string values, diverging from the runtime contract** — `MultiAnimBuilder.setParameter` is documented (and tested) to silently no-op when a string value isn't in the enum's value list — UI widgets like Button/Checkbox/Tabs unconditionally call `setParameter("status", "disabled")` on every client template, expecting templates whose `status` enum doesn't list `"disabled"` to leave all `@(status=>…)` arms unmatched while a parallel `disabled:bool` param drives the visual. The codegen path (8a3 in `ProgrammableCodeGen`) instead emitted `throw 'unknown enum value "$_value" for parameter "..."'` as the default arm of the string→index switch, so the first call to `setParameter("status", "disabled")` on any `@:manim`-compiled card or button crashed at runtime. Fix: the default arm now resolves to `-1` (mirroring the runtime fast path's `StringValue(s)` shape — no positive match against any `CoIndex` predicate). One regression test in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest` (`testCodegenSetParameterUnknownEnumValueDoesNotThrow`) builds a `codegenButton` (status enum: `[hover, pressed, normal]` — no `"disabled"`), calls `setParameter("status", "disabled")` and asserts no throw, then asserts a follow-up `setParameter("status", "hover")` still works.
- **Runtime `IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` did not short-circuit on no-op writes — listeners and tracked expressions fired even when the value didn't change** — the codegen typed setter (`ProgrammableCodeGen` field-level `setX` methods) already short-circuits when the new value equals the cached field. The runtime path commits unconditionally: `indexedParams.set(name, converted); changedParams.set(name, true); hasChanges = true;` then calls `applyUpdates()`, which fires `rebuildListeners`, re-evaluates `trackedExpressions`, and forwards into every `dynamicRefBindings` entry. Naive callers — UI state machines that don't track their own previous value, hover trackers that re-assert `setParameter("status", "hover")` per frame, slider widgets that re-emit the same value mid-drag — thrashed listeners and re-eval on the hot path with no observable effect. Fix: an O(1) gate after value conversion compares `Type.enumEq(existing, converted)` (cheap, no allocation, works across all `ResolvedIndexParameters` variants) and returns before flag-flip. Inside a `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate` batch, no-op writes for a name correctly remain no-ops without disturbing other params already flagged. Two regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testSetParameterNoOpDoesNotFireRebuildListener` (asserts a `result.addRebuildListener` callback fires on real changes but stays silent across 5 same-value calls), `testSetParameterNoOpDoesNotReEvaluateTrackedExpressions` (same shape with `bitmap(generated(color($level + 1, …)))` — tracked expression must not rerun on no-op writes).
- **Parameterized slot `IncrementalUpdateContext` did not inherit the builder's `TweenManager`** — `MultiAnimBuilder.buildWithParameters` already injects `tweenManager` into the programmable's `IncrementalUpdateContext` so transitions and tween-driven visibility flips work, but the two slot construction sites (`MultiAnimBuilder.build`'s `case SLOT(parameters, _)` arm at L5990 and `MultiAnimBuilder.buildSlotContent` at L7562) constructed their own contexts without the same injection. Future parser support for `transition {}` inside slot bodies — or any other code path that reads `slot.incrementalContext.tweenManager` — silently no-ops because the field is null. The non-slot programmable path doesn't have this gap. Fix: both slot context constructions now copy `tweenManager` (when non-null) onto the new context via `setTweenManager`. Two hygiene tests in `ParameterizedSlotTest`: `testParameterizedSlotInheritsTweenManagerFromBuilder` (asserts `slot.incrementalContext.tweenManager == builder.tweenManager` for the SLOT path), `testCodegenBuildSlotContentInheritsTweenManagerFromBuilder` (same assertion for the `buildSlotContent` path).
- **Deferred conditional bodies leaked IR registrations (interactives / slots / dynamicRefs / names / htmlTextsWithLinks) across every hide↔show cycle and across every tracked-expression rebuild while visible** — `IncrementalUpdateContext.materializeDeferred` and the tracked-expression closure that fires on inner-param change both called `entry.wrapper.removeChildren()` and re-built the body, but neither dropped the previous build's `BuilderResult` registrations. The IR collections (`interactives`, `slots`, `dynamicRefs`, `names`, `htmlTextsWithLinks`) accumulated stale entries on every flip — `screen.getInteractive("hit")` could return a wrapper whose Heaps `Interactive` had no parent, or `result.slots.length` could grow to >1 for a single declared `#name slot`. Both rebuild paths now call `MultiAnimBuilder.removeRegistrationsUnder(internalResults, wrapper)` BEFORE `removeChildren()`, mirroring the cleanup already performed by SWITCH/REPEAT structural rebuilds. Three regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalDeferredConditionalInteractiveCleanedUpOnReshow` (Path 2: no-tracked-param body, re-materialize on each visibility flip), `testIncrementalDeferredConditionalTrackedRebuildInteractiveCleanedUp` (Path 1: text interpolating `$label` inside the deferred body, tracked-expression closure rebuilds on `setParameter("label", …)` while visible), `testIncrementalDeferredConditionalSlotCleanedUpOnReshow` (slot in deferred body, asserts `result.slots.length == 1` across 5 hide→show cycles).
- **`dynamicRef` forwarding fired the child's `applyUpdates` once per forwarded binding instead of batching into a single update** — when the parent batched two `setParameter` calls (`beginUpdate`/`endUpdate`) and both forwarded into the same child `dynamicRef`, the parent's apply loop iterated `dynamicRefBindings` and called `binding.childContext.setParameter(...)` per binding without an enclosing `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate`. The child re-evaluated conditionals, transitions, and rebuild listeners after each forwarded write — so a strict child conditional like `@(p1=>X, p2=>Y)` saw new `p1` against stale `p2` on the first pass and could fire a spurious arm flip (and, with transitions wired, a visible flash). The codegen path already wrapped forwarded-param updates in `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate`; the runtime builder did not. Fix groups bindings by `childContext` into a temporary `forwardGroups` list, then dispatches each group inside one `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate` pair so the child `applyUpdates` runs exactly once with all new values present. Lazy allocation: the list is only created when forwarding actually happens. Two regression tests in `DynamicRefTest`: `testDynamicRefForwardingBatchesAcrossMultipleParams` (asserts the child's `addRebuildListener` fires exactly once per parent batch over two forwarded params), `testDynamicRefForwardingPreservesStrictConditionalEndState` (asserts the strict `@(p1=>X, p2=>Y)` conditional lands on `@else` after a batched parent flip and the child rebuilds exactly once).
- **`FloatingTextHelper.update()` lost reentrantly-spawned instances when two finished in the same frame** — the `pop()` micro-optimization landed earlier in rc.5 (see Changed entry below) only works while `instances.length == count + 1` holds across the whole loop iteration. `inst.onComplete()` is fired AFTER the swap-remove and may grow the array (`spawn()` reentrantly), breaking the invariant. On the next iteration's swap-remove the buggy `pop()` removes the just-spawned tail instead of the swapped-in `instances[count]`, dropping the new instance from the helper while leaving its scene object orphaned on the parent. The two earlier regression tests (`testOnCompleteCanCallClear`, `testOnCompleteCanSpawn`) only covered single-instance arrays and didn't expose the bug. Fix reverts to `instances.splice(count, 1)`. New regression test `FloatingTextHelperTest.testReentrantSpawnSurvivesAdjacentCompletion` spawns A and B, has A's `onComplete` spawn A2, advances past both lifetimes in one update, then probes with a follow-up update to assert the surviving instance is A2 (not B), B's object is detached, and A2's object remains parented.
- **`UITooltipHelper` fade tweens outlived screen teardown** — the rc.5 dispose-on-clear fix added `dispose()` to both `UIPanelHelper` and `UITooltipHelper`, but `UIScreenBase.clear()` only iterated `panelHelpers`. Tooltip helpers had no registry and were never auto-disposed, so the same fade-out closure footprint (`fadeOutTween.setOnComplete(() -> obj.remove())` capturing the tooltip `h2d.Object`) re-introduced the bug the entry below claims to fix — only for the panel half. Fix mirrors the panel plumbing: `UIScreenBase` gains `tooltipHelpers:Array<UITooltipHelper>` + `registerTooltipHelper`/`unregisterTooltipHelper`; `clear()` disposes each registered tooltip helper. `UITooltipHelper`'s constructor auto-registers when its host is a `UIScreenBase` (no call-site changes — UICardHandHelper-style). Existing manual `dispose()` calls remain idempotent. New regression test `UITooltipHelperTest.testScreenClearDisposesActiveTooltip` constructs a helper with fade, shows a tooltip, calls `screen.clear()`, asserts the in-flight fade tween is cancelled.
- **`Updatable.setObject` after multiple `addObject` calls left earlier children parented to the slot** — `Updatable` (in `MultiAnimBuilder`) and `ProgrammableUpdatable` both tracked only `lastObject:Null<h2d.Object>`, the most recent object attached via `setObject`/`addObject`. A `setObject(c)` call after `addObject(a)` + `addObject(b)` detached `b` (because `lastObject == b`) but left `a` orphaned — still a child of the updatable's scene parent, rendering under `c` until the enclosing programmable was fully torn down. Fix: replace `lastObject` with `addedObjects:Array<h2d.Object>` on both classes; `setObject` now detaches every tracked child, not just the most recent, before adding the new one. `clearObjects` resizes the array to 0. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testUpdatableSetObjectAfterAddObjectsDetachesAllPriorChildren`) exercises the two-add-then-set sequence and asserts both prior children are detached.
- **`UIPanelHelper` / `UITooltipHelper` fade tweens outlived screen teardown, pinning h2d.Objects past the screen's lifetime** — on hot-reload or full screen rebuild, `UIScreenBase.clear()` reset `panelHelpers = []` but never cancelled in-flight tweens. Fade-in/fade-out tween closures capture the panel/tooltip `h2d.Object` (fade-out closures explicitly call `obj.remove()` on completion), so the tween manager kept them alive through the clear cycle; after the screen root was reparented or destroyed, the residual tween would still advance and then remove orphaned objects. Fix: new `dispose()` on both helpers cancels every tracked fade tween (single-panel `activeFadeInTween`/`activeFadeOutTween`, named-panel per-slot tweens, and orphaned `namedFadeOutTweens`), removes any in-flight fading-out object, detaches the active result, unregisters interactives, and clears override maps. `UIScreenBase.clear()` now disposes every registered panel helper before resetting the array. Three regression tests: `UIPanelHelperTest.testDisposeCancelsInFlightFadeTweens` (single-panel + two named panels in flight), `UIPanelHelperTest.testScreenClearDisposesOpenPanelHelpers` (end-to-end via `UIScreenBase.clear()`), `UITooltipHelperTest.testDisposeCancelsInFlightFadeTweens` (fade-in over fading-out).
- **`repeatable($v, array(…))` / `stateanim(…)` / `tiles(…)` / `layout(…)` iteration bodies froze on outer-param change inside the incremental rebuild path** — `MultiAnimBuilder`'s param-change rebuild for `repeatable` only re-evaluated the count+body for `StepIterator` and `RangeIterator`; the four other iterator variants (`LayoutIterator`, `ArrayIterator`, `StateAnimIterator`, `TilesIterator`) fell into the `default:` arm of the switch, which left `newCount = 0` — so after rebuilding the child registrations the loop simply skipped the body and produced an empty container. `@(outerParam=>…) bitmap` conditionals inside `repeatable($v, array($val, $items)) { @(level=>1) … @else … }` therefore wiped on `setParameter("level", …)` instead of re-evaluating under the new value. Fix: the rebuild path now handles all six iterator variants — `newCount` is recomputed per variant (resolving the underlying `$items`/`.anim` selector/`tiles()` filter/`layout()` sequence), per-iteration loop vars (`ArrayIterator`'s value var, `StateAnimIterator`'s bitmap var, `TilesIterator`'s bitmap+tilename vars) are repopulated in `indexedParams` each iteration and cleaned up after, and `LayoutIterator` positions children via `newLayoutIterator.next()` while `StepIterator` continues to use `newDx`/`newDy`. One regression test in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testIncrementalRepeatableArrayIteratorChildConditions`): `repeatable($v, array($val, $items)) { @($level=>1) bitmap(15px red) @else bitmap(25px green) }` — asserts 3 green bitmaps initially, 3 red after `setParameter("level", 1)`, 3 green again after flipping back.
- **`TweenSequence.step` recursed per-tween when a single `dt` spanned several short tweens (stack overflow on long pauses)** — a debugger pause, paused tab, or sleeping OS tick can land a single `update(dt)` call with a dt larger than several chained tween durations combined. The old implementation recursively called `step(remaining)` for each tween that overflowed, one native stack frame per tween; a sequence of many sub-second tweens being resumed after a minute-long pause blew the native stack. Fix: replaced the tail-recursion with an explicit `while (currentIndex < tweens.length)` loop that drains as much of `remainingDt` as possible before returning. Same observable behavior (each tween's `onComplete` fires, leftover dt cascades into the next tween) without the stack growth. Also replaced three `Lambda.foreach(tweens, t -> t.cancelled)` calls in `cancelAll` / `cancelAllChildren` with a plain-loop `allCancelled(tweens)` helper to avoid allocating an anonymous function on every cancel call.
- **`PPTTile` parameter rejected raw `h2d.Tile` at initial build and at param-dependent subtree conversion** — passing an `h2d.Tile` directly to a `tile:`-typed programmable parameter (the documented runtime shape) had two silent failures: (1) `MultiAnimParser.dynamicValueToIndex`'s `PPTTile` branch always called `Std.string(value)` and wrapped as `TSFile(RVString(...))`, so the initial `buildWithParameters(..., {"icon": myTile})` call tried to load the tile as a file path; (2) `MultiAnimBuilder.dynamicToResolvedWithDef`'s `PPTTile` branch (used by `buildSlotContent`, `rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal`, `buildSingleNodeWithParams`) did the same, so a tile passed through a parameterized slot or param-dependent repeatable also reduced to a bogus file reference. `SlotHandle.setParameter` and the parser's `PPTTile` default-value path both already produced `TileSourceValue(TSTile(...))` — the initial-build/dynamic-convert paths now agree. Fix: both branches now recognize raw `h2d.Tile` and produce `TileSourceValue(TSTile(value))`; `dynamicToResolvedWithDef` additionally passes through pre-converted `ResolvedIndexParameters` untouched; mismatched types throw `BuilderError` naming the param. Guarded with `#if !macro` so the macro compile path (which cannot depend on `h2d.Tile`) retains the string fallback. Two regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testInitialBuildAcceptsRawTileForTileParam` (end-to-end — `bitmap($icon)` renders with the raw tile's 18×22 dimensions), `testDynamicToResolvedWithDefForRawTile` (unit — the private conversion produces `TileSourceValue(TSTile(t))`).
- **`UICardHandHelper.clearHand()` left the targeting arrow visible and the highlight callback stuck** — when a card was mid-drag in targeting mode (isTargeting=true), `clearHand()` reset `isTargeting=false` and restored the system cursor but never called `targeting.clearLine()`. The arrow segments and arrowhead stayed visible on the `dragContainer`, and any `activeTargetId` in `UICardHandTargeting` outlived the cards it was tracking — the next `updateHighlight`/`updateTargetingLine` saw a stale active id with no registered wrapper and failed to fire `onTargetHighlight(id, false, ...)`, leaving game-side highlight state pinned until an unrelated interaction flipped it. Reachable through `setHand()` (calls `clearHand()` internally) mid-drag and through any game-code path that clears the hand during a card play/abort. `dispose()` masked the bug via its explicit `targeting.clearTargets()` call after `clearHand()`. Fix: `clearHand()` now calls `targeting.clearLine()` inside the `isTargeting` branch, which hides the arrow segments and fires the un-highlight callback for any active target before the cards and helpers are torn down.
- **`@switch` arms did not rebuild on outer-param changes that flowed into `dynamicRef` / `staticRef` / `interactive` / `stateanim` / `stateanim_construct`** — `MultiAnimBuilder.collectNodeParamRefs` only walked `TEXT` / `RICHTEXT` / `NINEPATCH` / `BITMAP` / `GRAPHICS` / `PIXELS` / `REPEAT` / `SWITCH` node types (plus extended `pos`/`scale`/`rotation`/`alpha`/`tint`/`filter`). Every other `NodeType` that carries `ReferenceableValue`s in its payload was invisible to the switch-arm rebuild set and to `materializeDeferred`'s rebuild set.
