Compounded by arm children being built with `incrementalMode=false` (so `DYNAMIC_REF`'s `trackDynamicRef` at the `buildWithParameters` call site and `trackIncrementalExpressions` for `INTERACTIVE` / `STATEANIM` are skipped for those children), the only update route for the affected nodes was an arm rebuild driven by `collectNodeParamRefs` output — which never listed the relevant refs. Net effect: `@switch { a: dynamicRef($inner, v => $outer) }` stayed visually stuck on the initial value after `setParameter("outer", ...)` until the arm flipped for another reason. Same for `staticRef(...)` forwarded params, `interactive($w, $h, $id)` dimensions/id/metadata, and `stateanim(..., state => $outer)` selectors. `collectNodeParamRefs` now collects refs from `DYNAMIC_REF` / `STATIC_REF` (programmable reference + forwarded parameter values), `INTERACTIVE` (width, height, id, metadata key/value), `STATEANIM` (initialState + selector map values), and `STATEANIM_CONSTRUCT` (initialState + `IndexedSheet` animName/fps). Hot-path cost is zero — `collectNodeParamRefs` is invoked once per SWITCH build and once per `materializeDeferred`, not per `setParameter` or per frame. Regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalSwitchDynamicRefOuterParamRef`, `testIncrementalSwitchStaticRefOuterParamRef`, `testIncrementalSwitchInteractiveOuterParamRefInWidth`.
- **`staticRef`/`dynamicRef` cycles stack-overflowed instead of raising a structured error** — `MultiAnimBuilder.buildWithParameters` resolves refs by recursing back into itself, so a chain that re-enters a name already in flight (`A → A`, `A → B → A`, any longer variant) recursed until the native stack exploded. Users saw a generic native crash with no source position, no programmable name, and nothing a catch site could filter on. `getCurves()` already guarded this case with a `resolving:Map<String,Bool>` and threw `'circular curve reference'`; programmables had no parity. Fix: new `buildingRefs:Array<String>` field on the builder, pushed at the top of `buildWithParameters` (after a scan that raises `BuilderError code="circular_reference"` if the name is already present) and popped on every exit path, including a `try/catch` around the whole method so an inner `startBuild` throw doesn't leave the stack imbalanced and falsely reject the next call. Message includes the offending programmable name. Three regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testStaticRefSelfReferenceThrowsCircular` (`staticRef($a)` inside `#a`), `testStaticRefMutualCircularReferenceThrows` (`#a → staticRef($b)`, `#b → staticRef($a)`), `testDynamicRefMutualCircularReferenceThrows` (same shape via `dynamicRef`), all assert `BuilderError.code == "circular_reference"` and that the message contains "circular" + the programmable name.
- **`@final` constants used as conditional or `@switch` keys produced misleading errors or silently broke codegen** — `@final` is a parse-time alias with no runtime value slot. `matchSingleCondition` (runtime matcher) has no `ExpressionAlias` case, and `ProgrammableCodeGen.condMapToExpr` emits `$this.<finalName>` — a reference to a nonexistent field — for the codegen path. Previously a `@final` in `@(MY_CONST=>…)` / `@if(…)` / `@any(…)` / `@all(…)` / `@else(…)` / `@switch(MY_CONST)` either slipped past parse-time validation (because `@final` names are stored in `scopeVars` alongside loop vars, and the conditional/switch check treated any `scopeVars` entry as valid) or surfaced as a generic `does not have definition` at runtime, with `@final` vs parameter indistinguishable in the message. Fix: new `activeFinalNames:Null<Array<String>>` field in `MacroManimParser`, populated at the `FINAL_VAR` parse site alongside the existing `scopeVars.push` and `activeFinals.set`, saved/restored symmetrically with the other scope fields across PROGRAMMABLE and SLOT boundaries. `parseConditional` (covers `@()`, `@if`, `@any`, `@all`, `@else`) and the `@switch` parse head both consult `activeFinalNames` and raise a parse error with the exact `@final` name and actionable guidance ("use a programmable parameter instead"). The rule applies regardless of RHS literalness — both literal-RHS `@finals` (tracked in `activeFinals`) and derived-RHS `@finals` (not tracked there) are rejected. Seven regression tests in `ParserErrorTest` (one per conditional form: `testFinalAs{Conditional,If,Any,All,Else,Switch}KeyRejected` + `testFinalWithNonLiteralRhsAsConditionalKeyRejected`). `BuilderUnitTest.testTileGroupConditionalOnFinal_Throws` updated — the check now fires at parse time everywhere, so the `tileGroup` validator's `@final` branch is unreachable from source; the test asserts the new parse-time rejection path instead.
- **`@:manim` macro did not mirror `MultiAnimBuilder`'s tileGroup-conditional validator — errors surfaced only on first `create()`** — `MultiAnimBuilder.validateTileGroupSubtree` rejects conditionals inside `tileGroup { ... }` whose predicate references a programmable parameter (or `@final`, or anything other than an enclosing repeatable loop var) with `BuilderError code="tilegroup_conditional"`, because `tileGroup` bakes descendants once and the incremental-update path never re-enters them. The runtime builder raised this at the TILEGROUP case in `build`, but the codegen's `generateTileGroupCreate` had no equivalent walk — `@:manim(...)` fields compiled clean and only surfaced the error on first runtime `create()`, trading a macro-time failure (editor underline, compile break, file:line:col diagnostic) for a runtime exception with no IDE feedback. Fix: new `ProgrammableCodeGen.validateTileGroupSubtreeMacro(node, loopVarScope, pos)` ports the runtime validator to macro AST terms — `requireLoopVar(paramName, source)` raises `Context.fatalError` at the `@:manim` field `Position` when a `Conditional` / `ConditionalElse(extraConds)` key or `@switch` param name isn't in the enclosing `REPEAT` / `REPEAT2D` loop-var scope. `generateTileGroupCreate` calls it over every child before emitting the ctor. Error message matches the runtime message suffix (`[tilegroup_conditional, programmable="$currentProgrammableName"]`) so error-handling tooling sees the same code/programmable pair in both paths. No new tests — the existing `BuilderUnitTest` tileGroup suite exercises both paths via `simpleMacroTest` / direct `buildFromSource` and now reports the same message from either failure point.
- **`dynamicRef($X)` sibling collisions silently returned the wrong handle; hidden children received no propagated updates and surfaced with stale state after visibility flip** — `BuilderResult.dynamicRefs` was a `Map<String, BuilderResult>` keyed by the referenced programmable name, so two `dynamicRef($X)` sites in the same subtree (e.g. `@(a=>1) dynamicRef($X)` + `@else dynamicRef($X)`, or N iterations of `repeatable { dynamicRef($X) }`) collapsed onto one map entry. Build-order last-writer won, which was arbitrary with respect to which sibling was currently attached to the scene graph — `getDynamicRef("X")` could silently return a detached handle, and `setParameter` on it mutated an orphan. The `applyUpdates` propagation loop additionally gated `dynamicRefBindings` on `isEffectivelyVisible` as an optimization, so updates to forwarded params while a sibling was hidden were dropped — then when visibility flipped, the previously-hidden sibling surfaced with stale parameter state. Fix, two parts:
  - **`#name dynamicRef(...)` is now honored as the map key**. `#name dynamicRef($X, ...)` stores under `"name"`; `#name[$i] dynamicRef($X, ...)` inside a `repeatable` stores under `"name idx"` (and `#name[$x, $y]` under `"name x y"`) — each iteration is independently addressable. Unnamed sites continue to key by the referenced programmable name (`"X"`) for backward compatibility; multiple unnamed sibling sites writing to the same key are now tracked in a parallel `dynamicRefCollisions` counter, and `getDynamicRef(name)` throws `BuilderError` with a hint to use `#name` when the counter is >1. Two explicit `#name dynamicRef(...)` sites that share the same name throw at build time (that's a user error, not ambiguity). Dynamic-name refs (`dynamicRef($template)` where `$template` is a parameter) now carry a `stableKey` on the `DynamicNameBinding`, so a `#foo dynamicRef($template)` site stays addressable as `"foo"` across template swaps instead of moving to the new template's name.
  - **`applyUpdates` no longer skips `dynamicRefBindings` whose child is currently invisible**. Forwarding `setParameter` into a detached incremental context is just a state write (no rendering), and it keeps the hidden child coherent for the next time it becomes visible. The parallel `trackedExpressions` visibility gate stays (expression re-eval on invisible objects has no visible effect).
  - Fixtures updated with `#probe dynamicRef(...)` to demonstrate the disambiguation pattern in `test/examples/73-dynamicRefs/dynamicRefs.manim` and `test/examples/74-dynamicRefScope/dynamicRefScope.manim`. Five regression tests in `DynamicRefTest`: `testDynamicRefColocatedSiblingsThrowOnGet` (ambiguity error at getter), `testDynamicRefNamedSiblingsAreIndependentlyAddressable` (`#armOn` / `#armOff` under `@()`/`@else` both queryable through a flip cycle), `testDynamicRefIndexedInRepeatableIsIndependentlyAddressable` (`#item[$i]` produces distinct `"item 0"`/`"item 1"`/`"item 2"` keys), `testDynamicRefExplicitNameCollisionThrowsAtBuild` (two `#widget` sites → build error), `testDynamicRefConditionalSiblingsPropagateAcrossVisibilityFlip` (named siblings stay in sync across flip). `BuilderUnitTest.testDynamicRefPropagationSkipsHiddenSubtree` inverted to `testDynamicRefPropagationReachesHiddenSubtree`, codifying the new always-propagate semantics.
- **`IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` silently corrupted state on invalid value types; `beginUpdate`/`endUpdate` nested/unbalanced without complaint; retained `SlotHandle` after arm swap silently mutated orphaned objects; parameterized slots had no path to their own param defs; typed metadata refs diverged between builder and codegen**.
    - `setParameter(name, value)` walked an `isOfType` cascade (Int/Float/String/Bool/ResolvedIndexParameters/h2d.Tile) and silently fell through when no branch matched — but still flipped `changedParams.set(name, true)` and `hasChanges = true`, so dependent rebuild listeners re-ran against a stale `indexedParams` entry. Fix: resolve the converted value first, commit only on success, and on miss throw `BuilderError` with `code="invalid_param_value"`. The message names the param and rejected type. Unknown param names now short-circuit cleanly (return before flag flip) — UI widgets (Button/Checkbox/Slider/Tabs/TextInput) rely on `setParameter("disabled", ...)` being a no-op for templates that don't opt in, so unknown is not a throw.
    - `beginUpdate()` called while already in batch used to silently clear the outer's pending `changedParams` and shadow its `hasChanges`; `endUpdate()` without a matching `beginUpdate()` flipped `batchMode` off mid-scope. Fix: both now throw structured `BuilderError` (`code="nested_begin_update"` / `"unbalanced_end_update"`).
    - `IncrementalUpdateContext.getParamDefinition` only matched `PROGRAMMABLE(_, defs, _)`, so parameterized slots (which own their own `IncrementalUpdateContext`) returned `null` from every param lookup and lost the type-aware PPTFlags branch entirely. Fix: new `case SLOT(defs, _)` mirrors PROGRAMMABLE behavior (`null` defs → unknown-param no-op path).
    - External callers retaining a `SlotHandle` whose enclosing subtree was torn down (SWITCH arm swap, repeatable shrinkage) could still call `setParameter` and silently mutate orphaned `h2d.Objects` — the IR entry was evicted from `ir.slots`, but the handle's `incrementalContext` lived on. Fix: new `SlotHandle.disposed:Bool` flag set by `removeRegistrationsUnder` at the same site as the `ir.slots.splice`; `setParameter` throws `BuilderError` with `code="slot_disposed"` instead of mutating. `SlotHandle.setParameter` on a non-parameterized slot (declared as `#name slot { ... }` without a parameter list) also throws `BuilderError` with `code="slot_no_parameters"` — previously a bare message with no code, so catch sites could not filter it programmatically.
    - `MacroManimParser` accepted typed metadata like `key:int => $enumParam` / `key:bool => $floatParam` on `interactive(...)`, but the runtime builder's lenient `resolveAs*()` and the codegen's strict `RSV<Type>(_field)` emit resolved incompatibly — runtime returned the enum index, codegen compile-errored or returned raw flag bits. Fix: new `validateTypedMetadataRef(key, metaType, value)` runs at parse time from `parseInteractiveMetadataEntry`; compatibility matrix in `isMetaTypeCompatibleWithParam` encodes: `:int`/`:color` need int-backed param (int/uint/range/color/bool/dir); `:float` needs any numeric; `:bool` needs bool/int/uint/range/dir; `:string` excludes flags/array/tile. Mismatch throws a `parse error` with the key name, declared meta type, referenced param name, and its type. Literals, loop vars, and `$ctx` refs are unaffected. `@final` refs are also validated when the `@final`'s RHS is a direct literal (Int / Float / quoted String / `"true"`/`"false"`) — a sibling `activeFinals:Map<String, SettingValueType>` is populated at the `FINAL_VAR` parse site via a new `inferFinalLiteralType(expr)` helper, and `validateTypedMetadataRef` runs a parallel compatibility matrix (`isMetaTypeCompatibleWithFinalType`) against it. `@final`s with a non-literal RHS (binops, refs, ternaries) stay unvalidated since the type can't be inferred at parse time.
    - Six regression tests: `testReentrantSetParameterInRebuildListener`, `testReentrantSetParameterDuringSwitchRebuild`, `testBatchNestedBeginUpdateThrows`, `testBatchEndUpdateWithoutBeginThrows`, `testBatchFlatPairStillWorks`, `testSetParameterInvalidValueTypeThrows`, `testSetParameterNullValueThrows`, `testSetParameterInvalidValueDoesNotFireRebuildListeners`, `testSetParameterUnknownParamIsSilentNoOp` in `BuilderUnitTest`; `testParameterizedSlotSetParameterThrowsAfterArmSwap` + `testNonParameterizedSlotSetParameterThrows` in `ParameterizedSlotTest` (both now assert `BuilderError.code` — `"slot_disposed"` / `"slot_no_parameters"`); `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedIntFromEnum_RejectedAtParseTime` + positive `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedIntFromInt_StillParses` + `@final` coverage (`testInteractiveMetadata_TypedIntFromStringFinal_RejectedAtParseTime`, `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedBoolFromStringFinal_RejectedAtParseTime`, `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedIntFromIntFinal_StillParses`, `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedStringFromStringFinal_StillParses`, `testInteractiveMetadata_TypedIntFromExprFinal_StillParses`) in `CodegenIncrementalInteractiveStateanimTest`.
- **`setParameter` on a param referenced inside a param-dependent repeatable body silently no-opped (or crashed) in both paths** — when a `repeatable`'s iteration count is param-dependent (`repeatable($i, step($n, ...))`), codegen routes each child through `ProgrammableCodeGen.generateRuntimeChildExprs`, which only handles BITMAP / POINT / TEXT / RICHTEXT / NINEPATCH / GRAPHICS / PIXELS / MASK / LAYERS / FLOW inline; everything else (INTERACTIVE, STATIC_REF, DYNAMIC_REF, SWITCH, nested REPEAT, SLOT, APPLY, SPACER, …) fell into the `default:` arm that called `ProgrammableBuilder.buildNodeByUniqueName` with no param setup and no registration in the codegen's `untrackedParamRefs` map. Net effect on the codegen side: `create()` errored at construction with `reference X does not exist, available []` as soon as the subtree referenced a programmable param (or a loop var) — the fallback's `buildSingleNode` used whatever `indexedParams` happened to be on the builder, usually empty. The builder path had a parallel bug: `MultiAnimBuilder.build`'s REPEAT case disables `incrementalMode` for children of a param-dep repeat (they're full-rebuilt on count change), which skips `trackIncrementalExpressions` and therefore skips the `ctx.markParamUntracked` calls for INTERACTIVE id/metadata and STATEANIM selectors. `result.setParameter("myId", ...)` on a param only referenced inside such a subtree updated the internal map and returned without throwing or visually updating — a silent footgun that looked reactive in docs. Fix, codegen: new `MultiAnimBuilder.buildSingleNodeWithParams(node, progNode, parentParams)` mirrors `rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal`'s state setup (pushBuilderState, resolve parentParams via `dynamicToResolvedWithDef` / `dynamicToResolvedInferred`, set `indexedParams`/`builderParams`, build, pop); new wrapper `ProgrammableBuilder.buildNodeByUniqueNameWithParams` delegates to it; `generateRuntimeChildExprs`' default arm now emits a `_rt_pp` Map populated with every programmable param (`this._myId` etc.) plus each active runtime loop var (`runtimeLoopVars` keys → their `_rt_i` / `_rt_val` / `_rt_ix` / `_rt_iy` idents) and passes it to the new method. New `recordUntrackedParamsInSubtree(node)` walker called from `rebuildRepeatChildren` and `rebuildRepeat2DChildren` mirrors the INTERACTIVE / STATEANIM / STATEANIM_CONSTRUCT static-path calls to `recordUntrackedParams`, so a codegen setter on an untracked param throws the same `"untracked_param"` rejection as the static-count baseline. Fix, builder: new `markUntrackedParamsInSubtree(node, ctx, excludeVar)` walker called from the REPEAT case before `incrementalMode = false`, with `excludeVar = varName` so the loop var itself isn't marked. Two regression tests in new `CodegenRepeatFallbackUntrackedTest` (builder + codegen), backed by fixture `test/examples/114-codegenRepeatFallbackUntracked`, assert `setParameter("myId", ...)` on an `interactive(100, 50, $myId)` inside `repeatable($i, step($n, ...))` throws `"untracked_param"` through both paths. Still frozen (same policy as static-count): SLOT / APPLY structure inside a param-dep repeat body; those need separate codegen work and are not covered by this fix.
- **Dialog → Dialog modal overlay leaked old bitmap and dropped the new one** — `ScreenManager.modalDialog()` (and `modalDialogWithTransition()`) built and attached the new overlay BEFORE calling `updateScreenMode()`/`switchScreen()`. The mode switch ran the `case Dialog(oldDialog, …)` branch which starts with `removeModalOverlay()` — that call wiped the brand-new overlay just created by the caller, and the previous dialog's overlay (still referenced by `modalOverlay` at the moment the new bitmap was assigned over it) was never removed from the scene. Net effect on a Dialog → Dialog transition: two bugs simultaneously — the previous overlay orphaned in the scene graph at `sceneLayers.overlay`, and the new dialog rendered without any overlay at all. Fix: run the mode transition first so the old overlay is torn down, then create the new overlay. `applyBlurToUnderlyingScreens()` gained an `?exclude:UIScreen` argument because after the re-order the new dialog is already in `activeScreens` when blur is applied, and it would otherwise blur its own scene root. `applyBlurToUnderlyingScreens` now asserts `exclude` is in `activeScreens` at call time — the silent `contains() == false → no-op` path would mask a future refactor that restored the natural "prepare overlay before mode switch" ordering and would then blur the wrong screens. Also removed the explicit `removeScreen(oldDialog)` call inside the Dialog → Dialog branch of `updateScreenMode()` — the shared post-switch loop (`removedScreens`) already removes the old dialog, and doing it inline meant `UILeaving` fired AFTER the scene root was detached (`getSceneRoot().parent == null`) only for this one transition, breaking the invariant every other mode transition holds. Three regression tests: `ScreenManagerModalOverlayTest.testDialogOverDialogKeepsNewOverlay` asserts the new overlay is attached and the previous one is removed; `ScreenManagerModalOverlayTest.testApplyBlurExcludeMustBeInActiveScreens` asserts the new exclude-membership invariant; `ScreenManagerDialogTransitionTest.testDialogToDialog_UILeavingFiresWhileAttached` (with a `Single → Single` control case) asserts `UILeaving` fires with the scene root still attached.
- **`UIRichInteractiveHelper` left touch-initiated buttons stuck in `Hover` after click** — `UIClick` on a `Pressed` binding unconditionally returned to `Hover` and emitted `"hover"`, which is correct for a mouse cycle (Normal → Hover → Pressed → Hover → …) but wrong for touch (no prior hover; Normal → Pressed → `?` → Normal). Touch devices never fire `UIEntering`, so the binding was entering `Pressed` from `Normal` and then terminating in `Hover`, visually stranding the button in its hover state until the next `UILeaving` or unrelated event. Fix: new `enteredPressedViaHover:Bool` field on `InteractiveBinding` records whether the push transitioned from `Hover` (mouse) or `Normal` (touch); click now returns to `Hover` in the mouse case and `Normal` in the touch case, emitting the matching param value (`"hover"` / `"normal"`). One regression test in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest` (`testTouchCycleClickReturnsToNormal`).
- **`FloatingTextHelper.update()` crashed / lost instances when an `onComplete` callback called `spawn()` or `clear()` reentrantly** — the swap-remove loop re-read `instances.length` each iteration and fired `onComplete` BEFORE finishing the array splice, so a callback that appended (`spawn()`) pushed the active-window end out and re-consumed the new instance's dt on the current frame, while a callback that truncated (`clear()`) produced an out-of-bounds access on the next iteration. Fix: snapshot `count = instances.length` at loop entry, do the swap-remove *before* firing `onComplete`, and clamp `count = min(count, instances.length)` after the callback returns. Instances spawned reentrantly now sit in `[count..length)` untouched and start fresh on the next `update()` (they don't consume this frame's dt); instances cleared reentrantly are skipped cleanly. Two regression tests in `FloatingTextHelperTest` (`testOnCompleteCanCallClear`, `testOnCompleteCanSpawn`).
- **`graphics(...)` lost its vertex content on scene detach/re-attach in the codegen (`@:manim`) path** — follow-up to the builder fix shipped earlier in rc.5. Both codegen graphics sites (`ProgrammableCodeGen.generateGraphicsCreate` field path at L3629 and `generateRuntimeChildExprs` `_rt_g` runtime path at L2685) still constructed raw `h2d.Graphics`, so any `@:manim`-compiled programmable with `graphics(...)` that was detached and re-attached (`ScreenManager.switchTo`, screen-stack return, Heaps reparenting) came back with empty vertex buffers. The builder-only fix left a path-dependent bug that visual tests missed: they render once and never exercise the `onRemove`/`onAdd` cycle. Fix: both sites now emit `new bh.multianim.KeepGraphics()`. The field type stays `h2d.Graphics` (KeepGraphics subclasses it), so consumer code typed against `h2d.Graphics` keeps compiling; explicit `_g.clear()` in the `_redraw_<fieldName>()` method still fires because `suppressClear` only gates the `onRemove`-internal call. New regression test `test113_CodegenGraphicsReattach` exercises both sites in one programmable (top-level graphics + `$count`-dependent `repeatable { graphics(...) }` to force `processRuntimeRepeat`); without either fix, macro similarity drops to 86.35% / 93.93% respectively.
- **`graphics(...)` lost its vertex content on scene detach/re-attach** — `h2d.Graphics.onRemove()` calls `clear()` (wipes all draw commands). The manim redraw path only re-fires on conditional re-insertion or `$param` change, not when a whole screen root is detached and later re-added (e.g. `ScreenManager` switching screens, Heaps-internal reparenting), so screens returning from the stack came back with empty graphics. Fix: new `bh.multianim.KeepGraphics` subclass that suppresses `clear()` when it's invoked from inside `onRemove()` (flag toggled around the `super.onRemove()` call; polymorphic dispatch routes the inner `clear()` through the override). Explicit param-driven `clear()` calls still work normally. Builder now constructs `KeepGraphics` instead of `h2d.Graphics` at the `GRAPHICS(elements)` site in `MultiAnimBuilder`. Codegen paths migrated in the follow-up entry above.
- **Codegen `@else`/`@default` over-negated across chain boundaries** — `ProgrammableCodeGen.negatePriorSiblings` walked ALL prior siblings of a node and AND-negated every `Conditional` it found, with no stopping at chain boundaries and no handling of bare `@else` / `@default` or `NoConditional`. For two consecutive chains in the same parent (e.g. `@(x=>xa) e1; @else e2; @(y=>yb) e3; @default e4`) the codegen visibility expression for `e4` became `!(x==xa) && !(y==yb)` instead of the builder's `!(y==yb)` — under default params (x=xother, y=yother) the codegen showed `e4` while the builder correctly hid it (since `anyConditionalSiblingMatched` was already `true` from the bare `@else` in chain 1). Fix: replaced `negatePriorSiblings` with `computeChainStateBefore`, which symbolically replays the builder's `resolveVisibilityForChildren` state machine — `prevSiblingMatched` and `anyConditionalSiblingMatched` — across prior siblings. `Conditional(c)` sets `prev=c`, `any=any||c`; `ConditionalElse(null)` sets both to `true`; `ConditionalElse(extras)` propagates `prev=prev||extras`, `any=any || (!prev_before && extras)`; `ConditionalDefault` and `NoConditional` reset both to `false` (chain break). `@else` now emits `!prev`; `@default` emits `!any`. Visual test fixture `test/examples/112-chainBoundary/chainBoundary.manim` + four unit tests in new `ChainBoundaryConditionalTest` assert builder and codegen visibility match under default and chain-one-matching params.
- **`tileGroup { @(param=>…) … }` silently froze on first build (and double-baked `@else`/`@default` arms in incremental mode)** — `buildTileGroup` bakes descendants into a single `h2d.TileGroup` drawable and was never registered with `IncrementalUpdateContext` (no `trackConditional` hook), so conditionals whose predicate referenced a programmable parameter were resolved once and never re-evaluated when that param changed. Worse, in incremental mode `resolveConditionalChildren` returns all children unfiltered (expecting the main `build()` path to re-filter later via `isMatch`, which returns `true` for `ConditionalElse`/`ConditionalDefault` assuming pre-filtering) — so a `tileGroup { @(mode=>a) … @else … }` baked BOTH arms on initial build, not just the matching one. Same problem for `@switch(param)` inside tileGroup (arm resolved once via `resolveMatchedSwitchArm`). Fix: reject these up front at the TILEGROUP case in `MultiAnimBuilder.build` via a new `validateTileGroupSubtree(node, loopVarScope)` walk that throws `BuilderError` with `code="tilegroup_conditional"` when any descendant's `Conditional`/`ConditionalElse(extraConds)` predicate or enclosing `@switch(paramName)` names a key outside `loopVarScope`. Repeatable/repeatable2d loop vars introduced inside the tileGroup ARE in scope and allowed — they iterate at build time. `@else`/`@default` bare arms are allowed (their effective predicate comes from preceding `@()` siblings, which would throw first if mutable). Conditionals on the tileGroup node itself flow through the main incremental path unchanged. Six unit tests in `BuilderUnitTest` (`testTileGroup{Conditional,Else,Switch}OnParam_Throws`, `testTileGroupConditionalOnParam_Incremental_Throws`, `testTileGroupConditionalOnRepeatableLoopVar_Allowed`, `testTileGroupOuterConditionalOnParam_NotFlagged`).
- **Codegen `graphics(...)` / `pixels(...)` with `$param` refs froze at the initial draw** — the builder (incremental) path redrew on param change (`g.clear(); drawGraphicsElements(...)` / PixelLines tile+data swap), but the codegen path baked the draw calls into `createExprs` with `exprUpdates: []`. Any `$param` reference inside `graphics()` element sizes/colors/coordinates, or inside `pixels()` shape coordinates/colors/dimensions, was resolved once at construction and never re-evaluated. Fix: `generateGraphicsCreate` / `generatePixelsCreate` now collect param refs from every element/shape via new `collectGraphicsElementParamRefs` / `collectPixelShapesParamRefs` / `collectCoordinateParamRefs` codegen helpers (ports of the builder helpers); when any ref is found, a private `_redraw_<fieldName>()` method is emitted that contains `_g.clear()` + the original draw exprs (Graphics) or the full bounds-recompute + shape-draw + mutate-existing-PixelLines block (Pixels, so the scene-graph attachment is preserved). The method is called once from the ctor and registered as an `exprUpdate` so `setParameter()` re-fires it. All-static `pixels(...)` fast path unchanged. Two unit tests in new `CodegenIncrementalGfxPxTest` cover a filled rect Graphics (asserts `g.xMax` changes after `setVal(50)`) and a filled-rect PixelLines (asserts pixel data after `setVal(50)` via `assertPixelRect`).
- **`mask(w, h)` / `flow(...)` scalar properties with `$param` refs froze at construction in both paths** — neither path tracked any of MASK's `width`/`height` or FLOW's `maxWidth`/`maxHeight`/`minWidth`/`minHeight`/`lineHeight`/`colWidth`/`padding{Top,Bottom,Left,Right}`/`horizontalSpacing`/`verticalSpacing`. `setParameter` on a referenced param updated the internal field but left the `h2d.Mask` / `h2d.Flow` instance unchanged. Fix: builder adds `case MASK(...)` and `case FLOW(...)` in `trackIncrementalExpressions` that resolve+assign each scalar on change via a local `trackInt` closure; codegen `generateFlowCreate` refactored to use a `trackScalar(rv, assign)` inline helper that emits both the ctor assignment and an `exprUpdate` per prop, and the MASK case in `generateCreateExpr` emits width/height updates when refs are present. Enum-typed props (`layout`, `overflow`, `horizontalAlign`/`verticalAlign`), flow bool flags (`multiline`/`debug`/`fillWidth`/`fillHeight`/`reverse`), and the 9-patch background remain frozen at construction — those rarely take `$param` values and changing them implies a reflow the user may not want. Four unit tests in new `CodegenIncrementalMaskFlowTest` cover both paths × both elements.
- **`interactive(w, h, ...)` size frozen at construction in both paths** — builder created `MAObject(MAInteractive(resolveAsInteger(w), resolveAsInteger(h), ...))` once; codegen baked `$wExpr`/`$hExpr` into the ctor's `new MAObject(...)`. Changing a `$param` referenced in `w`/`h` (e.g. a resizable tooltip button) did not update hit-test bounds. Fix: `MAObject.multiAnimType` changed from `public final` to `public var` — all consumers (hit test in `UIInteractiveWrapper.containsPoint`, `MAObject.getBoundsRec`, card-hand targeting) already re-read on every call, so reassigning the enum propagates without wrapper rewiring. Builder adds `case INTERACTIVE(...)` that pattern-matches the current enum to preserve `id`/`metadata` and rebuilds with fresh `w`/`h`; codegen emits the equivalent exprUpdate with an exhaustive `MAInteractive | MADraggable` switch (the macro compiler rejected an implicit `default:`). `id` and `metadata` remain frozen at construction because `UIInteractiveWrapper` caches them at registration time — see Changed entry below for explicit rejection.
- **`stateanim(...)` `initialState` frozen in both paths** — builder did `animSM.play(resolveAsString(initialState))` once during `buildStateAnim`; codegen baked `Std.string($initialStateExpr)` into the ctor's `buildStateAnim` call. Changing a `$param` driving `initialState` (e.g. `stateanim("npc.anim", $action)` where `$action` toggles between `"idle"` / `"attack"`) left the animation on the initial state. Fix: builder `case STATEANIM(...)` / `case STATEANIM_CONSTRUCT(...)` tracks `initialState` refs and calls `sm.play(resolveAsString(initCapture))` on change; codegen emits `(cast $fieldRef : bh.stateanim.AnimationSM).play(Std.string($initialStateExpr))` as an exprUpdate. Selector references, filename, and construct sheet/animName/fps remain frozen — see Changed entry below for explicit rejection. Two unit tests in new `CodegenIncrementalInteractiveStateanimTest` cover both paths × both elements; fixture uses the existing `test/res/marine.anim` with `"idle"` / `"fire-up"` states.
- **Unconditional `apply { ... }` with `$param` expressions froze at the constructor value** — `apply { scale: $k; alpha: $a; ... }` without a `@()` wrapper baked the expressions once at build/construction time and never re-evaluated. `setParameter("k", 2.0)` left `scaleX` stuck at the initial value in both the runtime builder (incremental) and codegen paths. Conditional apply was unaffected because it flows through `_applyVisibility()` / `applyConditionalChains()` which already re-evaluate on param change. Fix: codegen `processApply` now registers each property update (scale/rotation/alpha/tint/filter/position) in `expressionUpdates` with collected `paramRefs`, mirroring the element path; builder drops the `node.conditionals != NoConditional` guard in the APPLY case so unconditional entries are also tracked via `captureApplyBaseline` + `trackConditionalApply`, and `resolveVisibilityForChildren` NoConditional branch calls `reconcileApplyParent` so the per-parent baseline-replay machinery re-fires. Two regression tests in `ApplyParamUpdateTest` (builder + codegen) backed by fixture `test/examples/108-applyParamUpdate`.
- **Interleaved `@(cond) apply { ... }` on the same parent corrupted state on unapply** — when two or more conditional `apply` blocks modified the same parent property (alpha/scale/rotation/filter/position) and their conditions could both be active, unapplying the outer while the inner stayed matched left the parent in the pre-any-apply state instead of the still-matched inner's value. Root cause: per-entry `savedAlpha`/etc. captured whatever the parent's property was *at the moment that entry applied* — which already included any earlier apply's mutation. Fixed by replacing per-entry save/restore with a per-parent baseline (`IncrementalUpdateContext.conditionalApplyBaselines`) captured lazily before the first conditional apply touches that parent. Any apply/unapply on that parent resets to baseline and replays all currently-matched entries in declaration order (`reconcileApplyParent`), so overlapping applies compose correctly and decouple from the order in which conditions flip. Three regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalApplyAlphaInterleavedSameProperty`, `testIncrementalApplyScaleInterleavedSameProperty`, `testIncrementalApplyAlphaInterleavedStartHidden`.
- **`repeatable`/`repeatable2d` loop variable could silently shadow a programmable parameter** — the parser accepted `repeatable($paramName, ...)` where `$paramName` was already a parameter of the enclosing programmable. `indexedParams` is shared between parameters and loop vars, so inside the body `$paramName` resolved to the loop index (int) instead of the parameter; worse, after the loop `MultiAnimBuilder.build` runs `indexedParams.remove(varName)` unconditionally, permanently erasing the parameter for any subsequent references in the same programmable. Parser now rejects: loop vars that match a parameter (`repeatable` + `repeatable2d`), loop vars that match any outer `scopeVars` entry (nested loops / iterator outputs / `@final`), `repeatable2d` with identical X and Y loop vars, iterator output vars (`array($v, …)`, `stateanim($b, …)`, `tiles($b, $t, …)`) matching a parameter or the main loop var, and the two `tiles(...)` outputs matching each other. One pre-existing `.manim` collision in `proto-game/res/manim/cards.manim` (`repeatable($cost, step($cost, dx:-12))` inside `#escDetailCard programmable(… cost:0..5=3 …)`) was updated to `repeatable($pip, step($cost, dx:-12))`; visible output is unchanged because the body never referenced `$cost`. Five new parser error tests in `ParserErrorTest`.
- **`@else`/`@default` chain after a terminal (bare) `@else` or `@default`** — conditional chains used to accept `@else` / `@default` following a bare `@else` or `@default`, even though those earlier arms are unconditional and make every trailing arm unreachable. Parser now rejects this at parse time with a clear "cannot follow a terminal @else or @default" message. Four new tests in `ParserErrorTest` (`testElseAfterBareElse`, `testDefaultAfterBareElse`, `testElseAfterDefault`, `testDefaultAfterDefault`). `test/examples/107-rubeGoldberg/rubeGoldberg.manim` restructured to make its `@default` start a fresh chain via `@(count >= 5) … @default …`.

- **Batched `setParameter` leaked transition specs across unrelated params** — `findTransitionSpec()` returned the first match from `changedParams` in `StringMap` iteration order, so a batched update over N unrelated params with an active transition on *any* one of them applied that spec to elements whose own controlling param had no transition. E.g. a `transition { paramA: fade(0.5) }` spec animated `paramB`-controlled elements during a combined `paramA + paramB` update. Fix scopes the spec lookup to the node being toggled: `findTransitionSpec(node)` collects the controlling params via a new `getRelevantParamRefsForNode(node)` (handles `Conditional`, `ConditionalElse` with preceding-sibling chain walk, `ConditionalDefault` with chain walk — `collectChainParamRefs` stops at `NoConditional` or an earlier `ConditionalDefault`, mirroring `resolveVisibilityForChildren`), then picks the alphabetically-first controlling param that both has a spec and was changed this cycle. When no controlling param has a spec, returns null (instant toggle). Regression: `testTransitionBatchedUpdatesScopedToParamRefs` in `BuilderUnitTest` builds a programmable with `paramA: fade(0.5)` + a non-transitioned `paramB`, batches both setters, and asserts that only `paramA`-driven elements carry tweens.
- **`@else` / `@default` branches flashed through a full fade-out when their chain decision was unchanged** — `IncrementalUpdateContext.applyUpdates` ran a flat `isMatch()` pass over `conditionalEntries` before calling `applyConditionalChains()`. `isMatch()` returns `true` unconditionally for `ConditionalElse` / `ConditionalDefault` (chain semantics only resolve inside `applyConditionalChains`). Pass 1 therefore `addToGraph`-ed the @else wrapper and started a fade-in whenever any param driving the chain's first arm changed; pass 2 then realized the @else was still inactive, cancelled the fade-in, and started a fade-out — leaving the element in graph at `savedAlpha` for the fade duration. Visible as a flash of a branch that never should have appeared. Fix removes the redundant pass: `applyConditionalChains` already walks the full tree from `rootNode`, handles `Conditional`/`ConditionalElse`/`ConditionalDefault` correctly via the per-node chain walk, and dispatches to both `conditionalEntries` and `conditionalApplyEntries` via `uniqueNodeName` lookup. `setPresenceWithTransition` and `setPresenceOrMaterialize` now take the `Node` so transition-spec lookup is node-scoped (see sibling entry). Regression: `testTransitionDoesNotFlashInactiveElseBranch` in `BuilderUnitTest` triggers the buggy path (transition on a non-chain param) and asserts no extra children, no extra visible bitmaps, and no tweens on the unchanged-visibility chain.
- **`dynamicRef` parameter propagation cascaded into hidden subtrees** — the `dynamicRefBindings` loop in `applyUpdates` gated only on whether a referenced parent param was in `changedParams`, with no visibility check. When a `dynamicRef` sat inside a hidden `@()` branch, parent `setParameter` calls to forwarded params still invoked `childContext.setParameter(...)` on the detached child — re-running its `applyUpdates` cycle (expression eval, conditional re-check) and firing any registered `addRebuildListener` callbacks. Wasted work and, for listeners that resync external state, a spurious notification. Fix adds an `object:Null<h2d.Object>` field to each binding (threaded through `trackDynamicRef` from both the initial build at `DYNAMIC_REF` and the `rebuildDynamicNameRef` path) and gates propagation on `isEffectivelyVisible(obj)`, mirroring the policy the `trackedExpressions` loop directly above already uses. Null-object fallback still propagates (matches the tracked-expressions `obj != null && !…` convention) so any caller that omits the new optional arg is unaffected. Regression: `testDynamicRefPropagationSkipsHiddenSubtree` in `BuilderUnitTest` attaches a rebuild listener to the child, hides the wrapper, changes the forwarded param, and asserts the child's listener does not fire.
- **`resolveAsString` RVParenthesis and `resolveAsBool` comparison ops silently swallowed all exceptions** — the `try { resolveAsNumber(e); … } catch (_:Dynamic) { return resolveAsString(e); }` pattern at two callsites (the string-interpolation `${expr}` path and the `<`/`>`/`<=`/`>=` numeric-fallback-to-string path in `resolveAsBool`) caught every thrown exception, including real errors like missing references, division by zero, or unsupported expression types. Users got unintended string concat / string comparison instead of the real error. Now catches only `BuilderError` with `code == "not_a_number"` (thrown from the specific sites where a value is well-formed but non-numeric: `RVString` NaN, `RVColor*`, callback returning `CBRString`). All other errors re-throw. `resolveAsBool`'s 4 separate try/catch blocks extracted into a shared `compareNumericOrString` helper with the narrow filter.
- **`generated(color(w, h, #c))` silently dropped explicit #RRGGBBAA alpha** — both the runtime (`MultiAnimBuilder.generatePlaceholderBitmap`) and codegen (`ProgrammableCodeGen` `SolidColor`/`Cross`) paths called `h2d.Tile.fromColor(color, w, h)` with no alpha arg. Heaps defaults alpha to 1.0 and masks the top byte of `color`, so parser-baked strict-D values like `#7fdbda33` (→ `0x337fdbda`, alpha `0x33`) rendered fully opaque — the author's explicit alpha byte was ignored. Same bug hit `.manim` `@final RGBA8 = #FF880080` constants used as `generated(color(...))` args, including those resolved through `$param` refs. Now both paths go through the shared `HeapsUtils.solidTile` helper which unpacks the alpha byte: `top = color >>> 24; alpha = top==0 ? 1.0 : top/255.0; Tile.fromColor(color & 0xFFFFFF, w, h, alpha)`. Top-byte=0 preserves the opaque default so legacy Haxe-side callers passing bare `0xRRGGBB` (e.g. `TileHelper.generatedRectColor(16, 16, 0x00FFFF)` in test code, `ScreenManager.ModalOverlayConfig.color`) still render opaque as before. Reference images for `#78 characterSheetDemo` (dividers written as `#7fdbda33` — 20% alpha cyan), `#107 rubeGoldberg` (`RGBA8 = #FF880080` / `graphics rect` with `#FF880080` — 50% alpha orange), and `#67 interactiveMetadata` (`MAObject` debug overlay alpha 0.5 re-encoded as byte 0x80, 1-LSB drift in quantization) regenerated.
- **Access violation when a programmable has two `@switch` blocks (or any second `@switch` following enough preceding siblings)** — `MacroManimParser.createNode` computed `uniqueNodeName` via `Std.string(type)`. For a `SWITCH(param, arms)` node the payload is a full subtree whose child `Node`s carry `parent` back-pointers up through the root programmable (and all its already-accumulated siblings). `Std.string` recurses the enum payload unbounded → HashLink stack overflow → `Access violation`. Only the second `@switch` tripped it because the first one's siblings hadn't yet grown the tree large enough. Fix: for `SWITCH` nodes only, `uniqueNodeName` now uses `Type.enumConstructor(type)` (short `"SWITCH"` identifier); all other node types keep the detailed `Std.string(type)` form to preserve uniqueness for `ProgrammableBuilder.buildNodeByUniqueName` / `findNodeByUniqueName` (REPEAT-child runtime forwarding). Regression coverage: new `test107_K_twoSwitchesWithBlockArmBug` and `test107_K_twoSwitchesOnDifferentParams` in `RubeGoldbergIncrementalTest`, plus the two restored `@switch` blocks in `rubeGoldberg.manim` exercise it in the visual test path.
- **`@final` constants at programmable-body scope lost after `setParameter`-triggered rebuild** — `IncrementalUpdateContext` deep-copies `indexedParams` at construction, which happens *before* `startBuild` evaluates `FINAL_VAR` nodes. Any `@final NAME = expr` declared at the programmable body was therefore missing from the context's persistent copy; the first `setParameter` call that triggered re-evaluation of an element referencing `$NAME` threw `reference NAME does not exist`. Fix: new `IncrementalUpdateContext.syncFinalsFromBuilder(live)` copies surviving `ExpressionAlias` entries from the live builder state into the context after initial build. Nested `@final`s (inside `point {}`, `repeatable`, etc.) are cleaned up by `cleanupFinalVars` before the sync runs and are correctly excluded — matching their lexical scope. Regression coverage: `test107_J_finalConstantsSurviveRebuild` plus the `@final SW / SH / RGB3 / RGB6 / RGBA8 / HEX_FULL / HEX_SHORT / TRANSPARENT` block in `rubeGoldberg.manim` referenced across multiple setter round-trips.
- **Codegen `setParameter` dispatcher Int-family coercion** — Int-family params (`int`, `uint`, `color`, `hexDir`, `gridDir`, `range`, `flags`) previously used an unchecked `cast _value` in the generated `setParameter(name, value)` dispatcher. On HL, passing a Dynamic `Float` (common when values come from JSON, arithmetic, or cross-target code) crashed on the downstream Int field write; on JS it silently passed the Float through, producing cross-target drift. Now coerces Dynamic input through the same pattern already used for `PPTFloat`: accepts Int directly, truncates Float via `Std.int`, parses String via `Std.parseInt` (throwing on unparseable), rejects other types. `PPTString` similarly gained an `Std.isOfType(_value, String)` guard to reject Int/Float/Bool silently coerced through `cast`. Regression tests `testCodegenSetParameterIntFamilyCoercesFloatAndString`, `testCodegenSetParameterIntFamilyRejectsUnparseableString`, `testCodegenSetParameterIntFamilyRejectsBool`, `testCodegenSetParameterStringRejectsNonString`, `testCodegenSetParameterFloatCoercion` in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest`.
- **Codegen `_fireRebuildListeners` fired on no-op setter calls** — typed setters unconditionally called `_fireRebuildListeners()` at the end, even when the incoming value matched the stored value. With N cards in a hand, repeated `setParameter("status", "normal")` on hover-leave (a hot path in `UIRichInteractiveHelper`) triggered quadratic `UIScreen.addInteractives` rescans. Setters now short-circuit at the top: `if (this._<param> == v) return;` before the field write and downstream `_applyVisibility` / `_updateExpressions` / `_fireRebuildListeners`. Bool guard compares after Int conversion (`true/false` → `1/0`), so repeated bool sets also skip. Reference-equality for Array/Tile params is acceptable (callers only pass fresh instances when they mean to rebuild). Regression tests `testCodegenNoOpSetSkipsRebuildListener`, `testCodegenNoOpGuardCoversAllTypes`, `testCodegenNoOpEnumStatusGuard` in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest`.
- **Codegen `setParameter` PPTBool string parsing only accepted `"true"`** — the generated dispatcher's Bool branch coerced String input via `(cast _value : String) == "true"`, silently returning `false` for `"yes"`, `"1"`, `"TRUE"`, or any typo. Inconsistent with `findEnumIndex` which accepts `true|yes|1|false|no|0` case-insensitive. Now aligned: `"true"|"yes"|"1"` → true, `"false"|"no"|"0"` → false (all case-insensitive), unknown strings throw explicitly (previously silently became false). Regression tests `testCodegenSetParameterBoolStringVariants`, `testCodegenSetParameterBoolUnknownStringThrows`, `testCodegenSetParameterBoolIntAndBoolPassthrough` in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest`.
- **Codegen root-tint emitter still inlined alpha bake** — `ProgrammableCodeGen.generateFields` wrapped `$tintExpr` with `if (c >>> 24 == 0) c |= 0xFF000000` before `d.color.setColor(c)`, contradicting the strict-D rule that Int is final (runtime `applyExtendedFormProperties` writes the resolved int verbatim). Dormant because the `Std.isOfType(_, h2d.Drawable)` guard skips `h2d.Layers` / `h2d.Object` roots, but latent if any root ever became a Drawable subclass. Bake removed; codegen now calls `d.color.setColor($tintExpr)` directly.
- **LSP `tryStringToColor` 0x-branch baked alpha in stale compiled artifact** — `vscode/server/server.js` shipped with the pre-strict-D `var hexLen = s.length - 2; if (hexLen == 8) ...; if (color >>> 24 == 0) color |= -16777216;` logic, while the current Haxe source is a plain `return Std.parseInt(s)`. Users got "opaque red" diagnostics for `tint: 0xFF0000` from the LSP while the runtime builder stored it as transparent red. Rebuilt `lsp/lsp-server.hxml` and re-copied to `vscode/server/server.js` (the bump-but-don't-rebuild pattern in `install-local.ps1` caused this).
- **`Particles.emitBurstAt` over-counted rejected particles in `liveCount`** — the unconditional `liveCount++` fired before the `if (accepted)` gate, so a rejected-then-recycled particle (or any path through `init()` that returned `false`) permanently left `liveCount` +1 in looping groups. Inconsistent with `start()` / per-frame `emit()` which never increment `liveCount` on rejection. Moved `liveCount++` inside `if (accepted)`.
- **Codegen ignored root-level `pos:` / `scale:` / `rotate:` / `alpha:` / `tint:` / `filter:` / `blendmode:` on `@:manim` programmables** — `MultiAnimBuilder.startBuild` honours a programmable's root `pos:` (wrapping the result in a holder so user `setPosition` stays compositional with the offset) and applies all other extended form properties via `applyExtendedFormProperties(root, rootNode)`, but `ProgrammableCodeGen.generateFields` only walked `rootNode.children` and never read any of these. Same `.manim` rendered at full position/scale/alpha/etc. via the runtime path and at the identity transform via codegen. Fix is two-part: (1) when `rootNode.pos != ZERO`, codegen now adds an internal `_root:h2d.Layers` field (h2d.Layers so layered children's `cast(parent, h2d.Layers)` succeeds), routes top-level children through it, and emits the position via the existing `generatePositionExpr` / `ensureHexLayoutIfNeeded` pipeline (so grid/hex-coord-based root positions work too) — children added to `_root` instead of `this`, matching the runtime's holder→retRoot wrapping so user `inst.setPosition(X, Y)` now composes additively with the `.manim` root pos in both paths. (2) Root-level `scale` / `rotate` / `alpha` / `blendMode` / `tint` / `filter` are emitted into the constructor targeting either `this._root` (when wrapped) or `this` directly (when not), matching the runtime's behaviour where `applyExtendedFormProperties` runs on the inner root regardless of whether a holder was needed. `$param`-dependent values are tracked through `expressionUpdates` so `setParameter()` re-fires the property. New test 105 (`rootProperties`) exercises all five non-tint/blend properties at once. Test 78 (`characterSheetDemo`) had a workaround setting `instance.x = 50; instance.y = 80` to compensate for the missing offset; workaround removed and the test now passes at 100% builder/macro parity.
- **`UICardHandHelper` cards with internal `@switch` arms had stale bindings** — the card hand's private `interactiveHelper` (a separate `UIRichInteractiveHelper` instance, distinct from the screen's `autoStatusHelper`) was registered once per card at `buildCardEntry` time and never resynced. Cards whose programmable contained `@switch` arms with different interactive ids per arm would have stale bindings after a parameter change — drag/hover/click on the new arm's interactive was not routed to the helper's state machine. Fix: `buildCardEntry` installs a per-card rebuild listener on the card's `BuilderResult` that calls `interactiveHelper.resync(result, interactiveId)` on every rebuild. A new `unregisterCardEntry(entry)` helper consolidates the `interactiveHelper.unbind` / `screen.removeInteractives` / `removeRebuildListener` cleanup, called from all three card removal sites (`discardCard`, `clearHand`, card-played handler on `onMouseRelease`). Two regression tests in `CardHandIntegrationTest`: `testCardHandRebuildListenerResyncOnSwitchArmFlip` (flips card's internal switch arm and verifies both screen wrappers and `interactiveHelper` bindings resync), `testCardHandRebuildListenerRemovedOnDiscard` (verifies the listener is dropped on discard so subsequent `setParameter` on the stale result is a safe no-op).
- **`@switch` arm rebuild leaked stale per-element bookkeeping** — when a `@switch` arm flipped, the rebuild closure in `MultiAnimBuilder` did not clean up the `IncrementalUpdateContext`'s per-element collections (`trackedExpressions`, `conditionalEntries`, `conditionalApplyEntries`, `dynamicRefBindings`, `dynamicNameBindings`, `deferredEntries`, `activeTransitionTweens`) for entries whose underlying `h2d.Object` lived inside the destroyed arm container. Entries became inert (skipped via `isEffectivelyVisible`) but stayed in their arrays forever, growing unboundedly across repeated arm flips and pinning detached scene-graph subtrees in memory. Same bug class for the param-dependent `repeatable` rebuild closure. Fix introduces `IncrementalUpdateContext.cleanupDestroyedSubtree(ir, container)` which walks every per-element collection and removes entries whose `h2d.Object` is `container` itself or a descendant. SWITCH/REPEAT closures call it before `removeChildren()` so parent links are still intact for the descendant walk. Also drops `dynamicRefBindings` whose `childContext` belongs to a removed dynamicRef result (parallel to `rebuildDynamicNameRef`'s existing cleanup), and cancels `activeTransitionTweens` on doomed objects so transition completion callbacks don't fire on dead h2d.Objects. Dev-only inspection accessors (`getTrackedExpressionsCount`/`getConditionalEntriesCount`/`getDynamicRefBindingsCount`/`getDynamicNameBindingsCount`) added under `MULTIANIM_DEV`. Three regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest` (dev-only): `testIncrementalSwitchTrackedExpressionsCleanedUp`, `testIncrementalSwitchConditionalEntriesCleanedUp`, `testIncrementalSwitchDynamicRefBindingsCleanedUp` — each flips arms 10 times and asserts the relevant counter returns to its initial value.
- **Screen-side `UIInteractiveWrapper` and `autoStatus` bindings went stale after `@switch` arm flip** — `UIScreen.addInteractives(result, prefix)` snapshotted `result.interactives` once and never re-synced, so any switch arm flip that introduced new interactives (or removed old ones) left the screen's `interactiveMap`/`interactiveWrappers`/`autoStatusHelper.bindings` pointing at the previous arm's MAObjects. `screen.getInteractive("idInNewArm")` returned `null`, hover/press events were lost, and panel/tooltip helpers couldn't find the new interactives. Fix installs a rebuild listener on the BuilderResult's incremental context inside `addInteractives` (tracked in a new `interactiveSubscriptions` array). On each rebuild, the listener calls `syncInteractivesFrom(r, prefix)` which diffs the wrappers (removes ids not in `result.interactives`, adds new ids via `addInteractive`) and calls `autoStatusHelper.resyncAutoStatus(r, prefix)` to update auto-bindings. `removeInteractives(prefix)` and `clear()` drop matching subscriptions to prevent leaks. Existing autoStatus binding state (Normal/Hover/Pressed/Disabled) is preserved across resync — `UIRichInteractiveHelper.resync` now only `bind()`s ids that don't already exist (the previous attempt that called `registerInternal` reset live bindings to Normal and broke 10 existing autoStatus tests). Four screen-level tests in `UIRichInteractiveHelperTest`: `testScreenAutoResyncOnSwitchArmFlip`, `testScreenAutoResyncBackAndForthNoAccumulation`, `testScreenAutoResyncRemovedOnRemoveInteractives`, `testResyncPreservesExistingBindingState`. Manually-registered `UIRichInteractiveHelper` instances (e.g. `UICardHandHelper.interactiveHelper`) are NOT auto-resynced — users must call `helper.resync(result, prefix)` explicitly.
- **`@switch` arm rebuild dropped registrations into a throwaway `InternalBuilderResults`** — the SWITCH and param-dependent REPEAT rebuild closures in `MultiAnimBuilder.build()` constructed a fresh local `ir = {names: [], interactives: [], slots: [], dynamicRefs: new Map(), htmlTextsWithLinks: []}` and passed it to the recursive `build()` call instead of the parent's `internalResults`. Anything inside the new arm or new repeat iteration (named elements, `interactive(...)`, `#name slot`, `dynamicRef`, `htmlTextsWithLinks`) was registered into the throwaway and orphaned: `result.getInteractive(id)` / `result.getSlot(name)` / `result.getDynamicRef(name)` / `result.getUpdatable(name)` / `result.hasName(name)` all returned `null`/threw for content inside the new arm or iteration. Tooltip/panel/rich-interactive helpers stopped seeing those interactives, autoStatus auto-wiring lost track of the new arm. Fix introduces `MultiAnimBuilder.removeRegistrationsUnder(ir, container)` (descendant scan that drops stale entries before rebuild) and routes both rebuild closures through `IncrementalUpdateContext.cleanupDestroyedSubtree(ir, container)` (which calls `removeRegistrationsUnder` plus the per-element bookkeeping cleanup described above), then passes the parent `internalResults` to the rebuild. Five builder-level regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest` covering each affected collection type: `testIncrementalSwitchInteractiveVisibleAfterArmSwitch`, `testIncrementalSwitchSlotVisibleAfterArmSwitch`, `testIncrementalSwitchDynamicRefVisibleAfterArmSwitch`, `testIncrementalSwitchNamedElementVisibleAfterArmSwitch`, `testIncrementalRepeatableInteractiveCountChange`, plus two stale-entry guards (`testIncrementalSwitchNoStaleAccumulation`, `testIncrementalRepeatableInteractiveShrinkNoStale`). Codegen `rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal` was verified to have no id-based interactive consumers (codegen instances expose typed `_eN:MAObject` fields only, no `getInteractive(id)` accessor) and is left as-is.
- **`@switch` pipe arms on color/int/bool params** — pipe-separated arms (`#FF0000 | #00FF00: ...`, `0xFF | 0x10: ...`, `true | false: ...`) stored the raw lexeme strings in `CoEnums`, while the runtime parameter is an integer — `matchSingleCondition` compared `a.contains(Std.string(currentValue))` (e.g. `"16711680"` vs `"#FF0000"`) and no pipe arm ever matched. Parser now routes each pipe value through `stringToConditional(v, paramType)` and wraps the results in a new `CoAnyOf` variant; builder and codegen both handle `CoAnyOf` (including negation via `CoNot` and param-ref collection). Enum/string params keep `CoEnums`. Two incremental-mode regression tests in `BuilderUnitTest`: `testIncrementalSwitchPipeColor`, `testIncrementalSwitchPipeHexInt`.
- **`setParameter` on `:color` param stored un-baked alpha** — `IncrementalUpdateContext.setParameter` stored `int` values directly without calling `addAlphaIfNotPresent`, while `.manim` literals (`#FF0000`) are baked to `0xFFFF0000` at parse time. `@switch`/`@()` arms then compared the baked literal against the un-baked runtime value and no arm matched. `setParameter` now bakes alpha for `PPTColor` params so set-equivalence matches parser default-equivalence.
- **`0xRRGGBB` color literals in `.manim` source stored un-baked alpha** — the parser's `tryStringToColor` `0x`-prefix branch returned `Std.parseInt(s)` as-is, so `tint:color=0xFF0000` stored `0x00FF0000` (alpha 0) while `tint:color=#FF0000` stored `0xFFFF0000`. Two `.manim` sources that looked equivalent produced different runtime values, and `@switch`/`@()` arms never matched `0x`-default programmables. Fix is length-aware on the source string (before `Std.parseInt` collapses leading zeros): 6-or-fewer-digit literals (`0xFF0000`) route through `addAlphaIfNotPresent` and bake `0xFF` alpha to match `#RRGGBB`; 8-digit literals (`0x80FF0000`, `0x00FF0000`) are preserved verbatim, so users can still express explicit alpha including fully-transparent colors. Regression tests `testParser0xColorLiteralAlphaBaking`, `testParser0xColorLiteralExplicitAlphaPreserved`, and `testParser0xColorLiteralExplicit8DigitZeroAlpha` in `BuilderUnitTest`.
- **Codegen setter/constructor on `:color` param stored un-baked alpha** — sibling of the above for the `@:manim` codegen path. The generated `setTint(v:Int)` assigned `v` raw, and the instance constructor forwarded `tint` into `_tint` without baking. `@switch(tint) { #FF0000: ... }` therefore never matched when the caller passed `instance.setTint(0xFF0000)` or `factory.create(0xFF0000)` — the arm condition compared against the parser-baked `0xFFFF0000`. Fixed at the type level: `PPTColor` storage fields now have type `bh.base.ColorUtils.ColorArg` (a new `abstract ColorArg(Int) from Int to Int` wrapper whose `@:from Int` calls `addAlphaIfNotPresent`). Every `_<name> = <int>` assignment — constructor forward, setter write, any future write site — goes through `fromInt` automatically; reads are zero-cost via `to Int`. Public arg types stay `Int` so default argument values remain literal constants (Haxe forbids cast / abstract expressions as arg defaults). Regression tests `testCodegenSetColorParamAlphaBaked` / `testCodegenCreateColorParamAlphaBaked` in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest`.
- **DevBridge HTTP DoS safeguards** — the JSON-RPC server's `HttpConnection` read headers and body with no upper bound and had no idle timeout. A malicious or stuck client could park a socket indefinitely and grow `headerBuf`/`bodyBuf` without limit. Now enforces `MAX_HEADER_BYTES=64KB`, `MAX_BODY_BYTES=16MB`, and `IDLE_TIMEOUT_SEC=30`, with `431 Request Header Fields Too Large`, `413 Payload Too Large`, and `408 Request Timeout` responses. `ScreenManager.update()` calls `devBridge.tick()` each frame to close expired pending connections. Header scanning also only re-scans the unread tail (`headerSearchFrom`) so parse cost is O(header size) not O(n²). All header/body reads now use `readBytes` into a single bounded buffer instead of byte-at-a-time.
- **`crossfade` transition with non-linear easing** — the sequential hide→show pivot was encoded as a `Custom` tween that tested `v <= 0.5` inside its setter, but `Tween.step` feeds `easedT = easingFn(t)` into the setter, not raw time. With `easeOutQuad + duration=0.3`, `easedT=0.5` at raw `t≈0.293` so the fade-in started at ~30% of `2·duration` instead of 50%, making the show phase ~70% of total time while the hide phase stayed at 50%. With `easeInQuad` the asymmetry reversed. Fix: show branch now uses `tm.sequence([pause, fadeIn])` so the pause is raw-time and the easing only applies to the fade-in half. Applied to both `CodegenTransitionHelper` paths and `MultiAnimBuilder`'s incremental transition. A new `transCrossfadeEased` row was added to `test/examples/95-transition` to guard the fix (the previous linear-only coverage couldn't detect the skew).
- **`flipX` / `flipY` transitions were concurrent, not sequential** — the show branch used `tm.tween(halfDuration, …)` starting immediately, so the hide on the outgoing element and the grow on the incoming element ran in parallel over the same `halfDuration` instead of hide-then-show. At any mid-point both elements were visible at partial scale. Fix: show branch now uses `tm.sequence([pause, grow])` with `halfDuration` for each phase, yielding the intended "old shrinks, then new grows" behavior over total `duration`.
- **`@switch` codegen `paramFieldExpr` prefix** — codegen's `paramFieldExpr()` used `"_p_"` prefix for param field references but param fields are created with `"_"` prefix, causing compile-time errors when a codegen programmable used `@switch` on its own parameters. Fixed to use `"_"`.
- **`@switch` nested param ref collection** — `collectNodeParamRefs` did not handle `SWITCH` or `REPEAT` node types, so nested `@switch` params and repeatable count params inside switch arms were not tracked. Changing the inner switch param or repeatable count would not trigger the outer switch rebuild. Both are now collected.
- **Incremental `applyConditionalChains` O(N²) → O(N)** — `resolveVisibilityForChildren` used linear scans of `conditionalEntries` and `conditionalApplyEntries` for every child node. Now builds `StringMap` lookup tables keyed by `uniqueNodeName` before the recursive walk.
- **`@switch` on `color`/`string`/`bool`/`int` params** — see "Changed" above. The single-value-arm fix closes a latent bug where `@switch` on a `bool` parameter silently never matched any arm (`CoEnums(["true"])` vs runtime `Value(1)` → string compare `"1" != "true"`).
- **`staticRef`/`dynamicRef` on `:bool` parameters** — `staticRef($prog, enabled=>true)` used to throw `expected integer, got "true"` at build time because `updateIndexedParamsFromDynamicMap` routed `PPTBool` through `resolveAsInteger`, which cannot parse the literal `true`/`false` string. Now routes through `resolveAsBool` (which handles `RVString` via `tryStringToBool`). Affected any code passing bool literals to `:bool` programmable params from `.manim`; the pre-existing `:[true,false]` enum workaround is no longer required.
- **HTML visual test report sort order** — tests numbered 100+ were interleaved with tests 10/11/... in the report because `reserveOrderIndex()` reflected utest's string-sorted method discovery order (`"test100_*" < "test11_*"`). Sort now prefers the numeric test number extracted from the `#NNN: name` display format, falling back to `orderIndex` only as a stable-compare tiebreaker for un-numbered entries.
- **Particle `advanceTime` accumulator** — when `externallyDriven: true`, multiple `advanceTime(dt)` calls between renders now accumulate per-call `dt` into `_externalDt` instead of overwriting it. Emission still runs per call (each with its own `dt`), and the accumulated total is consumed as a single physics step at the next `draw()`. Previously, all but the last sub-step's physics were dropped when the game advanced time multiple times per frame.
- **`.anim` `flipX`/`flipY` frame-size consistency** — validates at parse time that all frames share the same untrimmed size when `flipX`/`flipY` is enabled (extrapoints mirror around a single origW/origH, so mixed-size frames would desync points from visuals). Throws a clear error listing the mismatched dimensions. Extrapoint mirror math also uses `Math.round(origW - 2 * cx - p.x)` instead of `Std.int(origW - 2 * cx) - p.x` — the old form truncated the half-extent before subtracting `p.x`, losing fractional precision when `origW - 2 * cx` wasn't integral.
- **Tile source `center()`/`pivot()` nesting validation** — rejects nested pivot/center wrappers (`center(pivot(...))`, `pivot(0, 0, center(...))`) at parse time because the outer wrapper would silently override the inner one. Also validates `pivot(x, y, ...)` ratios are in 0..1.
- **`ScreenShakeHelper` baseline capture** — the previous implementation captured `target.x/y` at construction time and snapped back to it on every frame, which fought any gameplay motion of the target (camera scroll, character follow, etc.) and also snapped to stale coordinates if the target moved after construction. Now uses per-frame delta tracking (`target.x += totalX - lastDx`), so gameplay motion is preserved; `stop()` removes only the shake's residual offset. The unused `setOrigin(x, y)` method is removed. Jitter switched from a time-seeded integer hash to `hxd.Rand` for proper uniform angle distribution.
- **`.anim` tile center mutation** — `AFtoFrame` now clones the tile before applying center offsets (`dx`/`dy`), preventing mutation of shared cached atlas tiles. Previously, setting `center:` in an `.anim` file would mutate the atlas tile in-place, causing offset corruption when the same tile was reused across multiple animations or reloads.
- **`UICardHandHelper.dispose()` listener leak** — `dispose()` now clears `chainedListeners`, preventing stale references from accumulating after card hand teardown.
- **Negative hex integer literal in `EAny` context** — `parseAnything()` merged `TInteger` and `THexInteger` cases and called `stringToInt(n)` without prepending `"0x"`, so literals like `-0xFF` used anywhere an "any value" was expected (e.g. interactive metadata: `interactive(..., threshold => -0xFF)`) resolved to `0` instead of `-255`. Hex and decimal are now split into separate branches. `parseIntegerOrReference` already handled this correctly.
- **Unterminated double-quoted string silently accepted** — `MacroLexer`'s double-quoted string path lacked an EOF check: when the closing `"` was missing, the loop fell off the end of input and returned a `TQuotedString` holding partial content, producing confusing downstream errors several tokens later. Now throws `Unterminated string, missing closing double quote` with the file/line/column of the opening quote, matching the single-quoted path.
- **`.anim` filter `@else` / `@default` stacking with matching `@(cond)`** — `resolveAnimFilters` accepted every entry whose state selector didn't actively contradict the runtime selector, so `@else` / `@default` fallbacks (which parse to effectively-empty selectors that always match) stacked on top of the matching `@(cond)` entry instead of being mutually exclusive. Concretely: `@(level >= 3) outline` followed by `@else pixelOutline` would apply *both* filters at `level=5`, and `@(team=>red) tint: #FF0000` followed by `@default tint: #FFFFFF` would always end up white because the default ran last and overwrote the match. Parser now tags alternation chains with a `group` id (a new `@(cond)` starts a group; subsequent `@else`/`@else(cond)`/`@default` siblings join it; an unconditional entry breaks the chain and is always applied). `resolveAnimFilters` picks the best-scoring entry per group while still stacking unconditional filters on top. Five regression tests in `AnimFilterStateConditionalTest`.
- **`@switch` arm + parameterized slot build context inheritance** — `rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal` and `buildSlotContent` both built their children with `cast null` for grid/hex coordinate systems and a fresh `{callback: defaultCallback}` instead of inheriting the caller's context. Two latent bugs in both paths: (B1) an arm or parameterized-slot body that used `$grid.pos(...)` / `$hex.*` inside a nested element (e.g. `graphics(rect(...): $grid.pos(0, 0))`) crashed with `gridCoordinateSystem is null` on initial build and every rebuild, because the programmable's `grid:`/`hex:` declaration was not propagated into the nested build. (B2) a `placeholder(..., callback("name"))` inside an arm silently lost the user's `BuilderParameters.callback` after any `setParameter()`-driven rebuild, because the rebuild installed `defaultCallback` instead. Fix captures `builderParams` and resolves the enclosing node's grid/hex systems via the parent chain *before* `pushBuilderState`, then threads them into the nested `build()` calls in both `rebuildSwitchArmByOrdinal` and `buildSlotContent`. `placeholderObjects` and `scene` fields are also inherited. Three codegen regression tests in `ProgrammableCodeGenTest`: `testCodegenSwitchArmGridContext`, `testCodegenSwitchArmCallbackInheritance`, `testCodegenSlotParamGridContext`.
- **LSP `.anim` context detection in comments/strings** — `AnimAnalyzer.detectContext` walked every character of every line counting `{`/`}` and keyword-matched line starts without comment or string awareness. Braces inside `//`/`/* */` comments shifted the nesting depth, and keywords inside strings or comments (e.g. `// playlist disabled`) flipped the completion context to the wrong block, corrupting hover/completion results for any `.anim` file with prose. Lexer now strips line comments, tracks block comments across lines, and skips `"..."` string literals (including `\"` escapes) before counting braces. Keyword detection is suppressed while inside a block comment.
- **Particle `emitFilter` sub-emitter cascade** — when `emitFilter` rejected a particle, the particle's `OnBirth` trigger still fired during the next `init()` step and, once its forced-dead lifecycle tripped, its `OnDeath` trigger fired as well, producing an explosive spawn loop at the rejection boundary (every rejected particle spawned a full child burst plus a death burst). `Particle.rejected` now flags filter-rejected particles; `init()` clears it on every fresh spawn and `emitFilter()` sets it when discarding. Both `OnBirth` (delayed spawn step) and `OnDeath` (lifecycle expiry) sub-emitter triggers skip rejected particles.
- **Particle recycle double-init** — when a particle's lifecycle expired on a looping group with live headroom, `init()` ran to recycle the particle but `timeNormalized` (computed earlier in the same update step from the pre-init `life`) was still > 1, so the lifecycle branch immediately re-fired `OnDeath` triggers and potentially re-ran the recycle path with stale state. The recycle branch now returns `true` immediately after `init()`, skipping the stale lifecycle check for that frame.
- **Particle shutdown terminal multipliers** — when `shutdown()`'s curve reached `rate >= 1.0` and `shutdownTargetCount <= 0`, the shutdown flag was cleared so remaining particles could die naturally, but `shutdownAlphaMult`/`shutdownSizeMult`/`shutdownSpeedMult` still held their terminal (~0) values from the final curve sample. Any live particles finishing their lifetime after shutdown exit rendered at 0 alpha/size/speed instead of their natural values. All three multipliers are now reset to `1.0` at the moment `shutdownActive` flips to false.
- **Particle `removeGroup()` batch leak** — `Particles.removeGroup(id)` only removed the group entry from the map and left the underlying `h2d.SpriteBatch` attached to the scene with its particles. The batch and its elements stayed resident and continued rendering until the parent `Particles` object was disposed. Now clears and removes the batch before dropping the map entry.
- **Particle `tiles.length == 0` early return** — `init()` previously `return`ed from the middle of the function when a group had no tiles (waiting for async asset load), skipping the remainder of the setup (`p.vx *= speed`, gravity/acceleration/rotation assignment, `emitFilter()` check). First-frame particles from tile-less groups started with unscaled velocity and bypassed `emitFilter`. Now wraps tile selection in a conditional so the rest of init always runs.
- **`ProgrammableBuilder.setFontFromMultiple` empty-fonts crash** — when the font fallback array was empty (no best match and no fonts at all — e.g. misconfigured rich text), `fonts[fonts.length - 1]` evaluated to `fonts[-1]` which is `null` on HashLink and could crash further downstream. Now retains the original font when the array is empty instead of clobbering it with null.
- **`.anim` conditional duplicate state key** — `@(level >= 3, level <= 5, level != 0)` silently overwrote prior entries for the same state name in the conditional map, so only the last comparison for a given state was applied. Parser now rejects duplicate state keys in a single conditional with a clear error.
- **`.anim` negative integer comparison literals** — `@(level >= -1)`, `@(xp > -100)`, etc. failed to parse because `expectIdentifier()` didn't accept a leading minus. Comparison conditionals now use `expectSignedIdentifier()`, matching the negative-literal handling already in place for plain value lists.
- **`.anim` parser `throw` → `syntaxError`** — several late-phase validation errors (`no playlist for state X`, `animation X not reachable`, `Extra point X in anim Y not reachable`) were raised via raw `throw` statements, bypassing the parser's structured error reporting (file/line/column context). All now route through `syntaxError()` for consistent strict-mode output.
- **`@switch` on `:array` params** — `@switch` on an array-typed parameter previously hit the `default:` branch of the type check and silently emitted string arms that could never match. `PPTArray` now surfaces a clear "Array cannot be matched — @switch requires a discrete type" error at parse time.
- **`MacroManimParser` iterator error message** — the error listing valid iterator types for `repeatable()` / `tilegroup` named the leading option `grid`, but the parser rejects `grid` and accepts `step`. Corrected to list `step, layout, array, range, stateanim, tiles`.
- **`ManimKeywordInfo` missing `SWITCH` entry** — the element-ctor and node-type lists used by the LSP's exhaustive-switch compile-time guard did not include `SWITCH`, so any addition to those enums that touched `SWITCH` would have failed the exhaustive check silently. Added `SWITCH` to both lists.
- **`UICardHandHelper` arrow ignoring `canPlayCard`** — while dragging a card into a targeting zone, the arrow's valid/invalid color (green vs red) was based solely on whether the cursor hovered a registered target interactive. A `canPlayCard` veto (e.g. "this shuttle can't board that tile") silently refused the drop but the arrow still lit green during the drag, giving the player no feedback. `updateCardDrag()` now calls `canPlayCard(cardId, currentResult)` each frame and forces `targeting.forceValid` to match, so invalid drops flash red before release.

### Internal
- **`AnimationSM.setCurrentFrame` allocated a wrapper `Array<AnimationFrame>` on every state advance — `AnimationClip.setSingleFrame(frame)` reuses a per-clip buffer** — the call path was `setCurrentFrame(frame) → clip.setFrames([frame])`, where the `[frame]` array-literal allocates a fresh `Array<AnimationFrame>` on each frame swap. With dozens of `AnimationSM` instances (every animated sprite on a busy screen), each advancing through their playlist multiple times per second, this was a steady-state per-frame allocation cliff visible in HL alloc captures. Fix: new `AnimationClip.setSingleFrame(frame)` writes the frame into a `_singleFrameBuf:Array<AnimationFrame>` constructed once in the clip's field initializer and reused on every call; `AnimationSM.setCurrentFrame` now calls `setSingleFrame` instead of `setFrames([frame])`. The `setFrames(array)` entry point is unchanged — callers that legitimately pass a multi-frame playlist still allocate (correct: ownership of the passed array transfers to the clip). New `AnimationClip.setFramesCallCount` allocation watchdog (gated behind `MULTIANIM_ALLOC_TRACK`) so tests can pin the contract that the hot path does not hit `setFrames`. One regression test in `AnimFilterRuntimeTest` (`testFrameAdvanceDoesNotAllocateArrayPerFrame`) — 20-frame loop animation, 100 ticks of 0.05s, asserts `setFramesCallCount` delta is 0 across steady-state playback.
- **`UIInteractiveWrapper.containsPoint` allocated a fresh `h2d.col.Point` on every hit-test — replaced by a shared static scratch** — `containsPoint` is called per registered interactive per mouse-move on every iterator that walks `UIDefaultController.getEventElements` / `UICardHandTargeting.findTargetAt`. The defensive `new Point(pos.x, pos.y)` copy (`globalToLocal` mutates its argument in place, and callers iterate multiple wrappers with the same caller-owned `pos`, so `pos` itself cannot be passed directly) fired on every wrapper. Fix: new private static `_scratchPt:Null<Point>` on `UIInteractiveWrapper`, lazily allocated on first call, reused thereafter. HL is single-threaded so cross-instance sharing is safe.
